# Backlog — Open-Loops Ledger

> OPEN LOOPS is reconciled at every session open and close. Nothing is tracked only in chat.
> An item leaves OPEN LOOPS only by meeting its close-criterion or being explicitly tombstoned.
> When writing a close criterion, state what its instrument reads if the work never happened. If that's the same as success, it isn't an instrument. See oddities, "a close criterion whose instrument can't fail."
> A backlog line's stated **mechanism/premise** is a claim, not a fact — probe it before building on it, exactly like a logged bug's repro. Two lines in three HOs were wrong on inspection (HO 512's "`getNewsFeed` already joins `observation_entities`"; HO 514's "the spine is SOURCES"), both caught by checking, not trusting. A third would make it a convention problem, not coincidence.
> Sections: OPEN LOOPS (dated, owned) · QUEUED (next builds) · BANKED (gated) · WATCH (standing observations) · FIT & FINISH (audit findings) · DONE (tombstones, append-only).

Owners: **Corey** (human action) · **Code** (a build/run) · **cron** (happens on schedule).

---

- **Markets tape read burn (HO 577 — found during the Turso-outage diagnosis, unrelated to the primaries arc).** *Owner:* Code. `getLatestMarketTicks` is `unstable_cache(..., { revalidate: 60 })` and `MarketsTapeClient` polls it at `POLL_MS = 60_000` — **the TTL and the poll interval are the same number, so effectively every poll regenerates.** Each regeneration runs an unbounded `SELECT symbol, MAX(ticked_at) … GROUP BY symbol` over `market_ticks` plus a second scan whose `julianday(ticked_at) >= julianday('now','-9 day')` predicate **wraps the column in a function and defeats any index on it**. Runtime logs showed ~4 requests/minute sustained (consistent with ~4 tabs left open); the Jul-25 step plus flat writes fit polling, not crawling or cron. **The arithmetic is inference, not measurement** — derived from the log and the code, and this session produced two confident read-cost claims both wrong on inspection (see the read-budget WATCH). Also record that `app/api/markets/latest/route.ts`'s own header comment asserts **no per-request DB hit between writes**, which is **false**. *Close:* a probe HO sizing `market_ticks` and measuring rows-read per regeneration, then a fix — **both are code and belong to that probe HO, not here or the route comment.** *Instrument:* Turso rows-read against tab count. **HO 582 update — partly discharged, not closed (the REPLACED-criterion pattern).** STEP 0 (`markets-read-burn-582.ts`, `3baf98c`) MEASURED it — 12,240 rows/regen → ~17.6M/day per open tab, ~20% of the ~88M plateau — and C1–C4 shipped (`cf1516d` TTL 60→86400 · `8396068` sargable spark window · `2a86c3f` per-symbol seeks replacing the covering-index scan · `d0feb8f` visibility-gate the poll), so the original close criterion (a probe sizing the table + measuring rows-read per regen, then a fix) is MET. **Criterion REPLACED:** the loop closes on the **Turso per-DB rows-read slope over ~24h vs tab count** (prediction on the record: ~a fifth cut, a level shift not a resolution — roadmap "Also (HO 582)"; if the slope barely moves the burn model is wrong and *that* is the finding). The broader **read-budget attribution** item (WATCH) stays open aimed at summarize / LDA / traffic for the residual ~80%. Two children spun out below.
- **New (HO 582) · markets cron duplicate write (P3, instrument stated).** *Owner:* Code. Two rows per symbol tied **to the millisecond** on `ticked_at`, observed for **CPI (ids 6580/6582)** and **WTI (6581/6583)** at `ticked_at` **2026-07-31T20:00:35.801Z / :36.009Z** (read directly from the duplicate rows in STEP 0's dup probe, not derived from the ids). **Render impact is nil in every traced path** — duplicate spark points share a coordinate, the 1W anchor re-assigns the same value, and `change_pct` uses a strict `market_date < ?` prior-reference — and HO 582 C3's `LIMIT 1` now dedupes the tape read, so the symptom is invisible (which is *why* it needs its own instrument: a read-time dedupe removes the symptom of a write-path defect — oddities). **Byte-identical `ticked_at` is itself the strongest evidence, and it points AWAY from a second execution.** `ticked_at` is `new Date().toISOString()`, evaluated when the insert args are built — so concurrent invocation, a retried body, and a double `processSymbol` would each recompute it and land *different* milliseconds. Two rows sharing a timestamp to the millisecond means the **same args were sent twice**, i.e. a **transport/client-level retry of one `db.execute`** (libsql client or network layer), not a second run of the code path. That also explains the otherwise-puzzling symbol set: a transient retry hits arbitrary statements, so CPI+WTI sharing no source/cadence subset is the expected signature, not a constraint needing explanation. **The concrete candidate in this codebase is `lib/db.ts::boundedFetch`** (HO 238): it retries a timed-out/aborted request exactly once with a fresh clone, and an `INSERT` is non-idempotent — a first insert that committed server-side but whose response was slow enough to trip the 10s abort would be retried and land a byte-identical second row. Verify against `boundedFetch`'s retry path before assuming concurrency. **Byte-identity is already confirmed (this sweep, ids 6580/6582 + 6581/6583): `ticked_at`, `price`, `change_pct`, and `market_date` are all byte-identical within each pair** — the retry signature, so the DB-side evidence already favors retry over two real executions. **Revised instrument reading — the outcomes INVERT from the earlier draft:** `cron_runs` rows for `/api/cron/markets` around those ids still discriminate — `payload.outcomes` carries the per-symbol set (verified: `payload = {ticked, failed, outcomes}`) — but **one row** is now the PREDICTED result and CONFIRMS the retry hypothesis (NOT a falsification, and not a cue to check Vercel's cron-invocation log — a client retry never appears there); **two rows** would resurrect concurrency and require explaining how two independent invocations computed identical millisecond timestamps for two different symbols. The byte-identity check above is the paired second signal (any divergence within a pair would mean two real executions, not a retry). **Fix shape follows the answer, and a cron lock is probably NOT it:** a lock addresses concurrency and does nothing about a client retry — if the retry hypothesis holds, the options are an **idempotency guard on the write** (`UNIQUE(symbol, ticked_at)`, exactly the tie the duplicates violate) or **accepting duplicates as harmless** now that C3 dedupes the read; do not scope either until the instrument runs. **A lead, now a coincidence:** the ids sit at a **collision hour** — `0 */4` and the weekday `0,30 13-21` fmp pass both fire at 16:00 and 20:00 UTC, and 2026-07-31 was a Friday — but under the retry hypothesis this is coincidence, and recording a falsified lead is the point: the naive collision doesn't fit anyway (CPI is fred/monthly/units:pc1, WTI is fred/daily, `?source=fmp` filters both out, so the fmp pass cannot have written them; **no `?source=` filter produces CPI+WTI alone** — `?source=fred` would also write TNX and UNEMP, and two overlapping bare runs would double *many* symbols, not two). **The mechanism is documented as safe, and the duplicate is the counterexample.** `lib/db.ts:35-37` justifies the retry with "Writes are upsert/UPDATE/DELETE-shaped or benign append-only inserts (HO 238 idempotency grep), so a retried-after-timeout write is safe." The markets write is a bare `INSERT INTO market_ticks` with no conflict clause — append-only, and classified benign under that grep. **Both halves of the classification are individually true and the conclusion still doesn't follow:** append-only inserts are not *idempotent*, they are merely *harmless when duplicated*, and for the tape that harmlessness only became true at HO 582 C3 (before it, the duplicate rendered twice). The retry replays `retryInput`, a clone of the original Request (`lib/db.ts:44`), so the identical serialized args go out — which is why the duplicate is byte-identical rather than merely close, and why this fits the evidence better than any second execution. **Second instrument, cheaper than `cron_runs`:** the retry logs `[db] timeout, retrying` (`lib/db.ts:52`). A hit in the 20:00 2026-07-31 runtime logs confirms it directly; absence is weaker evidence (log retention), so check `cron_runs` regardless. **Check `cron_runs` retention/payload survival first** (the route comment references a 30-min window; `cron_runs` is the durable record, but confirm the old row + its payload persist before relying on it). *Close:* one run or two, named. **Scope note — this is not markets-only.** If it holds, the HO 238 idempotency argument under-covers every bare append-only `INSERT` reachable through `getDb()`, not just this one. Re-running that grep with "duplicates are harmless" separated from "the write is idempotent" is its own item; do not widen this P3 into it.

The `0 15 * * *` cron fired twice — 2026-07-29 (failure) and 2026-07-30 (**success**), both ~85 min after the slot (GitHub best-effort delivery) — so the schedule **delivers** and the latest run is green. **This closes the whole Phase-2 Playwright-in-CI loop**, since this was its one surviving thread. - **`pageErr` intermittent on the `smoke.spec.ts` prod crawl — STILL OPEN; every prod fire is UNATTRIBUTED and the cause COUNT is UNKNOWN (HO 572 → 574 → 589 → 590; partition retracted 591).** *Owner:* Code; opened 2026-07-29 (first observed run). **Two 574-era claims corrected in 589 (do not re-inherit them):** (a) `args[]=HTML` is the **structural / markup** variant, **not** "text-content" — `react-dom-client.development.js:5240` fills the first `%s` with `fromText ? "text" : "HTML"`, and CI captured `HTML`; a text-content mismatch would read `args[]=text`. (b) The "identical message across all four runs ⇒ **single cause**" inference is **withdrawn** — the component-naming diff (the second `%s`) is **DEV-only** (prod `react-dom` bundle: 0 occurrences of the `hydration-mismatch` link/template), so the minified prod string is **byte-identical for every structural mismatch anywhere in the app**; message identity carries **zero** cause-count information (589 M1/M2; oddities). Raising the HO 574 200-char cap buys nothing — the prod crawl can **never** self-identify a component. **What 589/590 found and fixed — two DISTINCT latent bugs, both real, both correctness hardening, and NEITHER proven to be a prod cause (deliberately UNNUMBERED; see the partition retraction below):** **(1) the markets tape (structural, `args[]=HTML`), FIXED HO 590 (`626c251`):** `MarketsTapeClient` seeded its clock with `useState(() => Date.now())`, a render-time read that gates the `<span class="markets-tape-arrow">` node via `showSlots`; under a manufactured clock skew across the 26h stale edge / 09:30–16:00-ET market boundary the arrows collapse → `args[]=HTML`. Named via the DEV owner tree (`TickItem` → the arrow span), fixed by prop-drilling a server `nowMs` (HO 489/490 shape). **(2) the `lob-age` text drift (`args[]=text`) — a SEPARATE defect, FIXED HO 590 (`601f54a`):** `FilingRow.relAge()`'s render-time `Date.now()` drifts the "Nd ago" string across a day boundary; distinct variant, kept distinct in the record. **The load-bearing finding (589 M4 + 590 C5 — why this does NOT close):** prod `/` is served **fresh per request** (`X-Vercel-Cache: MISS`, `Age: 0`, `no-store`, `Date` advancing), so the SSR→hydration gap is **sub-second** and **cannot straddle** a 26h/market boundary; the harness confirms `control(+0)` (that same sub-second gap) **never fires**. So the tape — the obvious structural candidate — is a real latent bug but is **NOT the mechanism of prod's ~42 `args[]=HTML` fires**, which **remain unattributed**. `isMarketOpen` is TZ-explicit (`America/New_York`), ruling out the locale variant too. **The "cause #1 / cause #2" partition is RETRACTED (HO 591) — it rested on a premise C5 destroyed.** That split existed for exactly one reason: `/` carried the tape and the inner pages didn't, so they *had* to be different bugs. C5 measured the tape out of prod, and the reason to separate them went with it. **The corrected statement: all ~42 prod `args[]=HTML` fires are UNATTRIBUTED** — `/`, the **six `?stage=` home variants**, **`/president`**, and **`/members`** alike — **not "one fixed, one remaining."** **Whether that is one cause or several is now UNKNOWN:** message identity carries **zero** cause-count information (589 M1), and the evidence that once separated the routes is gone. **`/president` stays named in the firing set** — it fired in prod (HO 574) and is **tapeless**; narrowing the record to "the members surface" would silently drop a route from a firing set whose *growth* is the very thing HO 574's scoping guard warns about. **Checked and DEAD as a single-cause candidate — a shared client island spanning the firing set (HO 591):** there is **no `SessionProvider` and no `useSession` anywhere in the repo** (auth is resolved **server-side** in `HeaderBar` / `DashboardV2Header` and prop-drilled into `AuthButton`), and **`app/layout.tsx` is bare** — no shared client wrapper. The *directly imported* client islands of `/` and `/members` are **disjoint** (5 vs 9, zero overlap); the **three** they do share arrive through their respective server headers — **`AuthButton`** (server-resolved `user` prop; `useTransition` its only hook), **`CyclingTimestamp`** (HO 489-verified hydration-safe by construction), and **`MobileNavDrawer`** (`useState(false)`, a constant seed, no non-deterministic read) — and **all three are deterministic at render, so none can skew.** *(Note the precise form: it is **not** true that `/` and the inner pages share no client component — they share three; the point is that all three are safe.)* Record this as a **ruled-out lead**, not as support for either partition. **Also RULED OUT, written down because it is a false lead a future reader will re-derive from scratch:** `/` **307-redirects an anonymous visitor to `/welcome`** (the `ct_seen` gate in `app/page.tsx`) — but that does **NOT** mean the `/` fires were on the landing page. `smoke.spec.ts` sets `ct_seen` **context-wide** (`:40` defines `GATE_COOKIE`, applied at `:143` for the route crawl and `:270` for the targeted interactions), so **every `pageErr`-collecting visit renders the terminal, not the landing.** The single no-cookie visit is the deliberate `gate / deep-link param drop` test (`:246`), which asserts the anon bounce and **feeds no collector.** **What stands UNEXPLAINED (an observation, not a lead):** `/members` fired at `control(+0)` in the 589 sweep and prod fired `committees-redirect`→`/members` (`/members/pass-rate` 307-redirects there, so it's one surface), but **25 + 12 fresh-context re-tries produced 0 fires** and the single-page-bleed hypothesis **failed to reproduce** (590 C2) — the sharpest unexplained observation in the set, and **not** a named second cause. **Reproduction lever (HO 590, committed):** `scripts/diagnostic/hydration-clock-harness-590.ts` — a **local-only** clock-skew rig (needs `next dev` for React 19's DEV describeDiff; never rides `e2e-prod.yml`) that manufactures the SSR-vs-hydration skew and captures the DEV owner tree + `args[]=HTML`/`text` variant. **HO 592 — H5 (data-dependent invalid HTML nesting) FALSIFIED, cleanly, in one pass.** The browser's tree-construction algorithm restructures invalid nesting before React hydrates, which would produce `args[]=HTML` with no time dependence at all — it fit every constraint the clock hypothesis failed. A static parse5 diff over **all 37 `ROUTES`** found **0 reparented / dropped / synthesised nodes**, with the detector self-validated first (6 positive fixtures + 2 negative, incl. a regression case pinning `<div>` inside `<a>` as VALID HTML5) and scanner coverage checked (1460 authored elements = 1460 parsed on `/`, every one matched by source offset — so the clean result is a real read, not a short one). The **HO 473 SVG `<title>` class is clean at HEAD both statically (4 sites, all single-child) and at runtime (0 multi-child titles in rendered prod HTML across 37 routes).** One hypothesis fewer; nothing named. **HO 593 — the intermittent `/members` 500 is a SEPARATE FAULT and is now split out of this loop.** The discriminator is the pageErr MESSAGE, which the crawl already logs: over 54 runs / 4,230 route-hit cells (one site-wide outage run excluded, see below), **87 `#418` instances vs 12 "Server Components render" instances**, and the 12 land only on `committees-redirect` / `members-pass-rate` / `member-detail` / `stale`. Every `/`-family route (home, all six `?stage=`, dashboard-v2/classic) and **`/president`** carry `#418` and have **never** 500'd. The 500 is a whole-route `__next_error__` shell, not a segment failing after a flushed shell, so it cannot be producing a structurally different tree. That 500 is now separately named, fixed and tombstoned (DONE, HO 593-595) — **this loop keeps only the `#418` fires, exactly as open and as unattributed as before.** **Standing instrumentation requirement (HO 593/594, oddities):** this class must be measured at the **QUERY level, never the route level** — `lib/db.ts` retries once, so a route failure needs *both* attempts to breach and the route-failure rate is roughly the **square** of the query-breach rate. Route-level counts systematically understate it; HO 593's M2 stage table is the worked example and is recorded unranked for that reason. *Close (unchanged, and now sharper — an intermittent's **absence is not evidence of a fix**; and since C5 proved neither fixed bug was ever the prod mechanism, a green crawl after them is **doubly** ambiguous and verifies **nothing** in this arc):* a **named cause for the prod fires PLUS green runs**, never "the crawl went green" alone. **HO 656 §D — THE RATE HAS NOT STEP-CHANGED, measured over the scheduled daily prod crawls from HO 590's deploy (2026-08-02) to 08-12.** Read-only; no fixes, no new crawls, no attribution attempts. **One day's log is GONE** (`gh run view`: `log not found`, retention), so the span is **10 observable days, reported as such rather than extrapolated**. Fires on **7 of 10**; events/day **08-02:2 · 03:1 · 04:4 · 05:1 · 06:— · 07:0 · 08:5 · 09:10 · 10:0 · 11:0 · 12:1**. **The clean days are scattered, not a step:** 08-07 (0) is immediately followed by the two heaviest days (5, then 10), and the 08-10/08-11 pair is broken by a fire on 08-12. **No deploy in the 604–655 train moved the rate in either direction** — which is the datapoint the entry wanted, and it is what this check reads **if nothing happened**, stated up front so a null is not mistaken for a finding. Affected routes are the age-rendering feed set throughout (`home`, `home-stage-*`, `dashboard-v2`, `bills`, `stale`, `committees-redirect`, `member-detail`), recorded as an OBSERVATION and not a hypothesis. **METHOD NOTE, because it inverts the obvious check: run CONCLUSIONS under-report fires.** `expect.soft` fails the test, `--retries=2` reports it **flaky**, and the workflow still concludes **`success`** — so a green run list reads exactly like a fixed bug. The counts here come from the per-route `pageErr=` log lines, not from conclusions.

- **ESLint absent — no dependency, no config** — opened HO 491 (Jul 2026); *Owner:* Corey/Code. Wiring it is **its own arc**: install `eslint` + `eslint-config-next`, add a config, then **triage the likely wall of pre-existing violations** across the runtime surface (app/components/lib) before a `next lint` step can go green. *Close:* an ESLint config exists and a `lint` step runs green in CI (or the pre-existing findings are explicitly waived). *Blocker/why-deferred:* the unknown-size cleanup — forcing it into HO 488 would have blocked CI from landing.
- **CI action runtime — bump `actions/checkout` + `actions/setup-node` to v5 when the Node 20 deprecation bites** — opened HO 491, scope widened HO 504 (Jul 2026); *Owner:* Code. **The annotation now spans BOTH workflows — `ci.yml` (Phase 1) and `e2e-prod.yml` (HO 504); both `e2e-prod` runs (`deployment_status` + `workflow_dispatch`) carried the same one.** Every run carries a GitHub-infra **Node-20-deprecation annotation on the actions themselves** (the `checkout`/`setup-node` JS actions run on the runner's Node, forced to 24 today — unrelated to our `node-version: 20` *app* toolchain). Harmless now, but it's a **countdown, not a steady state**: when GitHub drops the Node 20 runtime, CI fails for a reason that has nothing to do with the repo. *Close:* bump both actions to `@v5`. **Symptom to recognize:** an **unexplained CI failure on an unchanged codebase** — check the action versions before debugging anything else.
- ~~**News ingest cron timing out**~~ — CLOSED HO 369. `/api/cron/news` (`0 14 * * *`) had failed 5 straight days (since Jun 21): `getCandidateBills(30)` mis-planned into a `MULTI-INDEX OR` over `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` (`is_ceremonial = 0` ≈ the whole 16.5k-row corpus) + a temp-b-tree sort, ~20s, past the HO 238 10s bound + retry-once → route abort at ~20.2s (the `cron_runs` `status=error`, `elapsed ≈ 20,200ms` signature). **Fixed** by forcing `INDEXED BY idx_bills_latest_action` on the query (commit `95d0939`) — it drives the ~30-day window off the date index in DESC order, no temp sort, no MULTI-INDEX OR. Verified: EXPLAIN shows `SEARCH bills USING INDEX idx_bills_latest_action`; a local `sync:news` inserted rows with no `[db] timeout` line. Timeout left at 10s — HO 238 stands. *(Deployed-tick confirmation tracked as a soak below.)*

- ~~**`members.party` "wrong" for Kiley (opened HO 420)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 422 — NOT A BUG.** The premise was wrong: `members.party='I'` for **K000401 Kevin Kiley (CA-03) is correct.** He **switched Republican → Independent on 2026-03-09**, confirmed by **both** Congress.gov `partyHistory` (`{I, Independent, 2026}` ahead of `{R, 2023–2026}`) **and** congress-legislators `party_affiliations` (`2026-03-09 → 2027-01-03: Independent, caucus: Republican`). He **caucuses Republican**, which is why **Voteview `party_code=200` (R)** reads him R — DW-NOMINATE's `party_code` follows **caucus, not registration** (oddities). So `sync:members` derived `I` correctly from a `partyHistory` that correctly says Independent — **no derivation bug, no override, no code shipped** (Part 1 of HO 422 halted on the corrected premise). The `ideology-coverage-419` diagnostic's `party_code`-vs-`members.party` "disagreement" is a **registration-vs-caucus semantic gap**, not an error — a structural false-positive for any independent caucusing with a party (see the QUEUED diagnostic fix below). *(This also corrects the HO 419 DONE tombstone's "Kiley a CA Republican, members wrong" wording.)*

- ~~**Summarize backlog + `/api/cron/summarize` reliability — one loop, cause + symptom (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 406** (commit `82fb6bc`, live on prod). Both halves done. **Cause:** HO 383 had dropped the queue read's SQL LIMIT to fetch the full null set for the app-side stage-priority sort, so at 1,374 nulls it became a `SCAN` over all 16,623 `bills` rows and tipped the 10s DB wall (the 07-02/06-29 55s timeouts + the 06-30 20s error) — fixed with the partial index `idx_bills_summarize_queue ON bills(update_date DESC) WHERE summary IS NULL`, forced `INDEXED BY` (the statless planner mis-plans into a MULTI-INDEX OR unhinted). **Queue read is now a bounded partial-index `SCAN … idx_bills_summarize_queue`: 48ms drained (was the 16,623-row scan).** **Symptom:** serial ~1.9s/bill → a bounded **C=5** worker pool, and the cron per-tick cap raised **50→200** so the 45s budget is the wall — one real prod tick did **80** summarized (was ~23), 0 fail/timeout, `budgetStopped`, 49.6s. The one-shot `npm run summarize` drained the standing backlog **1,374 → 7** (the 7 are 15s per-bill timeouts, deferred 24h → retry next tick). `summary IS NULL` now trends down, and the **cron self-heals at cap 200 / C=5 against ~30/day inflow** (~80–120/tick clears a day's inflow with room to spare) — a manual drain is no longer the only thing that lowers the count. **Foundation's roadmap 96 hold is no longer fused on this cron:** the growing summarize backlog was the last correctness drag tying Foundation to this cron's health; with the read bounded and the cron self-healing, the score no longer depends on it. See DONE → HO 406.
- ~~**`/api/cron/weekly-report` erroring — reports 2 weeks stale (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 407** (commit `33cc707`, live on prod, verified). Gate verdict was §C, not the suspected transient: **six `gatherReportData()` queries over the fat `bills` table `MULTI-INDEX OR`'d onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` and cold-aborted at the 10+10s `boundedFetch` double-timeout** (`cron_runs` #559 06-29 @ 23.8s and #287 06-15 @ 20.1s are both that DB double-timeout — NOT the Gemini 503s #82/#74 that the catch-up already absorbs). This took down the Monday cron AND the HO 285 daily catch-up's gen identically (throws → row stays missing → "regenerates daily, never lands"), which is why the net wasn't covering it. Ruled out: §A delivery (the Monday row was present-and-errored; it's a Vercel cron) and §B placement (the catch-up already runs FIRST in `/api/sync`). **Fixed hint-only** — six `INDEXED BY` hints onto EXISTING indexes (`idx_bills_stage_changed_at`, `idx_bills_introduced_date`, `idx_bills_latest_action`), **no new index, no `migrate.ts`**. Cold-verified from idle: all six 20s TIMEOUT → 156–1948ms (Q6's `json_each + GROUP BY` hint held cold — the flagged fork didn't trigger); full cold `generateWeeklyReport` 18.6s. On prod the erroring cron flipped **#559 error 23.8s → #673 success 13.5s**; Jun 22–28 (the stale week) regenerated + `/reports` revalidated. **Catch-up hardening:** its once-swallowed failure is now surfaced machine-keyably (`payload.reportCatchup.ok` + a `report-catchup-failed:`-prefixed `cron_runs.error_message`), so the two-week silent-failure gap is now status-level diagnosable. See DONE → HO 407.
- ~~**`members` ↔ congress-legislators integrity sweep (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 411 (`db9d910`) + HO 412 (`a3a2a18`), both verified live.** It was **systemic, not the appointee churn HO 404 implied**: the read-only audit (HO 410, `scripts/diagnostic/senator-year-audit-410.ts`) found **18 of 100 current senators** carrying a `next_election_year` that disagreed with their Senate class — a wrong-anchor derivation in `sync:members` (`senateTermStart` / start-year+6), hitting long-serving elected members (Cornyn, Markey, Booker, Wyden, Murkowski, Scott, Kelly, Schiff), not just appointees. **Fixed by class-derivation (411):** `members.senate_class` is now ingested from `legislators-current.yaml` (Congress.gov carries no class) in the `sync:members` run, and the year-pair derives from it via `lib/derive-term.ts` residue math (class 1≡2, 2≡4, 3≡0 mod 6, computed from `now` so it doesn't rot at the November boundary), with a cycle-scoped self-expiring special-election list (`data/senate-special-elections.json`) for the OH/FL 2026 specials, checked ahead of the class default. The 20 drifted rows (18 + OH/FL) corrected; audit re-run **fully green** (§1=0, §2=0, §3=0, §4=0/0). **Race-table reconciled (412):** the CO card corrected in place (Bennet→Hickenlooper) via a self-healing `backfill:races` upsert scoped to `incumbent_bioguide_id` (which also healed 16 stale future-cycle cards), the `S-CA-2026`/`S-NY-2026` phantoms deleted (0 dependents), and the `fix:senate-term-years` footgun removed. **`backfill:races` now filters `is_current=1`** so a departed senator's fallback year can't recreate the two-at-2026 ambiguity. The 6 missing 2026 seats (NJ/MN/MS/NE + OH/FL) were created by 411's backfill and all carry ratings → all on `/races`. **Correction to the HO 404 flags:** the "impossible year" (Armstrong `A000383` `2031`) and "class drift" (Lankford `L000575` `2026`) were real and are fixed; the **`is_current` flag was a MIS-FLAG** — Mullin `M001190` `is_current=0` is **correct** (he resigned 2026-03-23, is absent from `legislators-current.yaml`, and Armstrong holds the seat), which HO 410 §4 proved (`is_current` clean, 0/0). The permanent regression guard is the HO 410 audit (now committed). See DONE → HO 411/412.
- ~~**`/lobbying` VOLUME sort — PROMOTED WATCH -> OPEN LOOP (HO 544; promoted HO 596)**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 597 (`82eaa24` guard split · `daf6dcc` migration · `387c56f` writer · `f2f4915` rewire · `418e80c` falsification), live on prod at `418e80c`, prod-verified. CLOSED FOR THE PATH IT NAMED; two residuals split out as their own entries below, one of them NEW and worse.** **Two defects, not one.** (1) *The one this entry named:* VOLUME ordered by a request-time `COUNT(*) FROM lda_activities GROUP BY filing_uuid`. Materialized to `lda_filings.activity_count` (+ `idx_lda_filings_activity(activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)`, forced via `INDEXED BY`), so the subquery and the JOIN are gone and it is a pre-ordered single-table walk. (2) *Found while fixing (1), and it is why the degradation lasted:* **the HO 440/448 guard sat INSIDE the `unstable_cache` callback, so its degraded `{items:[],…}` was the callback's RETURN VALUE and was CACHED FOR AN HOUR** — one cold miss poisoned that key for 60 minutes and the retry that would have succeeded never ran (the HO 552 class: a transient failure persisted by an exit path that records instead of deferring). The cached function now throws and the guard catches in an exported wrapper; the guard is NOT stripped. **FALSIFIED FOUR-SIDED.** *RED, co-located pdx1, shipped LIMIT 13:* the old shape breaches EVERY VOLUME variant and **does not finish in 55 SECONDS** (55002ms / 55001ms at a 55s ceiling) — worse than STEP 0 could see, because **every `20.00Xs` figure in this arc is the INSTRUMENT'S CEILING, not a measurement**: `lib/db.ts` aborts at 10s and retries once, so any query slower than 10s reports ~20.00s whether it takes 21 seconds or five minutes. STEP 0's RED is therefore **>20s (abort ceiling)**, and the true cost measured here is **>55s co-located** (itself a 55s ceiling). The data is RIGHT-CENSORED at the bound — a figure at the ceiling is a lower bound, only figures under it are durations. *GREEN, same conditions, worst of 3-5 cold runs:* volume **564ms (+9.44s margin)** · volume page-40 **1.48s (+8.52s)** · volume+linked **2.97s (+7.03s)**. *CONTROL* (`scripts/diagnostic/lda-volume-falsify-597.ts` — runs the OLD and NEW SQL against the SAME live state and diffs them, not a temporal before/after, because the corpus grows daily): the visible range **1,000 rows IDENTICAL position-for-position**, the clamp boundary identical in three forms incl. the shipped real LIMIT/OFFSET, the bill-linked branch identical, `SUM(activity_count)` == `COUNT(lda_activities)` exactly, **0 of 429 zero-activity filings visible** (rank-1000 `activity_count` = **11**, they carry 0), and the boundary key is unique so the visible set is fully determined. *DEGRADATION PATH FIRED:* matched RED/GREEN pair on one cheap cache key with one injected failure — RED served the stored empty on hits 2-3 in <600ms without touching the DB, GREEN re-queried and recovered 13 rows; the guard fired exactly ONCE in both, so the caching was the difference, not the instrument. **The comment that justified the join shape said "~295" zero-activity filings; measured 421 at STEP 0 and 429 now** — fixed in the rewire commit, since a stale number in a comment justifying a query shape is how this family gets through. (I then made the same error one line over — "~421k rows" for the scan size, conflating the zero-filing count with a row count; corrected to the measured 278,095 in `4051f9b`.) **The writer is proven end-to-end, not just by backfill:** a live `sync:lda` after the deploy ingested 63 filings / 97 activities through the new upsert, after which `SUM(activity_count)` 278,192 == `COUNT(lda_activities)` 278,192, **0 NULLs across 129,401 filings**, and 0 mismatches in the newest 80 rows. **Original WATCH text and the HO 596/597 correction history are preserved verbatim in git** (`git show d7fa0c2:docs/backlog.md` and `f190c38`) rather than re-copied here; the load-bearing part of the correction survives above, namely that "degradation, not a crash" was an accurate description of a working guard and it was the COST ESTIMATE that measurement contradicted.
- **`/lobbying` VOLUME x bill-linked x deep page still breaches — the residual HO 597 did NOT clear (opened HO 597).** *Owner:* Code. Three of four VOLUME variants clear the 10s bound with +7.03s to +9.44s of margin after the materialization. The fourth does not: **`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` measured 10003ms\*, 5097, 6022, 7582, 15620 co-located — worst 15.62s, margin -5.62s, breached 2 of 5 cold runs** (\* aborted at the ceiling). That is a large improvement — RED was ">55s, breaches always" — but it is **not a clear, and rounding it to one would be exactly the over-claim HO 594 had to retract.** **Reachable:** 33,800 filings are bill-linked and the pager offers 40 pages regardless of filter (the total comes from the rollup blob's corpus count, not the filtered set). **Mechanism:** the walk is pre-ordered, but the `EXISTS` is probed PER ROW, so reaching OFFSET 507 costs ~520 correlated seeks whose cold page fetches dominate — the **HO 594 page-residency residual class, not a plan shape**, so an index cannot save it either. *Named fix:* a second materialization — a `bill_linked` column on `lda_filings` + `(bill_linked, activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)` — which is beyond what HO 597 was scoped and priced for, so it was **filed rather than silently widened into**. *Severity is genuinely lower than it was:* after the guard split, a breach here degrades ONE request and the next re-queries, instead of poisoning the key for an hour, and the log line names the exact key. *Close:* co-located cold worst-case under 10s on that combination, margin stated as a number.
- **`/lobbying?q=` search — NARROWED, NOT CLOSED (opened HO 597, fixed HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **The wrong answer is gone and the loop stays open.** The defect was that `?q=boeing` rendered *"No filings match"* against **78 real matching filings** — a wrong answer with a 200. Prod now returns **13 rows, count 78**. **Four things shipped.** (1) `79d1276` — a timed-out read renders *"Search timed out — try a narrower term"*, DISTINCT from the genuine *"No filings match"*; the count line renders `—` not `0`. The guard always knew the difference and never said so. (2) `1afdd02` — the pager `COUNT` is bounded to the clamp it feeds (any total past 520 was already discarded by the code asking for it); common term 58.1s → 2.25s, rendered `520+`. (3) `4fd9b48`/`e7fdf30`/`85a173f` — `lda_names` (33,620 distinct `(kind, entity_id, name)` rows + `filing_count`) and the two id indexes, maintained by the sync. (4) `e4f09ba`+`06d03dd` — **the routed read**: leg 1 resolves the term against `lda_names` (ids AND density in ONE covering scan), then **empty → return without touching `lda_filings` at all** (a proof, since `lda_names` is complete), **sparse → seek by the literal ids**, **dense → hand back to the `dt_posted` walk**. **Numbers, co-located, 8 runs per regime:** sparse median **3510ms** worst 7735ms (was 19.8s) · dense median **3378ms** worst 6491ms · zero-match median **3119ms** worst 6461ms (was **91.3s**, now typically **~400ms** on prod because it never reaches the table). **All 24 runs under the bound.** **Threshold `m* = √(k·N) ≈ 1,297` is DERIVED, not tuned** — a formula over `PAGE_SIZE` and the blob's `stats.filings`, so it re-derives as the corpus grows; verified on a STRADDLING pair (`insur` 0.86× → seek, `hospital` 1.16× → walk), both row-identical to the shipped predicate. **SHIPPED OVER A FAILED GATE, recorded as a judgment call.** Gate statistic amended to upper-tail (worst − median), since a fast outlier is evidence of safety: dense **+3.51s vs 3.11s PASS**, empty **+3.54s vs 3.34s PASS**, **sparse +2.27s vs 4.23s FAIL**. Shipped on strict dominance — the replaced path breached on BOTH `lib/db.ts` attempts at its *best* observed (19.8s), so withholding preserved a ~certain failure to avoid a rare one, and the rare one now renders honestly. **HO 594's retraction was for CLOSING on a thin margin; this improves on one.** **WHAT REMAINS, and why this entry stays open:** the residual is **leg 1** — a ~33,620-row / ~1 MB covering scan ranging **0.3–6.6s**, dominating every path. It is ~1/23 the bytes of the walk this arc started from and the cost fell roughly linearly, *and the ~2-15× run-to-run spread came along at every size*. **The residual is OWNED BY THE VARIANCE THREAD**, not by more query design — there is very little data left to remove. **The honest-timeout render is the safety net and stays regardless of what happens to the residual.** *Close:* only when a sparse search clears the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail — i.e. when the variance question is answered. **Instruments:** `namesUnreachable` in the LDA sync payload + `chronicErr` + a greppable `[lda] names-unreachable:` (a gap makes a filing INVISIBLE to search and renders as a confident "No filings match"); `scripts/diagnostic/lda-names-maintenance-598.ts` proves the maintenance path; `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` re-runs equivalence. **A bug shipped inside this fix and was caught in prod in minutes — record both halves (HO 599).** `e4f09ba` rewired the page query onto the routed predicate but left the bounded `COUNT` binding **2 args against the 17-arg routed predicate**; prod degraded to an empty feed and the HO 597 log line named the failing cache key, fixed at `06d03dd`. **What let it through is a coverage gap worth its own note:** the page query and the pager `COUNT` run in **parallel inside one guard**, and the equivalence harness compared only the page query's rows — so it never executed the COUNT and reported green (oddities: *verifying one branch of a `Promise.all` is not coverage of the pair*). The equivalence harness above still has that gap; **extend it to execute the COUNT branch before it is trusted again** — that is a code change, filed here rather than done in a doc sweep. The offsetting fact, and the reason this reads as the system working: both safety nets that caught it — the honest-timeout render (`79d1276`) and the failing-key log line (HO 597 `82eaa24`) — were built in the preceding four HOs.
- ~~**Query-cost VARIANCE — the 2-15× run-to-run spread on byte-identical work; PROMOTED to its own loop (opened HO 599, the residual of HO 598).**~~ **CLOSED by the Aug 7 platform read — the DB tier is EXONERATED and the spread was CLIENT-SIDE PATH; the close paragraph is at the end of this entry, and the body below is kept because it records what was believed on the way.** *Owner:* Corey (the platform read) / Code (the re-measure). **Three HOs in a row were decided by this rather than by the query each was scoped around**, which is why it stops riding inside them: **594** (a covering index reached 140ms-1.5s steady state and the first touch after a >60s gap still cost 8.2-14.1s), **597** (`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` improved hugely and still breached **2 of 5** cold runs, worst 15.62s), **598** (the entire residual of `/lobbying?q=` is leg 1 — a ~33,620-row / **~1 MB** covering scan ranging **0.3-6.6s**, at ~1/23 the bytes of the walk it replaced, carrying the same spread). **The bytes model buys the median and does not touch the variance** (oddities), and there is very little data left to remove on this path — so more query design is not the lever. **The standing explanation is sub-60s page residency on a remote store, and it has never been probed on its own terms:** it was inferred from HO 594's `?order=` / `?warm=1` controls, which pinned *that* cold spike to pages that aren't there but say nothing about why byte-identical work varies 2-15× run to run. **Distinct from the read-budget WATCH below** — that asks *who is spending rows*, this asks *why identical work takes 2-15× longer on some runs*; they share a blocker, not a question. *Blocked on a platform read only Corey can run:* `turso org usage`, the plan tier, the **primary region against the Vercel function region**, and whether the DB carries replicas (the `turso` CLI is not installed in this environment and `.env` holds only the DB `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`, no platform API token — the same block that skipped HO 582's M4b). *Close:* the spread is **explained** (a named mechanism, with those platform facts on the record) or **bounded** (a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number) — and, concretely, a sparse `/lobbying?q=` clears the 10s bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail, which is also the close criterion of the search loop above; the two close together or neither does. *Instrument:* re-run `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` co-located and report **worst − median per regime**, never a range (oddities: gate on the upper tail, since a fast outlier is evidence of safety). A run that reads a materially narrower spread than 2-15× on identical work is itself the finding — it would mean the variance is conditional on something this arc never isolated.  **HO 602 — THE PLATFORM READ IS IN, AND IT KILLS TWO HYPOTHESES.** *(Turso dashboard, org `csu-j3`, read 2026-08-05; no script, not reproducible from the repo.)* Plan **Developer** ($5.99/mo, **2.5B rows read/mo**, +$1/B overage). DB primary **`aws-us-west-2`** (`libsql://cbt-csu-j3.aws-us-west-2.turso.io`), single group `default`, **no replicas, no branches**; Vercel functions pinned **`pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) — us-west-2. **Store and compute are in the same AWS region, so cross-region latency and replica routing are both DEAD.** **The standing candidate is Developer-tier multi-tenancy** (Turso's own launch post describes the tier as a massive multi-tenant server) — the shape that leaves a median alone while fanning an upper tail, and consistent with the sub-60s page-residency story rather than replacing it. **There is no purchasable lever:** the plan comparison table carries quota, retention and governance rows and **no performance row at any tier**, so a Scaler upgrade is an **unfalsifiable experiment** and was rejected rather than run. **This loop is therefore now committed to closing by BOUNDING, not by explaining** — a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number — which was always the second half of its own criterion; the *explained* half is no longer reachable with the levers available. **The blocker is discharged: do not re-file this as blocked on a platform read.** *Instrument:* unchanged (`scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts`, **worst - median per regime**).  **CLOSED — THE AUG 7 PLATFORM READ, AND IT CLOSED BY *EXPLAINING*, WHICH THIS ENTRY HAD GIVEN UP ON.** *(Turso Analytics + Vercel dashboards, read 2026-08-07; no script, not reproducible from the repo — same standing as the HO 602 read.)* **(1) THE DB TIER IS EXONERATED.** Turso's own **p50 / p95 / p99 run FLAT from Jul 18 → Aug 7** — the entire window in which the 2-15× spread was observed. A store whose percentiles do not move cannot be the source of a 2-15× fan on the caller's clock, so **Developer-tier multi-tenancy — the standing candidate this entry ADOPTED at HO 602 — is dead**, alongside the cross-region and replica hypotheses HO 602 had already killed. **(2) THE SPREAD WAS CLIENT-SIDE PATH, and the instrument was measuring itself.** Every run that produced the 2-15× figures fired **from the Windows box** — home route plus a fresh per-run TLS handshake to a US region — a cost path **prod does not have and never had**, and one that is **structurally invisible to the store's percentiles because it is spent before the query arrives**. *The numbers that opened this loop were measurements of the LINK, attributed to the QUERY.* **(3) The Jul 8-14 latency band predates tracking** — visible at the left edge, no instrumentation behind it, **unexplained and deliberately not chased**; recorded so its absence from this paragraph is not read as absence from the chart. **(4) TOPOLOGY, HEADER-VERIFIED rather than config-inferred:** `curl -sI …/api/version` returns `X-Vercel-Id: sfo1::pdx1::…`, so the **executing function region is `pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) against the Turso primary **`aws-us-west-2`** in the DB hostname — **HO 602's co-location claim STANDS and is now measured rather than inferred.** Fluid on, Standard CPU. **The near-miss is recorded because it is the lesson:** this session's dashboard read reported the function region as **`iad1` (Overridden)** — which would have put compute cross-country from its store and made HO 602's claim FALSE — and it was **caught by repo cross-check before it landed**; the Overridden badge marks the **INERT dashboard default**, config-as-code wins. ***A dashboard panel is a claim; `vercel.json` and a hostname are the record; a response header beats both.*** **(5) STANDING RULE:** do **not** reopen without new **PROD-SIDE** evidence — a co-located run, a runtime log, a response header. **A slow diagnostic run from the dev box is now a KNOWN NON-SIGNAL.** **AND THE SEARCH LOOP ABOVE DOES *NOT* CLOSE WITH IT, though this entry said the two close together or neither does.** That clause is honoured by naming what actually changed: `/lobbying?q=`'s criterion is **a co-located sparse run clearing the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail**, and **no such run has been made** — what closes here is only that its residual is **no longer owned by an unexplained variance thread**, and that the re-measure it still needs must be **prod-side/co-located**, because a dev-box re-run cannot clear a bound it was never measuring. *Criterion, honestly scored:* the entry had committed to closing by **bounding**, judging the *explained* half unreachable with the levers available — **it closed by explaining anyway, from a lever it never considered: measuring the instrument's own path instead of the query's.**
- **`getBillLobbying`'s cost note is undated and predates the table's current cost (opened HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **Not urgent, and filed rather than fixed because the SHAPE is sound.** `lib/queries.ts:3249` records the live below-threshold branch as *"measured 2.3s, 7.7s under the 10s cap"* — with **no corpus size and no date**, which is the exact SKILL authoring rule this arc added one HO earlier, catching one of its own. Bounded seeks, not a scan. It is also already `try/catch -> null`, so a breach hides the section rather than 500ing the bill page — which is the same silent-degradation shape HO 597 fixed elsewhere, and the reason this is worth a WATCH rather than a shrug. *Close:* re-measure the worst below-threshold bill co-located and restamp the comment WITH its corpus size and date, or promote if it breaches.

## QUEUED

*Next builds, roughly ordered. Pull from here when picking up work.*

- **`isSettled` freezes a contest the instant it is SCORED, so a winner that arrives late can never land — the parent of the 11 (opened HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. `lib/primaries-sync.ts:551-565`: a row is settled ⇔ `primary_date < today` **AND** some `primary_candidates` row carries a non-NULL `vote_pct`. **There is no winner term.** The guard runs at all three sync write paths (`:646` `scrapeSenateSpecialState`, `:905` `syncSenateCandidates`, `:1311` `syncHouseDistricts`) **before the incoming roster is built**, and `status` and `vote_pct` are written in the **same INSERT** (`:685` / `:948` / `:1380`). So a contest scraped in the window *after* Ballotpedia posts results but *before* it marks a winner takes shares + `running` and **becomes settled in that same instant** — every later tick refuses it. It is a race against Ballotpedia's call. **516 of 718 completed contests are settled, and most of those froze CORRECTLY**, having been marked before they settled: the damage is the 11 that froze mid-write, so **the mechanism is the defect, not the count**. The arithmetic closes exactly — 506 with-winner + 11 = 517 against 516 with-shares, the one-row difference being **`senate-MI-2026-R`** (1 candidate, winner marked, no share recorded — uncontested). **TWO dispositions, independent of each other.** **(1) STOP NEW LOSSES — the guard fix, and it is NOT a one-liner.** Adding a winner term to `isSettled` un-freezes every contest that legitimately has no winner — uncontested seats like `senate-ME-2026-R`, where Ballotpedia marks no votebox winner because there is no contest — and those would re-scrape forever. A correct fix needs a **bounded re-check** (eligible for N days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless) or an attempt counter, and **N has to be measured against how long Ballotpedia actually takes to call.** Probe, then build, under its own number. **(2) RECOVER THE EXISTING 11 — the script already ships; do not build one.** `isSettled` is module-private and guards only the three sync paths: **`npm run backfill:primary-results`** bypasses it entirely (`scripts/backfill-primary-results.ts` UPDATEs `vote_pct` AND `status`, no settled check — its only date clause *targets* past contests), as does `reingest:primary-slate`. **Gated on one measurement: has Ballotpedia marked those voteboxes since?** One live fetch against one contest answers it — a live fetch, therefore not a docs-HO action. **Framing, corrected in review:** the freeze is **unreachable by any automatic path, NOT irreversible**. Calling it permanent would justify building a repair that already exists. What is true is the reporting: the cron reports success every tick while never touching these rows — the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637) on the ingestion side. **HO 656 — DISPOSITION (2) EXECUTED AND PROVEN ON REAL DATA; DISPOSITION (1) NOW HAS A RECOMMENDED SHAPE AND AN HONEST NON-MEASUREMENT.** The gate was one live fetch of `house-NJ-09-2026-R`: **Pino now parses `isWinner=true` at 51.3%**, so Ballotpedia had marked it since — the freeze is **unreachable, not irreversible**, exactly as the corrected framing said. `npm run backfill:primary-results` then ran off-tick (**398 seats, 370 ok / 28 empty-or-failed, 1,926 rows updated, `vote_pct` 1,663 → 2,077 of 2,527**) and took the frozen set **11 → 6**: **7 recovered** (CA-04/14/16/43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09), **4 residual** (WA-05, WA-08, ME-D, ME-R). **The residuals were live-checked, not inferred:** all three pages parse `ok` with **zero winners marked**, so the residue is **upstream-unmarked, NOT a parse failure** — a distinction that changes the disposition, since a parse failure would be our bug. **THE POPULATION IS A MOVING SET, NOT A FIXED 11**, and that is the durable correction: two rows entered the class *during the run* (`house-AZ-03-2026-R`, 1 candidate/uncontested; `house-VA-10-2026-R`, 2 candidates, Aug 4) because writing shares ahead of the call is what settles a row. So the mechanism is **live and continuously recoverable**, and re-running the backfill is maintenance, not a one-off repair. **N CANNOT BE MEASURED FROM OUR DATA — say it plainly rather than fitting one:** we know only that Ballotpedia *had* marked the recovered 7 by the run date, never *when*, so those are upper bounds only; the live residuals give the other end at **9 days (WA) and 65 days (ME) still unmarked**. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE: a bounded re-check window, N = 30 days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless** — chosen **generously, not fitted**; cost is N wasted fetches on an uncontested seat, and at 30 the recent contests stay eligible while ME settles. The attempt-counter alternative needs a column, hence a migration, hence out of scope by construction. **Corpus figures re-derived: completed contests 768** (entry: 718), **with-shares 517 → 658** across the run. **The build is QUEUED below under its own number; `isSettled` was NOT edited here.**
- **QUEUED — build the bounded re-check that stops NEW freezes (recommended HO 656, parent above).** *Owner:* Code. **Shape: a contest is re-check-eligible for N days past `primary_date`, then settles regardless**, replacing today's settle-the-instant-it-is-scored. **N = 30, chosen GENEROUSLY rather than fitted, and the reason is that N is NOT MEASURABLE from our data** — recovery timing gives upper bounds only (we know Ballotpedia *had* marked, never *when*), and the live residuals sit unmarked at 9 days (WA) and 65 (ME). At 30 the recent contests stay eligible while the structurally-unmarkable ones (uncontested, RCV) settle instead of re-fetching forever — which is the failure the naive winner-term fix has. **Cost is N wasted fetches per uncontested seat; say that in the build rather than pretending the parameter is free.** **The attempt-counter alternative is DISQUALIFIED, not rejected on merit** — it needs a column, so it is a migration. *Close:* the window ships and a contest scored-then-called inside N lands its winner without a manual backfill. **Until then `backfill:primary-results` is the recovery path and re-running it is MAINTENANCE, not repair** — the frozen population moves (HO 656 watched two rows join mid-run).
- **QUEUED — the challenger harvest has no schedule; it runs when a human remembers (scoped HO 656, not wired).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. HO 656 measured the cost of that: the harvest sat at its FIRST-RUN figure (82 rows / 53 seats) until run by hand, and one run moved **94 seats** into having a challenger. **Two shapes priced. (a) Chain it after the daily primaries sync** — its input is exactly what that sync writes, so freshness is ideal; **but `/api/cron/primaries` is deadline-bounded at 50s inside a 60s ceiling and walks a per-unit cursor**, so added work trades cursor progress for harvest freshness, and the cursor is the thing that makes a killed tick resumable. **(b) Its own daily cron slot.** **RECOMMENDED: (b)** — the harvest is pure DB-to-DB (no Ballotpedia fetches), it is idempotent under its sentinel, and it does not need to ride the sync's transaction, so it buys freshness without spending the cursor's budget. Read cost is small against the HO 602 picture (the top consumers are the `bills_agg`/participation aggregates, not this). *Close:* a slot is chosen and wired under its own number, or the manual cadence is accepted **on the record** with the 94-seat staleness figure attached. **Nothing was wired at HO 656.** **RULED AT HO 659 — slot (b), its own daily cron slot, is CHOSEN on the record; the wiring is HO 660.** The recommendation above is adopted unchanged and for its stated reasons (pure DB-to-DB, idempotent under the sentinel, no claim on the primaries cursor's 50s budget). **This entry stays OPEN**: its close criterion is *chosen AND wired*, and only the first half is done — a ruling with no cron line is still a harvest that runs when a human remembers.
- **~~QUEUED — `race_candidates` has no `updated_at`, so a harvest's effect can only be bracketed, never dated (scoped HO 656, migration deferred).~~ — SHIPPED HO 659** (`00b07eb`). `updated_at TEXT` added by `ensureColumn` in `main()` (never the CREATE array — the HO 597 `activity_count` rule), with both writers stamping: the harvest's `INSERT OR IGNORE` and `seed-races`'s upsert, whose `DO UPDATE` sets it too. **Writers were re-derived, not trusted: exactly two**; every other reference in `scripts/`+`lib/` is a SELECT. **Semantics pinned — reach, not last content change** (an idempotent rewrite stamps), one `runStamp` per invocation so run identification is a GROUP BY, and **NULL = pre-instrumentation, never backfilled**. **Both falsification legs ran.** Post-migration non-NULL = **0** (the migration stamps nothing, proven). Post-harvest: non-NULL **206 = the sentinel row count**, **one distinct stamp** across 206 rows / 147 seats, and the sentinel content checksum **byte-identical** to the pre-migration capture (`127aeac…`, 206 rows) with zero row-count drift. Negative leg: the **11 curated rows are still NULL**, count unchanged — which proves the migration didn't stamp AND that the harvest's skip-curated guard still holds with the new column in the statement. Instrument: `scripts/diagnostic/race-candidates-stamp-659.ts`. *Original shape, retained for the record —* *Owner:* Code. The table records `source_url` (the `harvest:primary_winner` sentinel) but nothing temporal, so “when did this seat gain its challenger” is answerable only by diffing counts around a known run — **which is exactly the bracketing pain HO 642 hit** and why HO 656 had to capture a before-state by hand rather than reading one. **It is a migration and therefore out of scope for any HO that has not budgeted one** (the same rule that disqualified the attempt-counter above). *Close:* `updated_at TEXT` lands via `ensureColumn` with the harvest and the seed writing it, after which the sentinel rows carry their own provenance and a staleness check is one query instead of a run-diff.

- **A name market that resolves to no active roster member is a stale-roster detector — already computed, already discarded (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `favoredMember`'s name-market branch does `roster.find(m => nameKey(m.name) === nameKey(fav.favoriteLabel)) ?? null`: when a market names a **candidate** and no roster row matches, the function has already computed *"this market names someone who is not on this roster"* and **throws it away as a null**, falling back to a party label. On **2026-07-25** that condition went true for `S-ME-2026` on **both** feeds at once — Kalshi (65%) and Polymarket (69%) both naming **Troy Jackson** while the card's roster read Mills and Platner — and stayed true on **every render for roughly two and a half weeks**, on a card whose `last_verified` read a green 2026-07-03. **Scope it precisely: the signal is the NAME-market branch failing to match** (`!favoriteIsParty && favoriteLabel` present, and no roster hit) — **NOT** `favoredMember` returning null generally. An unresolvable **party** market is a normal, first-class state (bare-party is ~65% of matched markets), so gating on the general null would make the detector fire constantly and be muted within a week. Beats `races.last_verified` as an instrument on both axes: it is **derived from an independent feed** rather than curator-set, and it **reads differently on a stale roster** instead of carrying the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637).

- **11 contests carry recorded `vote_pct` but no `status='winner'` row — ONE class, not two; the two-class split is FALSIFIED (opened HO 638, rewritten HO 641 on HO 640's measurement).** *Owner:* Code. Surfaced as the third independent bar on the S-ME defect: `backfill:race-challengers` derives general-election challengers from primary **winners**, so a contest with shares but no winner row is inert to it. **HO 639 asserted two classes with opposite fixes — top-two/jungle where a single `status='winner'` row is a category error and the harvest's model is wrong, and ranked-choice as a genuine ingestion gap. HO 640 falsified the first half by measurement.** Across **completed** contest-party `open` contests: **CA 52 total / 48 with winners / 48 carrying exactly TWO winner rows; WA 10 / 3 / 3; and ZERO contests anywhere carry exactly one.** Fifty-one top-two contests are marked correctly with two advancers each — the schema, the ingester and HO 207's two-★ rule all handle top-two properly. The 8 are **not** a model error; **they failed the same way ME and NJ did**, so the 11 are one class: scored, unmarked, and frozen. **The shapes are descriptive, not causal** — 8 contest-party `open` (CA-04/14/16/43, WA-05/07/08/09) · **2** ME ranked-choice (`senate-ME-2026-D` and `senate-ME-2026-R` are separate `primaries` rows; HO 639 counted them as one, which is exactly where its phantom "2 uncharacterized" residue came from) · **1** `house-NJ-09-2026-R`, an ordinary partisan primary — two candidates, Pino 51.5%, a derivable winner, no top-two or RCV excuse. A **fourth shape rides inside ME**: `senate-ME-2026-R` has **one candidate, Collins at 100.0%** — uncontested, so there is no votebox contest to mark, which is precisely why the naive guard fix is wrong (see the freeze parent). **Product impact is 5, not 11:** only NJ-09, WA-05, WA-08, ME-D and ME-R sit on rated seats the harvest reaches; the other 6 are unrated and it never reaches them regardless of status. Measured on the harvest's **real** join — `state` + `chamber` + `CAST(district AS INTEGER)`, gated by `EXISTS(race_ratings for the cycle)` — **not** `primaries.race_id`, which is what HO 640's own first pass used before re-measuring 11 → 5 (oddities). **This line is now a SYMPTOM. Its parent is the `isSettled` freeze, above.** **HO 656 — 7 OF THE 11 RECOVERED, 4 RESIDUAL, AND THE COUNT IS NO LONGER THE POINT.** Recovered: CA-04, CA-14, CA-16, CA-43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09. Residual: **WA-05, WA-08** (Aug 4, top-two, 12 and 6 candidates) and **ME-D, ME-R** (Jun 9) — every one of them **live-verified as unmarked UPSTREAM** (`status=ok`, `winners=0`), so none is a parse gap. `senate-ME-2026-R` (Collins, uncontested, 100.0%) remains the expected-and-correct residual this entry already carved out. **The line is superseded as a COUNT** — HO 656 observed two fresh members join the class mid-run, so “11” was a snapshot of a moving set, not a fixed backlog. Track the mechanism on the freeze parent; this symptom stays only for its shape analysis (top-two two-winner marking is CORRECT, RCV and uncontested are structurally unmarkable).

- **There is no ingestion path for a convention nominee — S-ME was hand-seeded and the next one will be too (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. Every automatic route into `race_candidates` is primary-derived (`backfill:race-challengers` harvests `primary_candidates` winners), and a **ballot-vacancy / convention replacement never appears in a primary result** — Jackson was nominated at a 2026-07-25 party convention *after* the June 9 primary had already resolved to a different candidate who then withdrew. So the only way a `nominee` row exists today is a hand edit to `races-seed.json`, and the defect it fixes is invisible until someone eyeballs the card. **Size the demand before building:** how often a 2026 general-election candidate arrives by convention rather than primary is unmeasured. **Note the coupling to the vocabulary decision (HO 638):** `nominee` is a true claim whether or not anything ever writes it automatically, so this line can close as *"permanently hand-curated"* without disturbing the status value — what it must not do is quietly become an argument for collapsing `nominee` back into `won_primary`.

Bias floor = `f(N+7) − f(N)`: **N=0 → 47.0pp** (a flat week renders −47% WoW) · N=3 → 23.5pp · N=7 → 12.4pp · N=14 → 3.0pp · N=21 → −0.7pp. Live: w0 155 vs w1 106 is a naive **+46.2%**, but at equal maturity (both m=0) it is 155 vs **49**, **+216%** — understated ~4.7x. - ~~**`getPrimaryForRace` → prefer the soonest future contest (HO 560 §3.1).** The member-hub primary chip reconstructs `senate-{ST}-2026-{party}` literally, so it can't see `-special-` and keeps showing SC's June 9.~~ — **SHIPPED HO 576** (`e3cf396`), **but not as a soonest-future preference.** STEP 0 (M3a) **proved that query wrong** — with no `district` restriction it leaked per-district house rows and would have "fixed" 127 nulls with arbitrary district dates — and surfaced that the caller is **chamber-blind** (`district=null` for House *and* Senate members), so "prefer the soonest future" would hand a House member a Senate-only special that isn't theirs. Shipped instead as a query over structured columns with a **`memberChamber` argument** and the rule *a Senate special is never a valid proxy for a House member's primary in any state* (**no `p.chamber=?` in the WHERE**, so no House chip emptied). Verified delta: SC's two senators only. See roadmap "Also (HO 576–577)". Docs: HO 578.
- ~~**LA House 2026 follow-up (HO 577), with a clock.**~~ — **CLOSED HO 584** (`c40c447` C1 · `6188ac8` C2). Act 7 of the 2026 Regular Session restored the jungle system for LA U.S. House only (verified 2026-07-31): the six `house-LA-*-open` rows now carry Nov 3 / Dec 12 / `jungle` via the new `HOUSE_PRIMARY_OVERRIDES` map (the `HOUSE_PRIMARY_SUSPENDED` lock is gone). The precondition this item named — "remove LA from the lock in the same change that writes a real date" — was **necessary and insufficient**: unlocking alone would have COALESCEd the clean May-16 senate date onto the six rows (the HO 577 clobber through the front door), which is why the fix generalized the mechanism instead. Dates/type/mechanism all shipped and falsified; all six rows Nov 3 on prod. Two children spun out below (the `primary_type` fallback + the read-side chip disagreement). See roadmap "Also (HO 584)" + DONE → HO 584.
- ~~**`getPrimaryForRace` senate-proxy can't serve top-two and no-Senate-seat states (HO 576 STEP 0 carry-forward).** *Owner:* Code, low. The read-side twin of the frozen-gap WATCH's HO 209 coverage question; its own item. *Close:* give the chip a source that doesn't depend on a same-party statewide senate row.~~ — **CLOSED HO 586** (`a1a97a8`). The new district-first branch of `getPrimaryForRace` resolves a House member's OWN `house-{ST}-{DD}-2026-*` row (`p.party = ? OR 'open'`), which serves both the `-open` top-two states and the no-2026-Senate-seat states a party-keyed statewide proxy structurally couldn't — the **160 (a) null→non-null fills in the M3 full-corpus delta ARE this item's close** (534 members computed; (c) regressions = 0; senate = 0). The M3 table is the evidence. See roadmap "Also (HO 586)" + DONE → HO 586.

- **77 of 560 past-dated house primary rows carry 0 candidates (~14%) — roster data debt on `/primaries` + `/electoral` (HO 586 M5).** *Owner:* Code, P3. Surfaced (not created) by HO 586's chip fix — the district-first branch resolves a House member to their own `house-{ST}-{DD}` row, and 77 of the 560 past-dated house rows have an empty roster. Of the 77, the **19** that currently host a sitting member's chip were run down by M5: **11** are the incumbent's-party primary being genuinely uncontested (honestly no votebox — the district page parsed fine), **8** are both-party-empty district holes (**AR-01, SC-07, AL-02, ME-01, ME-02, NC-02, AL-07, AR-03**) — pre-existing HO 563-class roster residue or dual-uncontested, undetermined which; **the remaining 58 rows host no current member's chip and are uncharacterized as of HO 586.** Invisible on the member chip (date-only render), but the empty roster shows on `/primaries` + `/electoral`; independent of the chip fix. *Instrument:* count of past-dated house rows with zero candidates. *Close:* the zero-candidate past-row count either falls or every remaining row is explained as genuinely uncontested — the 8 holes explained-or-traced AND the 58 uncharacterized at least bucketed (uncontested vs hole); the count instrument must not read 77 while the item reads closed.
- **Independents get no primary chip — the caller gates `party ∈ {D,R}` (HO 586).** *Owner:* Code, low. `app/members/[bioguideId]/page.tsx` only calls `getPrimaryForRace` for D/R members, so an independent gets no chip; Kiley (`party='I'`, K000401) is the live instance. The data is correct per HO 420/422 (Kiley genuinely switched R→I 2026-03-09 while caucusing R — not a bug), and HO 586 makes the gap more conspicuous by giving nearly everyone else a working chip. *Close:* a decision — either an intentional-and-documented gate, or an all-party chip for I members.
- **`primary_type` is fallback-defaulted for ~45 states (HO 584).** *Owner:* Code, low (**P3**). `primaryTypeFor` (`lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) returns `open` for every state outside top_two/top_four/ranked_choice, so `open` is an **unverified catch-all** across ~45 states, not a determination — LA senate's stored `open` is the known-wrong instance (LA senate is `closed` under Act 1/640), and SC's `open` is right only by coincidence (oddities: *a default is not a determination*). *Close:* replace the fallback with a determined per-state map, or a distinct `unknown`/NULL default so a defaulted value is distinguishable from a determined one. *Instrument:* a determined-vs-defaulted count over `primaries.primary_type`.

- ~~**Member-hub Press tab has no out-link (HO 509–510)**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 512 (`af3ab90`).** `/news` gained a `?member=` mode and the Press tab its `View press coverage →` out-link. **The close-path's mechanism claim was WRONG, not just imprecise** — recorded so a reader who finds the old wording elsewhere knows which is right: `getNewsFeed` does **not** join `observation_entities`; it drives `news_mentions m INNER JOIN bills b`, so a person-filter bolted on there would have returned only the member's coverage that *also* matched a bill — a quietly short subset, not an error. The fix **branched to `getMemberNews`** (the exact query the Press tab previews) with **zero `lib/queries.ts` changes**. **Precedence:** when `member` parses it owns the view (other params ignored + not carried; ✕ → bare `/news`; `getMember` null → raw-id label + empty state, not a 404; parse-fail → unscoped feed). **Filter-bar asymmetry:** member mode renders the member pill only (observation rows have no source/topic/window/signal dims); bill mode keeps its chips (still `news_mentions`). **Cap asymmetry:** tab previews `getMemberNews(…, 8)`, out-link serves `NEWS_MEMBER_CAP = 50` — the out-link lands on *more* rows than the tab, so **no count in the label**. See roadmap "Also (HO 512)" + oddities. Docs: HO 513.
