- ~~**The `bills_agg` sponsor aggregate — the largest single read consumer on the database, untouched (HO 602).**~~ — **RESOLVED BY PROBE, HO 624** (`scripts/diagnostic/bills-agg-cost-624.ts`, on main at `55529d2`). **The 110M / 26% attribution was the PRE-595 statement, not `bills_agg`.** `participationAggCte()` full-scanned `member_votes` *inside the same query text* as `bills_agg`, and the per-query ledger attributes by statement text — so when HO 595 materialized that scan away in July, the dead cost stayed on a trailing 30-day leaderboard read 2026-08-05. **Measured current cost (2026-08-07, 17,463-bill corpus): 20,752 rows per call** for the full `getMembersRanked` statement — component sum 18,617 (`bills_agg` 17,463 + `part_agg` 554 + `members` 553 + `palestine_scorecard` 47), the ~2.1k remainder being ≈4 index seeks per member row across the three LEFT JOINs. That is **16.7× under the ledger's ~347k**, and the coincidence test is decisive: **`|347k − member_votes 365,996| = 18,996`** against **`|347k − measured| = 326,248`**. Plans re-verified covering on both sorts (`SEARCH bills USING COVERING INDEX idx_bills_sponsor_agg`); 14.1ms / 6.2ms, **one sample each**, laptop→us-west-2. **NOT the largest consumer; NOT worth a materialization** — the sketch would add ~1,092 written rows to save a 17k covering scan that reads in single-digit ms. **The congress lever is dead:** all 17,463 sponsored bills are congress 119, so a `congress = 119` clause excludes **zero** rows. *(`includeCeremonial` is not a lever either — both CTE variants read 17,463; the predicate filters rows RETURNED inside the covering scan, not rows read.)* **The probe's own instrument note is worth keeping:** `@libsql/client` v0.14.0 does not surface per-statement `rows_read`, so the probe reads Turso's hrana `/v2/pipeline` directly and *measures* what the ledger meters instead of deriving it — and a **bare `COUNT(*)` under-reports by 227–416×** (366,496 rows → `rows_read: 1617`) because it is answered from B-tree interior pages, so nothing may price a scan by bare count.

  **Variant-surface inventory** — the ledger groups by statement text, so these are **THREE separate lines**; confirm which one any future dashboard read is showing before attributing it: `getMembersRanked` (`queries.ts:6283`, key = filters × sort × page × pageSize — 72 combinations before state/q/page, × 56 states) · `getCommitteeRoster` (`queries.ts:6427`, per committee — 236 × 3 sorts × 2 = 1,416) · `searchMembers` (`queries.ts:7583`, per query string at a 600s TTL, **unbounded by construction**).

  **THE REUSABLE LESSON — date every per-query ledger read against the fixes that landed inside its window.** A killed statement stays on the leaderboard for the length of the window: a 30-day view read 2026-08-05 still contained weeks of executions from a query shape that stopped existing in July, ranked by a cost nothing had paid since. This is the HO 420/577 invalidation class at ledger scale, and it is the measured-number rule one level up — a ledger line is a measurement of `f(query, corpus, window)` being read as a property of the query.

  **STILL OPEN, found in passing (the residual this entry carries forward):** `searchMembers`' lean `bills_agg` copy (`queries.ts:7583`) carries **no `INDEXED BY`** and plans onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` as a **non-covering MULTI-INDEX OR** — the HO 277/329 misplan class, live on a request path. Measured **35,129 rows / 594.6ms, 2.01× the hinted plan** (the row-fetch doubling a non-covering OR predicts). Cheap fix — add the hint, mirroring `billsAggCte` — **not done at HO 624, which was read-only.** *Owner:* Code.
- **Absence Watch — a front-page strip naming members who have missed a vote threshold (HO 588 STEP 0; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Code. **State: SHIPPED at HO 622** (`abd8768` data layer · `12b3e57` band), after the HO 621 probe redux re-ran it against a corpus four months newer than the one that scoped it. **THE FORK RESOLVED TO NEITHER ARM — it went to the STREAK, and the ruling is three sentences.** A cumulative trigger makes a **present-tense claim off a career statistic**, and the 621 corpus priced exactly that: **6 sticky false positives to 1 blind false negative** — six House members above 15% cumulative had missed 0–2 of the last 30 roll calls and five of them voted on the final pre-recess roll call, while Biggs (26 of 30 missed, 9.0% cumulative) was invisible to it. **The copy is dated, never "now"** — *no vote cast since {date}* survives a recess, and the House had not voted in 15 days on the day this shipped, so a present-tense phrasing would have been false about that entire chamber the moment it recessed. **The division of labour is acute-vs-chronic**: this band is *acute* (gone), the HO 527 participation dot-plot and the HO 535 MISSED sort are *chronic* (spotty), so a 26-of-30-with-streak-0 member is correctly absent here and correctly present there — do not later "fix" one surface into the other. **WATCH — S = 30 is launch-conservative.** Revisit after a live cycle. A long-empty strip is C4 working, not a broken threshold (the empty state renders nothing at all, by design); the signal that S sits above the corpus is a strip that **never fires across a full ACTIVE session**, which is a different observation from "it is empty today during a recess." **Live at ship (2026-08-07): exactly two rows — McConnell (51 consecutive, no vote cast since Jun 11, 14.1% of 119th) and Wilson (34, since Jul 16, 29.3%)**, the streaks being the true figures past the probe's 30-roll window cap. Cost as shipped: **1,064 rows returned / ~516ms cold**, one regen a day on the `votes` tag. *Superseded below — the fork text is kept because it is the reasoning the ruling overturned:* **THE FORK WAS UNRESOLVED AND WAS RECORDED HERE AS UNRESOLVED:** **R2** = cumulative missed *rate* vs **R3** = a recent window. R2 was preferred in the design conversation as **sticky** (a member cannot fall off the strip by having one good week) and as **near-free**, because it reused `getParticipationStrip` at a marginal read of ~0. **THE COST HALF OF THAT ARGUMENT HAS EXPIRED — this is the reason not to resume from the design conversation.** HO 594/595 rewrote the participation path onto the materialized `member_participation` table, and HO 602 measured that the **pre-materialization `getParticipationStrip` had been 22% of the entire account read budget** (91.3M rows / 30d). *"Reuses `getParticipationStrip`, marginal read ~0"* was a true statement about a function that no longer exists in that form. **Re-derive the cost of BOTH arms against the current materialized path before choosing.** R3's standing merit is unchanged and is what cumulative is blind to: **a member who stopped showing up last month is invisible inside a long denominator.** **Population + delegate carve, copied from `getParticipationStrip` so the strip and the HO 527 dotplot cannot disagree:** `is_current = 1`; `HAVING COUNT(*) >= PARTICIPATION_FLOOR` (50); delegates carved by the territorial predicate `chamber = 'house' AND state IN ('DC','AS','GU','MP','PR','VI')` — **population-correctness, not outlier-trimming** (HO 527: the real high-miss tail is a *voting* member, Wilson at 29%). *Scope:* **re-read `scripts/diagnostic/absence-watch-588.ts` AND ITS OUTPUT before scoping. Do not resume from the design conversation that produced the fork — it predates the materialization.**
- **Dashboard redesign — the two-region declutter (HO 590 mock arc; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Design + Code. **State: SHIPPED across HO 608–610 (P3).**  Originally: iterated through **revision J** in conversation. **THE MOCK FILE DOES NOT EXIST** — `mock-590-dashboard-declutter.html` is absent from `docs/design/` entirely (not merely untracked; audited HO 603 against both the worktree and `git ls-files`). `docs/design/dashboard-2col.html` is **NOT** it — that is an HO 254 artifact (`f0cec04`, 2026-06-17) with none of this arc's markers. **So this entry IS the artifact; there is no pointer to follow.** Decisions, each of which was *corrected during* the arc and will be re-litigated if not written down: **(1) Two regions** — a full-height bills/movers column on the **RIGHT**, spanning **from the very top, level with the masthead**, NOT starting below the nav; everything else stacks in the left region (masthead, market/odds tapes, nav, absence watch, hearings, week strip, breaking news, distributions). **(2) The tapes stay HORIZONTAL** — they are not converted to a vertical rail. **(3) Iteration on the existing dashboard — NO parallel v3 route**; sequenced handoffs against the live page (the HO 311 `/dashboard-v2` swap is the precedent for why a parallel route accrues a sunset loop). **(4) Row-level packing:** elements pack **left** via flex, because far-right-anchored elements were stretching rows and opening horizontal voids. **(5) Bills-column readability:** no boxed bill IDs, no chip borders, no sponsor underlines; a quiet **two-line** format; colour reserved **EXCLUSIVELY** for stage status (ENACTED green / FLOOR amber / CMTE dim); date-grouped sections **TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK**. **(6) CUT IN THE ARC — do not re-add:** the **Polarization band** (slow-moving data not worth its space), the **five-column week grid** for hearings (collapsed to a single-day schedule + a week ribbon), and **masthead counts that duplicated the stage-distribution chart**. **SHIPPED ACROSS HO 608–610 (P3).** All six decisions landed against the committed `docs/design/dashboard-layout-target.html`. **Three of them shipped as REFINEMENTS of the decision above, not violations of it, and each is recorded so it is not re-litigated as drift:** **(a) the Polarization band** was CUT as a band (decision 6) but its numbers survive **in miniature** as three chips in the week strip (`.weekly-band-polchip`, HO 608 §5) — the committed mock shows them, and the mock is the later artifact, so "CUT — do not re-add" applies to the full-width band and NOT to the chips; **(b) date-grouped TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK** (decision 5) was **superseded by the mock**, which has no date groups in the feed — those strings belong to the hearings day bar — and the rows carry a per-row relative age on line 2 instead (HO 609 §0); **(c) the masthead** packs fully left (decision 4) **deviating from the mock**, whose own `.mast` pins SIGN IN right with a `.sp{flex:1}` spacer — that spacer is exactly the C1 pattern, and SKILL owns the conventions, so the mock loses this one (HO 610). The **nav one-row-at-≥1200px target is RETRACTED**, and its shape is the reusable part: **a fixed-width target set before the element moved into a narrower container measures the old container.** The nav moved into `.dash-left` at HO 608, the feed column takes ~432px of the width the target was computed against, and no amount of nav tuning could meet a number derived from the old geometry. If the nav's wrapping is worth fixing it needs a new target measured against `.dash-left`, not the retracted one. **TWO MORE MOCK DEVIATIONS, recorded at HO 623 so the mock's numbers stop being the record where the app has moved past them.** **(d) The feed column is `minmax(480px, 31%)`, not the mock's 400/27%** — an owner ruling; measured, the column reads **779px at 2560 and 480px at its 1440 floor** (from 678 / 400), both under the ~850px C8 ceiling past which a row of text buys nothing from more width. **(e) The `bxp` container query is `max-width: 520px`, not 440** — and the reason is the reusable part, because *the handoff's stated reason for it did not survive measurement*: at 1440 the panel's inner width is **420px**, already below the old 440, so 1440 would have stayed stacked either way. The boundary that actually moves is **1680**, where the inner width is **446px** — under 440 the widened column would have flipped it to wide mode at a cramped 446px. **A container-query threshold is derived from the container it was tuned against, so widening the container obliges you to re-derive the threshold or it silently changes which viewports collapse** — that is the transferable rule, not the two numbers. `/bills` was checked rather than assumed (its panel is 1140px at 1440, far past either threshold), which is the point of a container query over a route branch.
- **Layout audit — the C1–C8 conventions and their M1–M6 instrument (HO 591 origin; filed HO 603; C2 SHIPPED HO 604; audit BUILT AND RUN HO 606).** *Owner:* Code. **State: BUILT AND RUN.** **This entry now reads M1–M6, not M1–M7, and the count change is deliberate: M7 was the C2 blast-radius static measurement, and HO 604 discharged it PRE-BUILD** — its numbers live in the globals.css --fs comment block and arc-md §2, so the audit implements six. `scripts/diagnostic/layout-audit-606.ts` (`e794194`, corrected `94f6543`) is on main and its numbers are in the HO 606 pasted output. C2 shipped at HO 604 (identity `4185325`, ladder `fd069b9`, rider `5b9c34c`). **Still unbuilt: the C1/C3/C4/C7 remediation — P3 onward.** **Read `docs/design/layout-conventions-arc.md` §3 first — it holds the full spec; this entry holds only what must survive independently.** **THREE OF THE EIGHT CONVENTIONS HAVE NO AUDIT INSTRUMENT, BY DESIGN — a decision, not a gap:** C5 and C6 are **review rules** (enforced by a reader, not a script) and **C8 is C1 seen from the other end** (a too-wide row presents as a C1 gap). So the audit prices four conventions, not eight, and phases are done on M1–M4 reaching zero while C5/C6 ride the eyeball pass. **Scoping findings that cost a session to establish and must not be re-derived:** test against **`next start` (a local production build), NOT `next dev`**; **`/` is gated behind a `ct_seen` cookie** (`app/page.tsx`) that must be set context-wide or the crawl measures `/welcome` and reports a clean dashboard it never visited; **M6 needs no browser at all** and is static analysis; and **900px is a BAD single test width** because it is the breakpoint at which the dashboard grid collapses — bracket it (1024/900/720) rather than measuring only at it. **VERDICT CONDITION — and HO 606 is now the worked example, not HO 604: the detector must be proven to fire on a known defect before any zero is trusted.** Pointed at `/`, the first build of M1 read a **10px** interior gap on the breaking row whose headline visibly ends ~1,100px from its timestamp, and M2 read zero; the leg **refused the crawl**, which is the whole reason it runs first. **WHAT A RE-RUN MUST KNOW:** (a) **exclude or dedupe the three redirect aliases** — `/races` and `/primaries` 308 → `/electoral`, `/members/pass-rate` 307 → `/members?sort=passrate` — the script's route list **still contains them** (`layout-audit-606.ts` ~L95–98) and removing them is a **code change deferred to the next audit run**, not a doc sweep; uncorrected they inflate site-wide M1 from **1,329 to 1,902**, and **27 distinct pages is the real denominator**, not 30. (b) **M1b counts INSTANCES, NOT PROBLEMS** — `/members`' 565 is one row component rendered 565 times, so **remediation is priced per component**, and a phase plan that reads the raw count as 565 units of work is reading it wrong. (c) **Raw counts inflate ~1.4×** because one visual gap is counted once per ancestor wrapper; dedupe to the innermost element of an ancestor chain carrying the same gap. (d) **The per-route counts are the cross-run currency; selector paths are human pointers only** — remediation renames classes, so a cross-run comparison keyed on class names goes blind exactly where the fix landed (the HO 604 keyed-diff oddity). **Where the numbers live:** the pasted HO 606 output, and `docs/handoffs/606-artifacts/audit-2560.json` — local-only, repo-ignored, **regenerable by re-running**, so do not treat it as a historical record. **PHASE PLAN off the table:** P3 is `/` against the committed `dashboard-layout-target.html` (+ the `dashboard-classic` removal, now cheap per the corrected M6); **P4 opens `lobbying` then `members`** — `lobbying` because 78 of its 88 are **M1a** repeated-row anchors, the canonical C1 defect and the cleanest test of whether the fix generalises, and `members` because its 565 M1b wide singletons plus the worst narrow-width surface on the site (724/225/1300 overflowing elements at 1024/900/720) make it the C1/C8 case; **`changes` keeps its P6 slot but LEADS it**, with **`stale` riding alongside** — M3 14.34 and 14.23 against a 2–5 norm, and 42,255px + 25,555px reserved, i.e. **two routes holding two-thirds of the site's 102,450px of reserved space**, which is a C3/C4 problem and not a C1 one at all. **HO 610 — P3 CLOSED, and THE INSTRUMENT IS NOW v2; every number above this paragraph is v1 and they do not compare.** **What changed in v2 (`e6bbc08`), four things, no threshold moved:** vertical-interval **overlap** banding replaces `round(centreY/4px)` bucketing — v1 split any baseline-aligned row whose items differed in size, which under a six-token type ladder is EVERY row, and it scored a packed breaking row at **873px** because it measured id→age with the headline sitting invisibly between them; an **M1b container cut** (a candidate with a child taller than 60px is a layout container, not a row) which retires the `.home-shell` / `.dash-page` / `.dash-left` class whose "gap" is the two-region gutter C8 asks for; a **counted viz exemption** — rows under `[data-viz-row]` (today: `StageFunnel`'s `ul`) leave M1 and report on their own **M1x** line, because a chart row's bar-to-value space is the ENCODING and an uncounted skip grows silently; and **SHA-stamped artifacts that are never overwritten**, because v1's fixed filename meant this HO's own re-run **destroyed the 606 baseline it was supposed to be compared against** (rebuilt by checking out `f031673` and crawling again — do not assume the artifact dir holds a historical record it can regenerate). **v2's falsification legs are INVERTED from v1's and that inversion is the reusable lesson** — see the oddity on falsification-anchor expiry. **THE P4 BASELINE (v2, `docs/handoffs/606-artifacts/audit-e6bbc08-2560.json`, plus the pasted table in the HO 610 roadmap block):** site M1 **1,634** (M1a 327 · M1b 1,307) · **M1x 26** · M2 **17** · M4 **968 / 92,136px**. Per route: **`/members` 548 · `/changes` 471 · `/stale` 153 · `/bills` 78 · `/lobbying` 69 · `/news` 62 · `/trades` 55 · `/member-detail` 38 · `/committee-detail` 31**, and **`/` reads M1 0 · M2 0 · M4 42px with 13 exempted** — the phase's exit number. `/?stage=committee` keeps exactly one row, `.active-filter-strip` at 1,347px: **conditional chrome, invisible to every unfiltered pass**, which is a class worth remembering when a route reads clean. **P4 ORDER — RULED, revising this entry's earlier "lobbying then members":** phase order holds and `changes`/`stale` stay P6, leading it as a pair. Within P4: **`hearings` FIRST** — the HO 610 dashboard hearings interior transplants nearly whole, and `/hearings` now carries **M2 9**, the largest on the site, since the week grid replaced on the dashboard is still live there — then **`members`** (548 M1b wide singletons, plus by an order of magnitude the worst narrow-width surface: 721/222/1297 overflowing elements at 1024/900/720 — the C8 panes work), then `bills`/`news`/`trades`, with **`lobbying` LAST now that its generalisation-test role is spent** (C1 packing was proven to generalise on `/` at HO 610). **The C1 target needs restating for P4 in the terms P3 finished in: "M1 over-threshold = 0" is reachable, but only net of a counted exemption**, and a route's residual should be decomposed before it is worked — P3's 22 broke down 14 design / 5 instrument / 3 predicate, and only the 14 were about the page.

  **HO 615 — P4 IS CLOSED, THE INSTRUMENT IS v3, AND THE PHASE PLAN BELOW REPLACES THE ORDERING ABOVE.** **Every number in the paragraphs above this one is v1 or v2 and does not compare to a v3 number.** P4's remediation, v2 throughout so the routes compare: site M1 **1,634 → 312**, M2 **17 → 8**, M4 **92,136 → 65,278px**; `/members` 548→21 · `/lobbying` 69→64 · `/trades` 55→52 · `/news` 62→0 · `/bills` 78→1 · `/hearings` →0 with M2 9→0. **The two largest movers were never worked** — `/changes` 471→1 and `/stale` 153→51, by shared-component inheritance from the feed-row packing, which is the phase's best evidence that C1 is a convention defect rather than a per-route one. **Instrument v3** (`f40768f`) then changed what the ruler reads: M1 bands by **ink span** with a **continuation-band cut**, M5 extra-wrap clusters by vertical overlap, M5 overflow splits into M5x/(b), and **leg C left the product for a committed fixture**. Against the same DOM, v2 312 → v3 180 — **−132, of which only 27 is product work**; the rest is the instrument no longer lying, concentrated where the wrap artifact lived. **M2 and M4 did not move at all**, the control that v3 was confined to M1/M5.

  **HO 620 — THE ARC IS CLOSED, AND THE INSTRUMENT IS v4.   **THE MAINTENANCE LOOP — four steps, and they are the price of touching layout.** (1) **Run the fixture legs first**; v4 reads **18 of 18 exact** and a zero is not trusted before they are green. (2) **Label the era on every number**, and never present an instrument note as a remediation claim. (3) **Re-check BOTH identities after any change** — site M1 against the residue register, site M1x against the exemption registry — because they are what "done" means here and a drift in either is a missing line, not a rounding. (4) **Any new `data-viz-row` marking ships with an APPLIED curve and both units** (`M1x <candidates> / <rows>`), and the site total is restated.

  **A KNOWN CAVEAT THE LOOP CARRIES, and it is deliberately NOT another instrument revision.** M4's emitter has **three** false-positive kinds, and v4 closes two of them. It catches (a) short labels in repeated rows — that is **M4f**, split off and counted separately — and (b) short *populated* text generally, which HO 617 named when `/member-detail`'s 2,312px turned out to be 27 populated elements rather than a reserved panel (**M4 counts SHORT-TEXT elements, not empty ones**). The third kind survives v4 by construction: **populated non-repeated chrome**. `/members` reads **M4-true 2 / 84px**, and that 84px is the nav strip and the search box — real content, but not inside a repeated-sibling row, so the furniture clause cannot reach it. **Read a small M4-true figure as "check what it is", not as reserved space.** Widening the furniture clause to cover non-repeated chrome would need its own falsification leg and its own fixture pair; it is not worth an instrument revision for 84px, and the caveat is cheaper than the rev.

  **MAINTENANCE ITEMS, FILED WITH THEIR PIXELS AND NOT FIXED** (found by the terminal crawl; none is a defect the arc owed, and each is small enough that a future HO can take it or leave it). **(a) `/`'s document-overflow chain at 720, +19px** — `home-shell` / `home-main` / `dash-left`, three ancestors carrying the same 19px, which is what most of `/`'s residual bucket-(b) 8 at 720 is. **(b) `/`'s races box at 720, 44px of overflow.** Both are narrow-width only; 1024 and 900 read bucket (b) 0. **(c) `/members`' M4-true 84px**, per the caveat above — a nav strip and a search box, not reserved space. None of the three is on the residue register, because the register is M1 and these are M4/M5.
- **THE `data-viz-row` EXEMPTION REGISTRY (opened HO 610, extended HO 615 / 616 / 617 / 619, made standing HO 620).** *Owner:* Code, standing. **This entry exists so the exemption cannot grow silently — an uncounted exemption is how an audit stops measuring the thing it was built for.** **THE CREEP GUARD, verbatim, and it governs every future marking:** *the attribute marks rows containing a bar or shared-axis encoding, never plain text tables, and every new marking ships with its curve.*

  **THE KIND ANNOTATION (added HO 620, from the HO 617 axis pair) — record what the position ENCODES, because that is what decides the verdict.** Clause 1 asks for "a bar or shared-axis encoding", which reads as *bars only* and is too narrow: **a positional encoding qualifies with no bar in it at all.** The worked example is `.ideology-axis` (6) — a fixed −1..+1 DW-NOMINATE domain with positioned markers, where the **distance between two party medians IS the finding** and narrowing the track costs the encoding linearly. **The discriminator is MAGNITUDE vs SEQUENCE, and HO 617 got opposite verdicts out of the same applied method on the same page:** `.ideology-axis` encodes magnitude and was **marked**; `.v2f-bar`, the stage bar, encodes **sequence** — position says *which step*, not *how much* — so compressing costs the reader nothing, a cap reaches it (live 2486 → 379px worst · 1000 → 132 · 640 → 72), and it was **capped with no attribute spent**, scoped to `.bdp` per SKILL's HO 507 shared-rule guard. **File every marking with its kind (magnitude / shared-axis / sequence), and a `sequence` reading is a presumption AGAINST the attribute.** **The metabox outcome, recorded because it is the other direction:** `/bill/[id]`'s three topic chips were argued at HO 619 as exempt-by-context (a metabox *field* is not a repeated row, and C3's noise target is per-row multiplication) — **that argument was true and was not what the rule asks.** The rule asks what the chip *does*; they are labels, and HO 620 (`25493bd`) made them plain. **A context argument is not a kind argument, and only a kind argument reaches this registry.**

  **THE REGISTER — seven marked elements, each with the measurement that justified it:**
  **(1) `StageFunnel`'s `ul` (HO 610).** The dashboard stage funnel. A bar row is label · bar · value-on-a-shared-axis, so the space between a short bar and its number is the encoding. **M1x 13 / 6 visual rows** on `/` (and the same 13 / 6 on `/?stage=committee`, the same component) — the six stage rows plus their `li` wrappers plus the `ul`. Filed in both units retroactively at HO 616, when the caveat below was written.
  **(2) `FirmsLeaderboard`'s table (HO 615, `8a05ad4`) — CURVE CORRECTED HO 616; the exemption stands.** Void: between the firm NAME and **the bar column's fixed origin at x=2107**, 476–699px, median 600, on **25 of 25** rows. That origin is what makes the bars comparable at all.
    **THE HO 615 CURVE WAS MODELLED AND ITS KEY CLAIM WAS FALSE.** It read *"cap 260px clips 2 names and leaves 25 of 25 over threshold; cap 300px clips 0 and leaves 25 over — a cap cannot reach it."* It was computed from track arithmetic — subtract each label's own excess over the cap — which reports *no change* for any cap wider than the longest label. That misses the actual effect: capping a `1fr` label track **moves the fixed 150px bar track left**. **Applied and re-measured (HO 616):** cap 180px → **worst 114px, 0 of 25 over, 10 of 25 names clipped** · cap 220 → 154px, 3 over, 5 clipped · cap 260 → 194px, 7 over, 2 clipped · cap 300 → 234px, 15 over, 0 clipped. **A cap CAN reach it.**
    **The exemption is kept on the honest numbers, not the old ones.** The reachable cap is 180px, and it clips **10 of 25 firm names — 40%**. Against this arc's knees (`/members` 16 of 537 = 3%, `/trades` 4 of 1,209 = 0.33%) that is not an acceptable knee, so clause 2 is satisfied by the corrected curve rather than by the wrong one. Clause 1 was never in question: it is a bar chart on a shared origin.
  **(3) `TopicCrosswalk`'s table (HO 615, `8a05ad4`) — CONFIRMED HO 616 by the applied method.** Void: between **the bar's painted end and its right-aligned value**, 37px on the widest topic to 648px on the narrowest, median 522 — a short bar with a long run to its number IS the small number.
    Re-measured by applying each cap in the page rather than modelling it, the verdict is **unchanged and stronger**: live worst 648px / 22 of 23 over · cap 180 → **worst 873px** · 220 → 835 · 260 → 796 · 300 → 758 · 400 → 662. **Every cap makes it WORSE**, because the bar sits in a fractional `2fr` track: width taken off the label is handed straight to the bar, and the fill is `widthPct%` of whatever that track becomes. The modelled curve happened to reach the right answer here; the applied one explains it.
  **(4) `/amendments` — both `RankRow` tables (HO 616, `780da6d`). M1x 94 / 30 visual rows.** Most-amended bills and Top amenders, 15 rows each. Applied curve: live worst 652px, 27 of 30 over · cap 200 → **917px**, 26 over, 13 clipped · cap 320 → 828px · cap 400 → 753px, 27 over, 10 clipped. Same fractional-`2fr` mechanism as (3): every cap is worse. Marked on each `div.border` table.
  **(5) `/nominations` — the agency `RankRow` table (HO 616, `780da6d`). M1x 65 / 15 visual rows.** Applied curve: live worst 1,347px, 15 of 15 over · cap 200 → **2,071px**, 14 over, 8 clipped · cap 280 → 1,998px · cap 400 → 1,888px, 15 over, 0 clipped. Every cap is worse. Marked on the `div.border` table.
    Both share `components/RankRow.tsx`, extracted from two near-identical page-local copies in the same commit. **Neither table has a column header today** — the table-not-rows clause below still decided the attribute's placement (on the table, not the `ul`), so a header added later inherits the exemption instead of being remediated off its own axis.
  **(6) `MemberIdeology`'s `.ideology-axis` (HO 617, `cd8c3d7`). M1x 4 / 2 visual rows.** Kind: **magnitude**, and the first marking with **no bar in it** — a fixed −1..+1 DW-NOMINATE domain with positioned markers, end labels marking the domain's ends, and the party-median spread as the finding. Applied curve (axis width → worst gap / scale / party-median spread): 2490 → 2299px / 1195px per unit / 1878px · 1400 → 1209 / 672 / 1055 · 1000 → 809 / 480 / 753 · 700 → 509 / 336 / 527 · 500 → 309 / 240 / 376. **Narrowing the track is the only lever and it costs the encoding linearly** — reaching the 120px threshold needs a ~420px axis, a **6× loss of resolution** on the one distance the rail exists to show. The 4 candidates are the ends row and the track; the container and the rail are wrappers. Consumers checked: `MemberIdeology` only — `PolarizationBand` is a different class family, so no other route moves.
  **(7) `PatternBars` on `/patterns` (HO 619, `d56608e`). M1x 18 / 6 visual rows** (5 bar rows + 1 header). Kind: **magnitude on a shared origin** — bar **length** is the bill count and bar **colour** is % past committee, in a shared `2fr` track, which is what makes five patterns comparable at a glance. Applied curve, read in the page: the void is the header `Pattern` → `Bills`, spanning the empty cell over the bar column, **781px live and monotonically WORSE at every cap tried** — 600 → 746 · 500 → 846 · 420 → 926 · 340 → 1,006 · 260 → 1,086 · 180 → 1,166 — while clipping **0 of 5** labels, because the bar sits in a **fractional** track that absorbs whatever the label column gives up. **That is the `TopicCrosswalk` shape, not the `FirmsLeaderboard` one: no cap reaches it.** Placement by the table-not-rows clause, because this one **does** have a column header; the bar rows themselves are never over threshold (14px). The arc's last new exemption.
  **(8) `TopicDistributionList`'s `ul` on `/` (HO 627, `3d0d6d1`). M1x 17 / 8 visual rows.** Kind: **magnitude on a shared origin** — the DISTRIBUTION panel un-tabbed (BY STAGE over BY TOPIC, co-visible per the committed mock), and BY TOPIC ships as a ranked bar list whose bars all start at the same x and are scaled to one max. Applied curve, read in the page — void = bar-end → value: **2560 8–395px, median 353** · **1440 8–155px, median 140**, against the `StageFunnel` directly above it in the same panel at **8–488 / median 454** and **8–166 / median 155**. **No cap reaches it, and this is the STRONGEST form of the exemption rather than the `FirmsLeaderboard` kind: the bar is scaled to the track, so the void is PROPORTIONAL TO THE VALUE ENCODED** — a topic at 1/8 of the max *must* leave ~7/8 of its track empty, and capping shrinks track and bar together, preserving the proportion exactly while making the small topics unreadable. The sibling `StageFunnel` marking (1) is the calibration: same shape, same panel, slightly larger magnitude. Placement is the `ul` and not a table, because this list has **no column header** (the `.dist-sub` "BY TOPIC" is a section label above the list, not a header over its columns) — the table-not-rows clause does not bite. Leg B refused the crawl until the curve existed (`HOME_M1X_EXPECTED` 13 → 30), which is the guard working as designed.
  **THE ATTRIBUTE GOES ON THE TABLE, NOT THE ROW LIST** (2) and (3) both — a column header's geometry is not independently choosable, it has to sit over the columns it labels, and both headers were over threshold (742px, 1051px) for exactly that reason. Exempting the rows and remediating the header would pull the labels off their own axis.
  **THE UNIT CAVEAT, and record BOTH numbers for every future marking: M1x IS A CANDIDATE COUNT, NOT A ROW COUNT.** `/lobbying` reads **M1x 125** for what is **50 visual rows** (25 firm rows + 1 header + 23 topic buttons + 1 header); the other **75 are wrappers and tracks** that pass a width-based candidate gate — a 1255px bar track and a firm-name span stretched by its `1fr` column are both ≥600px wide. The same inflation has always been inside the funnel's 13, and HO 615 is simply the first marking large enough to make it visible. **A future marking files `M1x <candidates> / <visual rows>`**, or the registry's total drifts into meaning nothing. **Site M1x: 332** (terminal, v4 `62c25e4`) = 13 + 13 (`StageFunnel`, on `/` and `/?stage=committee`) + 125 (`/lobbying`, 50 rows) + 94 (`/amendments`, 30 rows) + 65 (`/nominations`, 15 rows) + 4 (`MemberIdeology`, 2 rows) + 18 (`PatternBars`, 6 rows) — **115 visual rows across seven markings on six routes** (6 + 6 + 50 + 30 + 15 + 2 + 6). **This sum IS one of the arc's two closing identities: the crawl's site M1x must equal it, marking by marking.** It balanced on the first check at HO 620.
  **RESTATED HO 627 (`3d0d6d1`) — site M1x 332 → 366**, the eighth marking. `/` and `/?stage=committee` each go **13 → 30** as the DISTRIBUTION panel un-tabs and mounts BY TOPIC alongside BY STAGE (**both arms MEASURED, not derived** — `--route home` and `--route home-stage-committee`, each M1x 30 with M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px). New sum: 30 + 30 + 125 + 94 + 65 + 4 + 18 = **366** — **131 visual rows across eight markings on six routes** (14 + 14 + 50 + 30 + 15 + 2 + 6). The route count is unchanged because the new marking shares `/` with `StageFunnel`. *Close:* never — this is a register, not a task.

New sum: 0 + 0 + 125 + 94 + 65 + 4 + 18 = **306**. **The visual-row count is UNCHANGED at 131 across eight markings on six routes, and that asymmetry is the entry** — this is the unit caveat above finally biting in the other direction. Widening the bills column to 45% narrowed the left region until the widest node under `[data-viz-row]` read **518px against the 600px candidate gate**, so the funnel and topic rows stopped being M1 *candidates* at all; the attribute still reaches the same two `ul`s and the same 86 visible nodes beneath them (measured at 45% and at an injected 31% on one build — only the widest moved, 659 → 518). **A marking can therefore be DORMANT: present, correct, and exempting nothing, because nothing under it currently qualifies.** M1x is a candidate count and is thus a function of layout as well as of the marking, which is why leg B no longer asserts it — see the HO 629 roadmap block. **Consequence for the closing identity: site M1x is width-dependent and must be recomputed, never carried forward, whenever a column fraction moves.**
- **THE PERMANENT-RESIDUE REGISTER (opened HO 619, made standing HO 620).** *Owner:* Code, standing. **The twin of the exemption registry above, and the pair of them IS the arc's definition of done: site M1 = named residue + M1x, nothing unexplained.** Where the registry names what left M1 by exemption, this names what **stayed in M1 and was formally accepted** — with its pixels, its class, and **its price**, so that reopening any line is a priced decision rather than a rediscovery. **Site M1 17 at the terminal crawl (`62c25e4`), entry by entry:**
  **(1) `/members` 13** — **11 knee-marginal** `a.mc-row` at **121–131px** (one to eleven pixels over threshold; the HO 612 knee, priced deliberately: the 240px name cap bought 11 rows for 16 truncated names, and buying these 11 costs **16 more truncations**) + **2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230px** (a short column label over a wide data column — the header-axis class, irreducible without moving a label off the column it labels). Both classes were **probed rather than assumed** before being called accepted (HO 616 `c400450`).
  **(2) `/vote` 2 — the plateau residual** (HO 617 `cd8c3d7`). The applied column-width curve plateaus at 132px and then breaks: (260,24) → 196 · (240,16) → 160 · (228,12) → 132 · (220,12) → 132 · (200,12) → **1192**. Below ~240px the binding term stops being the column width and becomes the ragged fill of the final column. **No width reaches zero**, so this is filed decomposed rather than chased; 0 names wrap at the shipped 240.
  **(3) `/lobbying` 1 — a column header**, priced at HO 615 and re-affirmed at HO 619. Same class as (1)'s header pair.
  **(4) `/trades` 1 — the 196px amount-column header** (HO 616 `7eba4be`). Entirely the amount column's header-label slack: a 191px track right-aligning "Amount" (52px) leaves 139px, plus the type track's slack on "Type" (45px) and the 12px gap. **Irreducible without either narrowing a column sized to stop the money wrapping, or moving a header label off the column it labels.** The 50 data rows read 114px, under threshold.
  **THE HEADER-AXIS CLASS IS THREE OF THE FOUR ENTRIES** (`/members` 2, `/lobbying` 1, `/trades` 1) and it is the arc's honest floor: a column header must sit over the column it labels, and when the column is sized by its widest datum the label cannot fill it. **Do not re-derive these as new findings.** *Close:* never — this is a register, not a task. **Adding a line requires the same standard as the registry: px, class, price, and the site M1 arithmetic restated.**

  **RESTATED HO 633 (`18f6e68`) — entry (1) `/members` 13 → 9, and site M1 17 → 13.** Measured, not derived: `--route members` against a local production build at this commit, fixture legs green first (18 of 18 exact), read off `m1.rows` rather than the summary line. **Decomposition: 7 knee-marginal `a.mc-row` at 121 / 123 / 123 / 124 / 124 / 126 / 129px + the 2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230 / 230px.** So **four of the eleven knee rows crossed under threshold, the band narrowed 121–131 → 121–129, and the header pair HELD at 230px, unmoved.** That split is the entry: the knee was always a distribution sitting one to eleven pixels over the line, so a change in text metrics moves it, while the header-axis class is structural and cannot be moved by type at all — which is the register's own claim about why those two entries are different, arriving as a prediction that held. **The intervening change is the HO 633 sans face swap to IBM Plex Sans, and it is named as the CANDIDATE, not as established cause** — no before/after was run on one build to isolate it, and the `/lobbying` WATCH is the standing proof that this crawl reads deltas the font did not cause. **The four crossed rows are NOT a remediation and buy nothing forward**: they were already priced as accepted residue at HO 612, and a metric change that happens to carry them under a threshold can carry them back over on the next face, weight, or cap change. **Site M1 arithmetic restated: 9 (`/members`) + 2 (`/vote`) + 1 (`/lobbying`) + 1 (`/trades`) = 13.** **CITED, NOT SUBTRACTED, per the accepted-residue rule: this 13 excludes the two unattributed `/lobbying` rows held in WATCH (179 / 129px), so the honest reading is 13 with up to 2 unexplained — 15 if they attribute.** The closing identity is therefore **open on one line** until that WATCH resolves; it is the only thing between the register and balancing again.

  **RESTATED HO 634 — the `/lobbying` WATCH closes, the register takes a SECOND `/lobbying` line, and the closing identity BALANCES. Site M1 13-with-up-to-2-unexplained → 15, nothing unexplained.** Disposition **(a) accepted residue**; `(c′)` per-row content sizing was measured and DECLINED; **`.lob-filing-row` ships unchanged at `minmax(0, 340px)`** — no code, no CSS, no component touched.
  **FILED AS ADOPTED-UNVIEWED, and that is part of the record, not a footnote.** The eight paired renders under `docs/handoffs/634-artifacts/shots/` were **never opened**. The ISSUES-column loss is the thing this HALTed for and it was ruled **on the written recommendation, not by eye**. A later reader must know which kind of decision this was: the shots stay on disk as the **unexercised record**.
  **(i) THE HEADER LINE, RESTATED ON THE FOUR-TERM FORM — WITH ITS PRECONDITION IN THE LINE, NOT A FOOTNOTE.** The header-axis gap is **`gap = track_A − ink_A + column_gap + inset_B`**, where `inset_B` is the next cell's ink offset inside its own track (0 when left-aligned, `track_B − ink_B` when right-aligned). **VALID ONLY WHERE `inset_A = 0`** — the label's own ink flush to its own track's left edge; where it is not, the outgoing gap is not four-term and the form OVER-PREDICTS. Live counterexamples, measured: `.mc-r-num` on `/members` (`inset_A` 21 / 20) and AMOUNT on `/trades` (`inset_A` 139) — the form gives **52 where the surface reads 32**. All three header-axis entries close **EXACT, delta 0**, measured at `1e6435a`, fixture legs 18/18 green first: `/members` **209** (240 − 43 + 12 + 0) and **230** (320 − 122 + 12 + 21) · `/trades` **196** (80 − 35 + 12 + 139) · `/lobbying` **215** (340 − 137 + 12 + 0). **`/trades` is the exhibit for why this is not cosmetic: its `inset_B` is 139px and the old three-term form predicts 57 — off by more than the 120px threshold itself.** **Two of the four terms are TEXT METRICS** (`ink_A`, and `ink_B` inside `inset_B`), which corrects HO 633's "cannot be moved by type at all" — see the append-only correction below.
  **(ii) A NEW LINE, `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row` — AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT A PX CONSTANT. This is the amendment to the admission standard.** The existing standard (px, class, price, arithmetic restated) assumes a **fixed px** figure, and the fixed-px form is exactly what makes the closing identity checkable arithmetic. **This class cannot have one**, so it is admitted as **class + bound + the statistic behind the bound**: *class* `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row`, data filing rows, NOT header-axis · *bound* **`/lobbying` M1 varies 1–3 per crawl** (header always 1, plus 0–2 data rows) · *statistic* **7 of 81 data rows over threshold pooled across seven filing sets, magnitudes 121–179px**. **The 1–3 bound is measured twice, independently:** the HO 633/634 HALT crawl read per-set M1 3/2/2/3/1/2/1 and the HO 634 close crawl read 1/3/2/2/1/2/1 — same band, different days. *Mechanism:* **`gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12`**, with the 340 cap fitted at HO 614 to a then-current **minimum** ink of 239; the corpus has since produced registrant→client strings shorter than anything in that sample, and every pixel below 239 goes into the gap. **No commit, no regression — the data crossed under a constant.**
  **(iii) THE PRICE, STATED SO REOPENING IS A PRICED DECISION AND NOT A REDISCOVERY.** `(c′)` — the rc track sized per row and capped at today's 340, `fit-content(340px)` — was **measured, not reasoned**, both modes read in the same page instance against the same live state. It **buys**: over 5 → 1, header gap 215 → 12, and **zero vertical cost** (feed +0px, page +0px, wrapped 63 → 63 — a row wraps iff its natural ink exceeds its track, and that condition is identical under both modes). It **costs**: **ISSUES stops being a column — x-origin spread 0 → 167px at 2560 and 0 → 201px at 1440**, worst at the narrower width because the bound is `340 − min_ink` and min ink shrinks with the type ladder. The header's retirement to 12 is **not a second win**: it retires **because the column it labelled no longer exists**, the same event counted once. And the result is a **partial** column, which is why it was declined by recommendation: **78% of rows (63 of 81) wrap at the bound and hold the old x while 22% (18 of 81) pull left to their own text** — neither a column nor an honest pack. **Residual under `(c′)` is 1 row, and it does not close the class:** that row is the wrap-at-bound case, and its population is **63 rows whose widest rendered line runs 231–338px (spread 107) against the 232px line that decides over-threshold**, on ~24px of margin. **Reopening `(c′)` means re-taking that trade, not rediscovering it.**
  **(iv) SITE M1 ARITHMETIC RESTATED — CITED, NOT SUBTRACTED.** **9 (`/members`) + 2 (`/vote`) + 3 (`/lobbying`) + 1 (`/trades`) = 15.** The `/lobbying` term is **3 = 1 header row + 2 data rows**, read at the crawl this entry cites. **NOTE THE TWO COUNTS THAT ARE EASY TO CONFLATE, because the ruling instruction itself did:** `/lobbying` now carries **2 register LINES** but contributes **3 ROWS** to M1. M1 counts rows. *(The ruling as typed itemised "2 (`/lobbying`)" against a stated total of 15; 9+2+2+1 is 14, and 15 requires the header's row. Written as 3 to match both the stated total and the "15 if they attribute" already on the record — flagged rather than silently picked.)* **The "13 with up to 2 unexplained — 15 if they attribute" line is RETIRED: they attributed, and nothing unexplained remains.** **The `/lobbying` term is itself a sample, per (ii)** — 15 is the figure at the cited crawl under a 1–3 bound, not a constant, and that is the price of admitting a varying class rather than refusing to price it.
  **(v) THE ADMISSION STANDARD GAINS A CLAUSE: A REGISTER LINE PRICES THE ROW'S *WORST* GAP, NOT THE ROW.** Measured, not asserted: `/members` carries **4 over-threshold gaps across 2 header rows** (209 and 230 on each of the two panes) and the register carries **2**. Without this clause an entry reads as "this row's gap", which is false the moment a row has two, and the next reader who re-measures finds a number the register does not carry and mistakes it for drift.
  **(vi) APPEND-ONLY CORRECTION TO HO 633's STATED REASON — HO 633's BLOCK IS NOT EDITED.** HO 633 predicted the `/members` header pair would HOLD at 230 while the knee rows crossed under, on the reasoning that the header-axis class "**cannot be moved by type at all**". **The prediction was right and the stated reason is over-strong:** two of the four terms are text metrics, so the class *can* be moved by type. It held because those two metrics are **short fixed labels rather than corpus data** and the tracks either side are **authored constants**. Prediction and reason are graded separately; only the reason is corrected. Also settled and **not to be chased**: the pair now reads **230 / 231 / 230** across three crawls, all four terms fixed — sub-pixel rounding, not a move.
  **(vii) BOTH ATTRIBUTION WINDOWS ARE RETIRED, AND ARE RECORDED AS RETIRED.** The owner's candidate window (**HO 597–598**) and the reviewer's bound (**after HO 620**) both assume a commit. **Neither contains the change, because the change is not in the code.** Verified by **byte identity**, which is the evidence that actually settles it: the `.lob-filing-row` block in `app/globals.css` hashes to **md5 `bc49313d103615a3f976173dd7791770` at `62c25e4` AND at `1e6435a`** (HEAD at this sweep) — the same bytes, not merely no commit found. Corroborating: `git log 62c25e4..HEAD -- app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` returns **ZERO** commits, and the governing grid landed at **HO 614 `13203eb`** and has not been touched since. **AND THE HANDOFF'S OWN STEP-0 FIGURE IS WRONG, CORRECTED HERE:** HO 634 §Step 0 stated that this log "returns four commits and none of them is lobbying work: three absence-band commits and one `searchMembers` index hint, **all in `lib/queries.ts`**", and instructed the reader to *verify, not re-derive*. **The correct reading is ZERO** — and the claim contradicts itself, since a log filtered to `app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` cannot return commits whose changes are all in `lib/queries.ts`. **A commit-absence argument is the weaker instrument anyway: hash the bytes, don't count the commits.** A count answers "did anyone touch these paths"; a hash answers "are these the same bytes", which is the question actually being asked. The WATCH asked for the two readings to be reconciled rather than assumed, and **"neither applies" is a reconciliation only because it is written down as one.**
- **~~P3 · M5 needs a third category: SCROLL-BY-DESIGN (opened HO 615, filed NOT taken)~~ — TAKEN AT HO 620 as instrument v4's `M5s` column** (`59a45cd`, corrected `62c25e4`). Shipped exactly where this entry said it belonged: an instrument commit with its own falsification leg, and the fixture carrying its positive and its negative. **One DEFINITION DEVIATION from the shape below, stated because it is a definition and not a detail:** the literal *"…with an animated or explicitly-scrollable **CHILD**"* misses `pre.bdp-rawpre` — **one of this entry's own two worked examples** — because a `<pre>`'s only child is a text node. v4's clause (a) reads the **element itself** as the scrollable thing (own `overflow-x` auto/scroll); clause (b) keeps plain clipping out by requiring an animated descendant. **And the other worked example falsified the first draft:** the marquee read M5s 0 because the audit runs under `reducedMotion: "reduce"` and the app's own `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { animation: none }` suppressed the property the predicate keyed on — animation is now read from the **stylesheet**, and `overflow-x: clip` is accepted alongside `hidden`. See the oddity and the SKILL clause. **Two M4 findings that were flagged "filed for the sweep" during P5/P6a and never got entries of their own are discharged by the same commit and recorded here so the trail is not lost:** the **height-keyed blindness** (HO 618 — `/stale`'s 52px `.row-hslot` was that route's whole M1 while M4 read nothing, because a horizontally-reserved slot is invisible to a `≥40px tall` emitter by construction) became **M4w**; the **short-text-not-empty** finding (HO 617 — `/member-detail`'s 2,312px was 27 populated elements) became the **M4-true / M4f** split. The third false-positive kind, **populated non-repeated chrome**, is a live caveat and not another instrument rev — see the layout-audit entry above. *Original shape, retained for the record —* *Owner:* Code, low. M5x as shipped captures **`text-overflow: ellipsis` and line clamps only**, so a container that scrolls by design lands in bucket (b) and reads as real narrow-width breakage. **Two worked examples, both measured:** the **markets marquee** on `/`, which contributes **~85 of `/`'s 86–88** at each narrow width because it is counted at six nesting levels (`markets-tape--scroll` 7519/976 · `-track` 7542/7538 · `-half` 3773/3769 · `-set` 1888/1885 · `-item` 179/175 · the label 131/59) — its overflow is the mechanism, not a defect (see the HO 606 oddity on marquee trailing gaps) — and **`pre.bdp-rawpre`** on `/bill/[id]` at 720 (710/686), the raw-JSON block, which is bucket (b)'s single entry on that route. **Deliberately not taken at HO 615:** §1 specified ellipsis and clamp, widening a measurement definition mid-close is how an instrument gets tuned rather than corrected, and the two examples are enumerable by hand today. *Shape when taken:* classify an element whose own computed `overflow-x` is `auto`/`scroll`/`hidden` **and** which carries an animated or explicitly-scrollable child — and **count the outermost of a nesting chain once**, since six levels of one marquee is the whole distortion. *Do not take it as part of a remediation HO* — it changes what the numbers mean, so it belongs in an instrument commit with its own falsification leg, and the fixture is where its positive and negative go.
- **The padding half of C2 — row padding and fixed-size glyph strips stepping at the type breakpoints (deferred from HO 604, named not dropped).** *Owner:* Code. HO 604 shipped **type only**, deliberately: once the ladder is live you can see which paddings actually need it instead of guessing. **The 2560 eyeball produced its concrete worklist, measured against the pre-arc build at the same width** — each is a fixed-width container that did not scale with the type it holds, not a type bug: (a) **`/lobbying`** — the BY-TOPIC section blurb reflows 1→2 lines (the *only* leaf text element on any crawled route to gain a line), and the TOP FIRMS top-issue label clips harder (20/25 → 23/25) because its column is pinned at `minmax(0,120px)`; (b) **`/`** — the hearings filter bar reflows onto a second row; (c) **`/welcome`** — a feed title goes 1/7 → 2/7 clipped. At **1440 there is no change at all** (0 new wrap, 0 new clipping, 0 computed-size differences vs pre-arc), so this is strictly a wide-viewport item. **The non-scaling set that drives it:** ~68 one-off CSS sizes (8 / 8.5 / 9.5 / 10.5 / 11.5 / 12.5 / 15–38) and **34 SVG `fontSize` presentation attributes**, which are user-unit attributes and **cannot take a CSS var at all** — so a 20px heading sits over 15px body at 2400 instead of over 12px body and the hierarchy compresses at wide widths. That consequence is **recorded in the `globals.css` ladder comment as bounded and real — do not re-file it as a bug.** **Measured nav baseline (HO 606):** `nav.primary-nav.home-header-nav` wraps **1→2 lines at 1024 and 1→3 at 900/720 on every route crawled** — the single most consistent narrow-width finding in the audit. **Nav compaction is P3 scope** (the arc moved it there from the old P1); this is its baseline. *Gate:* run after the audit (HO 606) so padding is fixed against measured rows rather than guessed ones.

A later reader can cite these twenty from the entry; the other thirty need the re-run. **The other 30 are NOT all real, and the list is deliberately unpruned so that is visible** — the residual is dictionary-word UI labels (`Watching`, `Voted`, `Solid`, `Safe`, `Republican`, `Dem`, `Bills`, `Legislation`) and symbols that lived inside another file rather than owning one (`StageKey`, `TopicKey`, `PrimaryExpandProvider`, `SectionShell`). A list already tidied to look sensible cannot be checked, so the rules that fired are emitted per entry instead. *Close:* every PHANTOM mention is repaired or ruled correct. **(HO 652) `### Pages` IS DISCHARGED, and the counts above are now historical — they remain true of `0ee0570` and are NOT restated here.** A re-run measures **77 PHANTOM mentions, down from 94**, with the `### Pages` section going **47 → 30**. The section was repaired as a unit, every clause grounded in the route's own `page.tsx`, one bullet deleted (`/dashboard-classic`, whose removal IS recorded — `1d9c339`, HO 608, and the roadmap names it twice, so no loop closed unrecorded). **The 30 survivors are NAMED RESIDUE, not unmeasured drift, and 25 of them are ONE classifier limitation worth knowing before 653 touches `### Server / client split`:** a single deletion marker serving a LIST of names — *“`A` / `B` / `C` are retired”* — lands in only the LAST name's ownership scope, so the earlier names read PHANTOM while the sentence is a correct record. The canonical-list section 653 inherits is built almost entirely of such lists, so expect its raw PHANTOM count to overstate its real drift by a similar margin, and price the repair from the mentions rather than the count. The remainder: a paragraph-level historical frame the per-mention scope cannot see (`/primaries`), a locally-defined component the probe's own limits section predicts (`ActivityCaption`), and vocabulary. **And the probe's C1 control expired mid-repair, by a route its own C4 did not predict:** it was anchored to SKILL **line 2043**, and deleting one line 300+ lines above shifted the file so the control silently tested a different mention. **A line-number anchor is invalidated by ANY edit above it, not just by a repair of its subject** — which matters directly to 653, since any `### Server / client split` edit shifts every line below it. C1 is now an inline fixture and no longer expires that way. **(HO 653) `### Server / client split` IS DISCHARGED, by DELETION rather than repair.** A re-run measures **73**, down from 77, with the section going **11 → 10** — reconciled, not asserted: 3 lost at L1711, 3 lost with the deleted name list, 2 gained in the demoted changelog, all other 63 unchanged. **The hardcoded snapshot and its name list are gone**; what remains is one unambiguous refresh command plus the residue a grep cannot produce (the 2 hook files; that one file can carry two client exports — `BillExpandPanel` also exports `BillStageBar`; `MobileNavDrawer` dormant; and the 13 server components that LOOK interactive, each verified present and directive-free). The changelog survives DEMOTED, as history of how the set changed rather than a claim about membership. **THE EVIDENCE THAT THE SNAPSHOT COULD NOT BE MAINTAINED IS NOT THAT IT WAS STALE — IT WAS SELF-CONTRADICTORY.** Measured at `f47b807`: `.tsx`-only **67**, `.ts`+`.tsx` **69**, recursive **69**, none under `svg/`; so the line's own “69 matches incl. the 2 hooks → ~67 components” was arithmetically RIGHT. What was wrong is that the same line closed with “the live count is 55 grep matches = ~53 islands, as of HO 328” — **HO 650 corrected the headline and left the tail, so the `~53`-vs-`~67` disagreement never ended, it moved inside one line.** A figure that reads as authoritative while contradicting itself four hundred words later is a stronger argument for deletion than mere staleness, and it is why the refresh instruction had to become an exact invocation: *“grep under `components/`”* admits readings that differ by exactly the two hooks, so a `.tsx`-only reading followed by the documented hook-subtraction double-subtracts. **A NOTE FOR WHOEVER PRICES THE REMAINING SECTIONS:** the 10 survivors are the list-marker artifact, exactly as predicted before the edit — a demoted changelog is built of *“`A` / `B` / `C` were deleted”* sentences and the marker lands only in the last name's scope. Two of them are names this HO ADDED while demoting. **The count going down by 4 while a section was gutted is the correct reading, not a disappointing one**, and any section repaired by demotion-to-history will behave the same way. **Scope 653 FROM THE ENUMERATION, by re-running the probe — not from this line.** Editing SKILL against a hand-assembled list is exactly how the `~53`-vs-`~67` contradiction HO 650 resolved came to exist, and this list is long enough that a hand transcription of it would repeat that.
- ~~**`fit-finish.spec.ts:143` pins a class the v2 feed row stopped rendering (found in passing, HO 654).** *Owner:* Code. The test asserts `a.bill-id-chip` inside `.v2f-group` and it **fails today**; the class is absent from the render entirely (0 occurrences against 30 `.v2f-group`), because `V2FeedList` renders `className="v2f-id"` — its own header records the swap, *“bordered `BillIdChip` → plain amber id text”*. **PRE-EXISTING and NOT caused by HO 654**, established by control rather than asserted: the pre-edit and post-edit renders are identical on both counts. **The failure mode is the one that matters here** — a committed test that cannot pass is indistinguishable, in a red run, from a test catching a real regression, and it trains the next reader to skim past a red `fit-finish`. *Close:* the assertion targets what the row actually renders, or the test is retired with its reason. **Do not “fix” it by re-adding the chip** — the row markup is the deliberate HO 609/613 compaction and the fixture is what drifted.~~ — **CLOSED HO 655** (`c201985`), and BOTH halves of the HO 654 entry were wrong. **THE FRAMING FIRST: there was no red run to skim past.** The entry said a committed test that cannot pass *“trains the next reader to skim past a red `fit-finish`”* — but **nothing in CI executes this spec**: `e2e-prod.yml` runs `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` by **explicit path** (no `e2e/` glob, deliberately) and `ci.yml` invokes no Playwright at all, both read off the workflow files at HO 655 §0. **That claim came from the reviewer's memory of CI coverage rather than from the workflow file**, which is the reasoning-over-reports class arriving in the entry that names it. **The mechanism RELOCATED rather than evaporating:** `npm run test:e2e` is `playwright test` with **no path**, so the default glob picks up BOTH specs — anyone running the documented manual command got a failure in `fit-finish` unrelated to whatever they were checking. The audience is a person at a terminal, not a workflow. **THE STATED CLOSE WAS ALSO WRONG, and that is what needed a handoff rather than a selector swap.** *“The assertion targets what the row actually renders”* produces a **still-failing test**: `.v2f-id` exists and is a real `<a href="/bill/…">`, so repointing satisfies `:143` and **fails four lines later at the navigation assertion**, where it reads as an app REGRESSION rather than as fixture rot and the next person spends the budget on the app. The behaviour was deliberately reversed by HO 609/613/627. **Measured on the running app** (not taken from the handoff or the component comment): **plain click → url unchanged, `.v2f-group.open` 1, tabs 1** · **ctrl → open 0, tabs 1→2, popup `/bill/119-s-3266`** · **middle → open 0, tabs 2→3, same popup**. The test now pins all three legs (real link · plain expands · modified opens a tab and does NOT also expand — the mirrored defect `rowLinkClick`'s `stopPropagation` guards, which had **no coverage at all** before). **Both new legs proven able to fire on scratch perturbations of HEAD, reverted:** strip `onClick` → **RED `:169`**; strip `stopPropagation` → **RED `:194`**. One perturbation would have proven only leg 2 and left the newly-added leg unfalsified. **`BillIdChip` is NOT deleted** — live at `components/BillIdChip.tsx:33` + `app/globals.css:4340`; HO 654's “0 occurrences” was scoped to `/` and correct there. **ROT RULING: (a) ACCEPT MANUAL ROT, plus this note.** **(b) a static selector check was declined on a MEASURED null, not a predicted one** — the HO 655 census (70 selectors, 41 distinct class tokens) returns **0 with no producer on the very suite that was carrying the defect**, so a static gate would have shipped **green over a broken test**. That is stronger than the handoff's prediction that it *“would not have caught this one”*, and it is the part that stops (b) being re-proposed: the class here is **selector exists, behaviour moved**, and no static check reaches it. **(c) a cadence tied to an existing event** is a convention nobody enforces, which is how it rotted. **AND THE OPTION NOBODY COSTED, recorded so it is DECLINED rather than omitted: running `fit-finish` unattended against LOCALHOST.** Running the test is the **only** thing that reaches the real class. The prod blocker is BotID, but that is a **prod-domain** reason and does not apply here; the localhost blocker is **different and unpriced** — the spec needs feed rows, so it needs `TURSO_*` credentials, and **`ci.yml` is secretless by deliberate decision at HO 488**. **NOT RECOMMENDED and unpriced:** taking it means reopening a property that was established on purpose, and nobody has costed the fixture/seeding work that would avoid doing so. Do not treat it as a bright idea; treat it as a trade someone must argue for.
- **WATCH — `WeeklyBandMetricCard.prior` went OPTIONAL at HO 654, so a metric that FORGETS it renders no delta instead of failing to compile.** *Owner:* Code. Before 654 the prop was required and omission was a **type error**; now a fifth metric card added without `prior`/`priorDate` silently renders a metric with no week-over-week delta, and **“absence is the claim” is indistinguishable from “someone omitted it” to everything except a human reading the comment.** The prop's meaning moved **out of the type and into a comment**, which is the only place it is now written down. **That is the HO 635 shape arriving inside the fix for HO 635's consequences** — a field whose semantics the type system cannot enforce (`stage_observed_at` was a timestamp column that could not say which clock it was on; `prior?: number` is a prop whose absence cannot say whether it was reasoned about). **It STAYS as-is:** the blast radius is **4 call sites in one file** (`components/WeeklyBand.tsx:189/233/269/292`), all four visible in one screen, so the cost of the looser type is bounded and observable. **The eventual fix shape, named here so the next person adding a metric card doesn't re-derive it: an explicit opt-out prop** — e.g. a required discriminated `delta` (`{prior, priorDate}` | `{none: "no-correct-delta"}`) or a required `noDelta` flag — **so that OMISSION is a compile error again and DECLINING a delta is a positive statement in the type.** *Close:* a fifth metric card lands, or the opt-out prop ships. Do not close by re-requiring `prior`: that would force TRANSITIONS OBSERVED to pass a number it has no correct value for, which is the thing HO 654 declined.
- **A tight string-presence filter is unsound for this class, and the instrument can re-enter the same trap (HO 651).** *Owner:* Code. **165 of the 198 M1 candidates are PAIRED-DRIFT** — the name occurs as a token somewhere in source, so a string-presence check would CLEAR it, while it has no file and no export. The motivating case is the whole argument: **`DistributionsTabs`'s only occurrence anywhere is `app/globals.css:1563`, inside a comment describing the deleted treemap's pane** — one stale artifact vouching for another, and two pieces of drift read as one live component. Hence the probe's rule: **existence means a DEFINITION, never a mention**, with presence reported as a column rather than used as an exclusion. **The same shape re-entered through the instrument itself and was caught by measurement, not by reading:** this probe NAMES components in its own comments and `scripts/` is inside the scanned tree, so before self-exclusion it vouched for a phantom it was enumerating (`DashboardBubbleChart`, vouched solely by `skill-phantoms-651.ts`). **An instrument that discusses its subjects in its own source will vouch for them if its own tree is in scope** — worth carrying to the next probe that scans repo text.**STATE IT AS ONE MECHANISM WITH TWO INSTANCES, NOT AS A PROBE QUIRK:** *an artifact that merely MENTIONS a name can clear a string-presence check for it, and that artifact may be stale prose OR the checking instrument's own source.* Instance one is `app/globals.css:1563` — a comment about a deleted pane vouching for a deleted component. Instance two is `scripts/diagnostic/skill-phantoms-651.ts` naming the very things it hunts, with `scripts/` inside its own scanned tree. **Any future check of this shape inherits both**, and the second is the easier to miss because the instrument looks like the one trustworthy thing in the room. **OWED TO ODDITIES** — flagged here rather than written there, because a probe does not get to add a standing rule; whoever acts on this list should land it. **AND A SECOND WAY THE INSTRUMENT READ AS SUCCESS WHILE MEASURING NOTHING, from the same arc.** HO 651 §4 states the deletion marker sits **four characters** from the L2043 phantom. It does not: **the real distance is ~162 characters**, and the “four” was measured off an ELIDED QUOTE inside the handoff — its own `…` hiding ~145 characters of clause — rather than off SKILL. The consequence was concrete: the control was first built with a ±120-char naive window, which cannot reach a marker at 162, so **it passed under both the correct classifier and the broken one and discriminated nothing.** An inert control that passes is this repo's most-repeated defect, and this is the reasoning-over-reports class arriving *from the reviewer, inside the handoff for the probe built against exactly that*. The fix was to make the control assert the MECHANISM — PHANTOM *and* the adjacent-marker trap demonstrably present — so it now fails under `PHANTOM_NAIVE=1`. **Measure a distance in the artifact, never in a quotation of it.**  *Watch:* `app/globals.css:1563` is the known vouching artifact and is untouched; it is named in the HO 650 entry above as part of that repair, not here. **THIRD INSTANCE, AND THE OWED-TO-ODDITIES DEBT IS NOW PAID (HO 655).** The mechanism is filed at the END of `docs/oddities.md` as *a matching check whose pattern cannot fail for some inputs reports those inputs as passing* — recorded here rather than as a second entry because it is **one mechanism, and splitting it across two entries is exactly what this entry argued against**. The third instance is HO 655's selector census: a **bare substring match** over `components/` + `app/` attributed `.open` to `AbsenceCardBack.tsx` and `.v2f-id` to `BillIdChip.tsx`, **both false** — `open` matched the English word in a comment and `v2f-id` matched inside the longer string `bill-id-chip`. **A short token cannot fail a substring test over a large tree**, so v1's *“0 missing”* was partly a property of the matcher rather than of the specs. **The two polarities are now named:** instances one and two **cannot MISS** (an artifact, or the instrument's own source, supplies the match); the ±120-char window **cannot MATCH** (unreachable evidence, so everything reports absent). **And the result stood while the instrument did not** — the strict re-run (whole-word on a `className=` line, or `.token` in CSS) returned the SAME 0 with correct attributions, so **nothing in the first output distinguished the broken matcher from the sound one.** Agreement is the silent case; disagreement would have announced the defect.
- **WATCH — `fit-finish.spec.ts:466` (`/nominations`) failed ONCE under the full-glob parallel run and passed twice (HO 655).** *Owner:* Code. Passed in isolation and on a second full `npm run test:e2e`; **1 failure in 3 runs, cause unidentified** — most likely contention against a single local server when both specs run together, but that is a guess and is labelled as one. **Recorded rather than dropped** because an intermittent failure nobody wrote down is how a suite stops being run: the next person sees a red they cannot attribute, learns the suite is unreliable, and the NEXT real failure is skimmed. **Not attributable to HO 655** — that HO touched only the feed-id test, and `/nominations` shares no component with it. *Close:* it recurs and is diagnosed, or three consecutive clean glob runs retire it. **SECOND ROUTE, HO 657: `/news loads clean` failed the same way** — red under the full `npm run test:e2e` glob, **passes in isolation** (`console=0 pageErr=0 bad=0`), and shares no component with that HO's change (the dashboard races panel). **Two unrelated routes now exhibit it, which is evidence for the contention guess and against a per-route cause** — the common factor is the parallel glob against a single local server, not the route. The close criterion is unchanged and now has a wider net: diagnose it, or retire it on three consecutive clean glob runs. **Neither HO's change is implicated**, and both were checked in isolation rather than assumed.

## BANKED

*Gated/dated; each states its gate.*

- **Consolidate the two party-split mechanisms (HO 540).** The D/R/I split over a roll call is now computed two ways: a SQL `GROUP BY` in `getBillAmendmentVotes` step 3, and an in-memory reduce on `/vote/[id]`. They **agree by the structural skip-unless-D/R/I guard** (oddities), but it's two implementations of one number. *Gate:* a third consumer — the HO 428-A extraction trigger (extract to a shared helper when the count hits three, not before). *Owner:* Corey.

- ~~**Procedural amendment-outcome surface (HO 537 M8)**~~ — **PROMOTED to QUEUED, GO'd on measurement (HO 550).** The HO 550 probe closed the "is it worth building?" question — vocabulary CLOSED at 4 classes, 60 amendments would gain a line, polarity 96% against full 64/64 coverage — see QUEUED "Motion-aware procedural-amendment-outcome surface" + roadmap "Also (HO 550–552)". *Original framing retained —* the 64 senate roll calls that TOUCH an amendment without being its up-or-down vote — Motion to Table, Cloture Motion, Decision of the Chair, and `Motion to Waive Budgetary Discipline Re: … Amdt. No. N` — are real signal (amendments that died by tabling or never cleared cloture) but can't ride the "voted" cut's amendment-framed `VoteLine`: their yea/nay is on the motion, inverted or orthogonal to the amendment's fate, and `deriveDisposition` on their `votes.result` is misleading-on-tally (oddities). Needs a **motion-aware model** — invert tallies for a tabling, distinguish cloture from adoption, suppress when an anchored decisive vote already exists (`119-samdt-2360` etc.). Its own arc. *Gate:* appetite, not a fast-follow. *No fixed HO number* (HO 418). *Owner:* Corey.

- **Dead-symbol prune (`market_ticks`)** *(cosmetic, want-not-need)* — the HO 474 recon found ~200 frozen Stooq-era rows (DOW/GOLD/BTC/VIX/DXY/SILVER/NATGAS/ITA/XLK/XLV/XLF/XLE/XLI) inert in `market_ticks` since ~mid-June — not in `MARKET_SYMBOLS`, never rendered. A one-time `DELETE` is pure hygiene, zero functional benefit. *Gate:* only if `market_ticks` size ever matters (it won't at ~74 rows/day).

- **cron-health refinements (HO 477, deferred)** *(monitoring, want-not-need)* — (a) **fmp-specific freshness:** the markets route logs one `route="/api/cron/markets"` for both the bare and fmp crons, so fmp stopping while bare keeps firing isn't row-detectable — needs payload inspection (symbol-count or a `?source=fmp` marker). (b) **window-aware thresholds** for `/api/cron/weekly-report` + `/api/sync-race-ratings`: the flat 9-day `windowOnly` threshold catches a total stop; precise next-fire alerting needs cron-expression parsing. (c) **per-route data-freshness layer:** a route that times out forever while committing nothing reads alive on `cron_runs` status alone — folding HO 135's bills / summary / news freshness reads into `computeCronHealth` would catch the degrade-not-stop mode. All low-priority; the realistic failures (stopped firing, erroring every tick) are already caught.

- **Nominations secondary surfaces** — (i) a **military-confirmations view** (the 1,049 bulk service PNs excluded from the civilian `/nominations` surface; `is_military` makes it a clean filter/toggle) — this item now **also owns the `nominee_count` hydration**: it's military-only (civilian PNs are 1 nominee) and the natural (only) consumer of officer counts, and the same `--hydrate` detail walk reads it cheaply from the embedded `nominees[]` array (the HO 458 rider), so the military view carries both the `is_military` toggle and the `nominee_count` populate; and (ii) a **`/nomination/[id]` detail page** (rows are non-linked in v1). Both gated on demand — smaller, may never be needed. *Owner:* Corey.

So 26% is a *floor* on total misclassification. *Gate:* when stage accuracy starts mattering for a shipped surface (MOVERS hop-count, the funnel, any stage-gated query). See WATCH → guard-rejection telemetry for the priority signal.
