- **Prod arm for the `unstable_cache` key-rotation clause (LOW — the mechanism is already grounded in source).** *Owner:* Code. The oddity *An `unstable_cache` key hashes the callback's own source text* (HO 647) states a prod consequence — a rotated key has no prod entry, so the first post-deploy read is a cold miss that builds the new value directly — which is **derived, not observed**. The derivation is sound and handler-agnostic (`unstable-cache.ts:133-134`: `generateCacheKey` receives `fixedKey` + args in Next's shared path *before* any cache handler is consulted), which is why a local `.next/cache` reading carries to the Data Cache at all. It stayed unobserved because **the band's public surface cannot discriminate** — a fat payload is a superset of a thin one and `AbsenceCardBack` renders byte-identically from either — so no number of prod hits settles it. **Design: TWO arms, with OPPOSITE predicted readings, because one arm alone confirms whichever way it lands.** *Arm A* — a deploy touching nothing inside `queryAbsenceWatch`'s body — predicts key **stable** → cache HIT → log **ABSENT** (clause (b), callees are not in the key). *Arm B* — a deploy that edits the body — predicts key **rotates** → MISS → log **PRESENT** (the clause under test). **Arm A NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL, because its pass condition is the ABSENCE of a signal** — and an absent log is indistinguishable from a log path that never worked. `/members` calls `getMemberCardExpansion` **directly**, so hit `/members` in the same deploy and confirm the log fires there: the same shared call site that makes the log noisy is what proves it is wired. Without that control arm A is unreadable, and this is the piece the first draft of the design was missing. **Three traps.** (1) **The callback-side instrument is invalid:** a log added *inside* the callback changes its text, rotates the key, guarantees a miss, and so manufactures its own pass — the same-as-success shape entered through the instrument rather than the metric. (2) The log must therefore sit in a **callee**, whose text is not in the key — but `getMemberCardExpansion` is **also called by `/members`**, so it must **identify its caller**. Note this supersedes the tempting alternative of hiding it in a callee only the band reaches: that variant destroys the positive control above, so caller-tagging is required rather than optional. (3) **Read the two outcomes asymmetrically — ABSENT is unambiguous, PRESENT is not.** ABSENT means key stable → hit → the callback never ran (given the control passed). PRESENT has three live explanations: the body genuinely rotated the key (arm B), the bundler **inlined** the callee — which folds the log's own string literal into `cb.toString()`, rotating the key and making the log **fire**, so inlining can never produce a null reading — or the entry was simply gone for an unrelated reason (TTL expiry, a `revalidateTag("votes")` from the votes cron, eviction, a cold region). So arm B confirms nothing on its own; it is arm A's absence, backed by the control, that carries the result. *Gate:* a session already deploying for another reason can take arm A for free; do not spend a deploy on this. *Close:* arm A reads ABSENT with its control firing, or the prod clause is corrected in the oddity. **Deliberately LOW: the cost of the prod arm exceeds what proving it buys, and the clause is flagged derived-and-unfalsified where it lives.**
- ~~**A deploy-liveness gate that stops at the FIRST matching read can green on a promotion transient.** *Owner:* Code. Observed landing `226bd99` (2026-08-12): a poll loop broke on a matching read of `/api/version`, and **the very next request served `daf06b5`** — two instances briefly on different builds mid-promotion. **Not a parse artifact:** the identical parse then read **12/12 clean** once promotion settled, which is what distinguishes a real transient from a broken harness. This is the convergence rule already in oddities (*A shipped VALUE change behind `unstable_cache` doesn't land on the first prod read — verify by convergence, not hit-count*, HO 555) **applied one layer down: to the SHA gate rather than to a cached value** — see that entry for the mechanism rather than a restatement here. **The placement is why it matters.** `/api/version` is the gate every post-deploy measurement runs behind, so a gate that false-greens **hands its doubt to everything taken after it** — a byte reading, a render diff, a latency sample all silently inherit “we were on the new build”. **`scripts/verify-deploy.ts` has exactly this shape today:** it polls until the first match and stops (that is its documented behaviour, not a regression). **Fix:** require a run of **N consecutive** matching reads before declaring live, not one. *Gate:* the next time a deploy check gates a measurement that matters — not worth a session before then. *Close:* `verify:deploy` requires N consecutive matches, or the transient is shown not to recur.~~ — **SHIPPED HO 653** (`0144f66`), and it closes on TWO observations rather than one, which is what makes it a shape instead of an anomaly. **(1) `226bd99` — BANKED, retrospective:** a matching read, then the very next request served `daf06b5`; caught by hand while landing, and filed on the strength of a single sighting. **(2) `4051206` — FIRED, AND CHANGED THE ANSWER:** read 9 matched, **read 10 served `f47b807`**, the run reset, and convergence came at read 15. Under the first-match shape the check exits green at read 9 — one request before prod served the predecessor again. That second instance is the citable evidence: the first showed the transient exists, the second showed the gate would have passed through it. `scripts/verify-deploy.ts` now requires **`REQUIRED_CONSECUTIVE = 5`**, and a break **prints the breaking SHA and resets the run** rather than continuing the count — a silent reset would hide the very event the check exists to catch. **Both branches were exercised before this was called done** (an untriggered guard is unproven): the confirm branch ran live against prod to 5 consecutive and exit 0; the break branch was falsified with a scratch stub sequencing match / match / other / matches, printing `BREAK — served f47b807deadbeef, run reset after 2/5` before restarting and confirming at 5/5, with the scratch reverted clean. **The general form, worth carrying:** the convergence rule (hit until it converges AND HOLDS) applies to a SHA gate exactly as it does to a cached value — and it matters more there, because `/api/version` is what every post-deploy measurement runs behind, so a gate that false-greens hands its doubt to every figure taken after it. Cost is ~40s of extra wall clock at a 10s interval. *Residual:* none — the by-hand convention and the unattended script now agree.

## WATCH

- ~~**New (HO 645) · the absence-band payload doubled at a CONSTANT population, and 96% of it renders nothing.** *Owner:* Code. **The re-price HO 645 §4 called for**, and the number that matters is not the one the criterion gated on. Measured against the live cached `getAbsenceWatch` blob (`scripts/diagnostic/absence-payload-645.ts`, M4, 2026-08-11, prod DB, 2 members): **29,520 bytes — 1.91x the 15,481-byte post-HO-631 reading, so the §4 ship gate (2x) was NOT exceeded and commit B shipped.** But the population did **not** move: the closed entry above reads *"at today's 2 members the payload is not the problem it becomes at ten"*, and it went from **7,740 to 14,760 bytes PER MEMBER on corpus drift alone**, with no code change between the two readings. **A trigger phrased as a population threshold cannot see a per-member one**, which is why it fired unwatched — the same shape as a measured number in a comment going stale while the decision it justified stays put. Re-priced accordingly: **watch bytes-per-member, not member count**, and re-run M4 when either moves. Projection at HO 642's pooled max of 6: ~88.6 KB. **The narrowing is now measured rather than suspected (§4's "note the direction", verified by id):** `AbsenceCardBack` replaced `SponsorExpandedPanel` on this surface and reads exactly three things out of the bundle — `committees[].name`, the first three `recentBills`' type+number, and `stats.total`. The blob is **`recentBills` 78.2% (23,098 B) · `committees` 17.4% (5,127 B) · `topics` 0.8% (224 B) · `stats` 0.6% (191 B) · `affiliations` 0.6% (182 B) · `key` 0.1% (18 B)** — so **`topics` (224 B) and `affiliations` (182 B) are carried, cached and serialized for NO CONSUMER AT ALL** now that the back is not `SponsorExpandedPanel`, and the **23,098 B of `recentBills` exists to print THREE bill numbers**. **NOT taken in HO 645**, deliberately: it is a data change and commit B was scoped to the tier. Two moves when it is picked up — narrow the band's assembly to the three consumed fields (consumer-side, `getMemberCardExpansion` untouched, the HO 631 shape), and the still-unclaimed SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics`. *Close:* **a consumer-side slice at the band's assembly site** — `getMemberCardExpansion` stays untouched, because `/members` reads it too (HO 507) — and M4 re-reads; or bytes-per-member is shown to have stopped growing. **Verify by id, not byte total: a total can coincide (HO 631), which is exactly how the HO 631 slice looked like it had done nothing.**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 647** (`754a0f6`), local-verified; **the narrowing this entry measured is taken, and the payload is now smaller than the post-HO-631 baseline it was filed against.** `queryAbsenceWatch` no longer spreads `MemberCardExpansion`: it projects a named `AbsenceCardPayload` carrying exactly the three keys `AbsenceCardBack` reads. Re-read with the same instrument at the same population (`absence-payload-645` M4, **2 members — MIA 2 / AT RISK 0**, prod DB, 2026-08-12; the cache entry deleted between the two reads, per §4): `recentBills` **23,098 B / 78.2% → 230 B** (1.0% of its pre-value) · `committees` **5,127 B → 1,299 B** · `stats` **191 B → 24 B** (narrowed to `{ total }`) · `topics` **224 B → absent** · `affiliations` **182 B → absent** · `key` **18 B → absent** · **blob 29,520 B (14,760/member) → 2,167 B (1,084/member)**. So the two unread keys are **gone from the object, not merely small** — the PASS criterion was their absence, not their size — and the 23,098 B that existed to print three bill numbers is 230 B. Against the 15,481 B post-HO-631 reading that is **0.14x**, so the WATCH this entry re-priced (**bytes-per-member, not member count**) is satisfied for now and stays worth re-reading when either moves. **Rendered output is unchanged, and that is the load-bearing check rather than the byte figure**: the served `.abw-bk-*` subtrees are byte-identical for both members (md5 `7308325226933bfc`), all four `+N` closers unmoved at +11 / +21 / +4 / +9 — a broken `cmteMore` is exactly what a plausible-looking render would hide, which is why committees are projected but **NOT sliced** (that closer counts off the full array; `recentBills` is sliceable only because HO 631 moved its closer onto `stats.total`). **`ABSENCE_BACK_BILLS_CAP` = 3** is the shared cap now, and `lib/member-card-caps.ts` no longer describes this band at all — its header carried two clauses this change falsified. **NOT closed here, and named rather than dropped:** the **SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics`** is still unclaimed (it has survived two entries in this lineage as a “then consider”; `topics` no longer reaching this payload removes the symptom on THIS surface, not the JS-side count `/members` still runs). **No prod byte figure is reported here, and the reason is NOT the one §4 gives** — this is a correction of that premise, not an inheritance of it. §4 says the key is the `keyParts` literal, so the fat blob would keep serving and shrink only on regeneration. It does not: the key is `${cb.toString()}-${keyParts}` (Next 15.5 `unstable-cache.ts:95`), this edit was inside the callback body, so the key ROTATED and prod holds no entry under it. The first read after deploy is therefore a **cold miss that builds the small blob directly** — there is no convergence window and nothing shrinks on regeneration; the fat pre-647 entry is orphaned under the old key and ages out unread. The figures above are local, and a prod re-read should agree on the first hit rather than after settling. The three HO 646 lines — the unfired `card === null` cause, the 119TH denominator, the `SponsorPhoto` fallback residue — are untouched by this build and stay open.

*Standing observations; each notes what would promote it.*

- **The doc-sweep deletion gate is `git diff --numstat`'s deletions column — `grep -c '^-[^-]'` is not a deletion count (owed from HO 639–641, filed HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. In unified diff a deleted markdown bullet `- **text**` renders as `-- **text**`, which `^-[^-]` **excludes by construction** — so the count reads **0** on a diff that deleted bullets, and 0 is indistinguishable from a clean append. It was reported as verification by two different readers across the 639–641 arc before anything caught it. Use **`git diff --numstat HEAD~1 HEAD -- <file>`** and read the deletions column (normal-format `^<` also works). **Do NOT diff against `git show HEAD:file`** — `core.autocrlf` is true with no `.gitattributes`, so an LF blob against a CRLF worktree reports every line changed. Mechanism is filed in oddities as instance 3 of the proxy-instrument family. *Promote to SKILL as a doc-sweep verification rule when:* a third arc either misreports on it or has to rediscover it — two arcs is an oddity, three is a convention.

- **A DOCUMENTED COVERAGE RATIO GOES FALSE WHEN ITS DENOMINATOR GROWS, AND NOTHING RE-RUNS THE BACKFILL THAT WOULD FIX IT (opened HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. Two halves, and the first is why the second stays invisible.
  **(a) The doc-form failure.** A sentence of the form *"N of M, gaps are X and Y"* is **exhaustive when written and stays grammatically true forever**. When M grows, the sentence does not become ungrammatical, self-contradictory, or greppable — **no word in it changes**, so no sweep, no typecheck and no diff flags it. It just quietly stops meaning what it says: the gap list goes from *complete* to *a sample*, and a reader with no reason to doubt it treats the un-listed remainder as non-existent. This is the measured-number rule's blind spot — that rule catches a figure that is *wrong*, and this failure keeps the figure *right* while the ratio around it rots.
  **(b) The operational gap.** **There is no trigger that re-runs a backfill when its parent set is re-seeded.** The backfills are manual/quarterly and the sets they cover (`race_ratings` re-seeds, `sync:members`) grow independently, so every re-seed silently widens every ratio taken over it. **Un-run is not un-resolvable**, and the docs cannot tell them apart: `backfill:2024-margin` has reached 106 House rated seats against a House rated set of **151**, so ~**45** have no margin because nobody has re-run it — while the entry's two named gaps (ME-02 RCV, TX-09 uncontested) describe seats that are genuinely unresolvable. Same shape one table over: `sync:crosswalk` is gated on `members`, so a member added by a later `sync:members` sits `skipped_no_member` until it is re-run by hand (the Gallagher CA-01 instance is already in SKILL).
  **Exhibits.** **(1) the challenger harvest — see the harvest-currency QUEUED line above**, which owns the scoping and is not duplicated here; it is the fullest instance (reach frozen at `primary_date` 2026-06-02, 57/186). **(2) `backfill:2024-margin`, 106/151** — corrected in SKILL at HO 643 with its gap list explicitly de-exhausted, NOT re-run. **(3) the rated-set predicate figures themselves** — 137/44 and 61 drifted to 186/46 and 59 with nothing re-checking them, which is how the class was noticed at all. **Also in the class, dated at HO 643 and therefore accurate TODAY rather than fixed:** incumbent cash 177/186, Kalshi 185/186, incumbent photo 184/186 — one denominator, and they will all go stale together at the next re-seed. **Explicit NON-member, because it shows what the class is actually about:** `backfill:primary-results` states ~47% and is *not* in it — the forward sync fills each contest as it votes, so it **names its own trigger** and self-heals. The defect is the absent trigger, not the ratio.
  *Promote to SKILL as an authoring convention when:* a **second arc** hits this — three exhibits inside ONE arc is an oddity with three instances, not yet a convention, and promoting on a single arc's evidence would be the same over-reach the two-arcs rule exists to prevent. **That rule is being applied to itself here, deliberately.** The convention it would become: *a documented ratio over a growing set carries its denominator's provenance and its backfill's last-run reach, or it states no gap list at all.*

- **`primaries.primary_type` is NULL on 91 of 876 rows and the MECHANISM IS UNKNOWN (HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. **91 NULL of 876 overall; 75 NULL of the 718 completed**, spread across 12 state+chamber groups — NY house 36, AZ house 14, CT house 10, **WA house 10**, NV 5, UT 5, HI 4, LA senate 2, VT 2, GA senate 1, PA 1, SC 1. **WA is the only special-system state left NULL**: CA `top_two` 52, AK `top_four` 4, ME `ranked_choice` 6, LA `jungle` 6 / `open` 2 / NULL 2 — and WA **NULL 10**. **Mechanism unknown, in those words.** `primaryTypeFor` (`lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) is **ruled out**: its fallback is `return "open"`, so it cannot emit NULL. Nothing else has been read. **This is NOT the HO 584 fallback-default WATCH** — that one is about `open` being an unverified catch-all across ~45 states, a different value and a different mechanism, and citing it here would file a live gap under a cause that reads as already understood. **Consequence, and it is the reason this is worth carrying:** a branch on `primary_type = 'top_two'` catches **52 CA and 0 WA** — it silently misses an entire top-two state, and the miss looks like an absence of data rather than a bug. *Promote* the moment anything is about to branch on this column; read the write path before writing the branch.

- **The candidate status-precedence ladder is transcribed TWICE in `lib/queries.ts` with no shared constant (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `getRaceCandidates` (the `/race/[id]` hub + the prefetched dashboard card) and `getRaceCandidatesForCycle` (`/electoral`) each carry their own `CASE … WHEN status IN ('won_primary','nominee') THEN 0 …` rung list. HO 638 updated both and left cross-referencing comments at each site, so they are **in step today**. **What makes this a WATCH rather than a nit is the failure mode: a status added to one and missed in the other does not error — it falls to the `ELSE` rung and sorts the new status BELOW withdrawn candidates**, i.e. a silently wrong order on exactly the surface the new status was added to serve. *Promote* when a third status enters the vocabulary, or when either ladder is opened for any other reason — extract the rung mapping to one constant then. Same shape as the four inline `median()` copies (HO 427/428): duplicated-and-currently-correct, with no mechanism that keeps it correct.

- **`seed:races` validates ratings but NOT candidate status — an invalid value inserts silently and sorts to `ELSE` (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/seed-races.ts` carries a `VALID_RATINGS` set and warns-and-skips an unrecognized rating (deliberately non-fatal, so one bad rating can't abort a seed run); the candidate `status` field goes straight into the upsert as `c.status ?? null` with **no membership check at all**. A typo — `nominnee`, `Nominee` — therefore **inserts clean**, and because both ordering ladders route anything unrecognized to the `ELSE` rung, it renders in the **wrong position rather than erroring**. **This is why HO 638 read the raw status column back as the literal string `nominee` before trusting either ordering fire: at the seed step, a typo and a correct value produce a byte-identical failure signature.** *Promote* on the second status typo reaching the table, or fold a `VALID_STATUSES` set in beside `VALID_RATINGS` the next time `seed-races.ts` is opened for any reason — the surrounding pattern already exists, so it is a ride-along, not a build.

`gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12`, so every pixel below 239 goes straight into the gap. **Both candidate windows (HO 597–598, and after-620) are retired as retired: neither contains the change because the change is not in the code** — the `.lob-filing-row` block is **byte-identical across the whole window, md5 `bc49313d103615a3f976173dd7791770` at both `62c25e4` and `1e6435a`**, with `git log 62c25e4..HEAD -- app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` returning **ZERO** commits as corroboration. *Disposition:* **(a) accepted residue**, ADOPTED-UNVIEWED off the written recommendation; `(c′)` `fit-content(340px)` measured and declined at a partial ISSUES column; `.lob-filing-row` ships unchanged. **The new line is class + bound + statistic, NOT a px constant** — that amendment to the admission standard is the substantive part, and it is recorded in the register entry above. *(Original framing retained below for context —)* The permanent-residue register carries **`/lobbying` 1 — a column header**; a WATCH is the correct holding form for a measured number with no owner.** **The classes are measured and they are NOT all the register's class, which is the substantive part of this entry:** the **215px** row is `div.mc-row.mc-row-hdr.lob-filing-row` — the column header the register already carries — while the **179px and 129px** rows are `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row`, i.e. **data filing rows, not header-axis**. - **The HO 560 settled-row freeze is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE for substitution (HO 601).** *Owner:* Code. The freeze was built for one hazard: Ballotpedia rebuilding a seat's page around a LATER contest, so a delete-rebuild overwrites a past election's results with a different contest's field. **The HO 601 router removes that hazard upstream** — a special-classified box never routes to the base id — so the freeze can no longer *observe* a substituting roster. Leg 2's `isSettled consulted and REFUSED: true` is real and was reported honestly, but **the payload it refused was the correct June roster, not a substituting one** (and `isSettled` is evaluated before the roster is even built, so it short-circuits unconditionally). The guard **stays live and useful for its general case** — any rewrite of a settled row: Ballotpedia editing a past result, a re-scrape parsing differently — and leg 3's OH `settledSkipped` is exactly that case firing. Its status is **second line behind the router**, not first line against substitution. Do NOT record it as "exercised for substitution"; that reading is available from every individually-true line of the HO 601 ship report and is the thing this entry exists to block. *Promote:* if the router is ever narrowed or bypassed — which re-exposes the freeze and makes its substitution behavior testable again. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/special-router-falsify-601.ts` leg 2 (it prints which branch fired, incl. the `neither (never reached)` case).

Since `efa858f` the **router** is that thing, and the freeze has **never observed a substitution**. **The wrong assumption here is the natural one** — the comment is the first thing anyone touching this code reads, and it currently overstates the guard's role. Same shape as the `lib/queries.ts:2985-2993` item above: **a code comment outranks a doc for anyone working in the file.** *Close:* add two or three lines at `isSettled` naming the router as first line, the freeze as second, and the general case it still serves. **Deliberately NOT its own HO and NOT a doc-sweep edit** — it is a code file, so the HO 581 scope guard keeps it out of the HO 602 sweep; the Aug 25 runoff work is the likely carrier. *Instrument:* `grep -n "LATER contest" lib/primaries-sync.ts` shows the un-updated block.

The two weekly ones are plausibly a zero-invocation display filter, but **`amendments` is daily and should have fired.** Unverified; may be nothing. *Promote/close:* confirm each fires on schedule (a `cron_runs` row per route) before treating it as a real gap.

- **`motionFate(waiver, failed) = killed` over-claims on withdrawn amendments (HO 557 M6).** *Owner:* Code. *Instrument:* `npx tsx scripts/diagnostic/motion-outcome-model-554.ts` — M6 splits waiver+failed by action text and prints ids for anything that isn't "ruled out of order". Reads **48 / 1 withdrawn (`119-samdt-5806`) / 0 neither**. *What's wrong:* 5806 renders "Budget waiver rejected 48–51 → amendment killed" against a `latest_action_text` of "…SA 5806 withdrawn" — and HO 555 deliberately classified withdrawn as **neither** (survived the vote, pulled later), so the motion line contradicts the disposition model on that row. *Why shipped:* 1 row against 48 correct; the obvious narrowing (test the action text for "withdrawn") reintroduces exactly the motion-model↔dot coupling the HO 557 §1 gate rejected on principle. *Promote to a fix* if the withdrawn count reaches 3+ — then narrow as a **measured carve** with the count in the comment (the HO 555 exclusion-list shape), not a patch. *Re-run:* on any waiver-class change or if a new budget-waiver batch syncs.
- **Two HO 557 motion-line paths with no live instance.** *Owner:* Code. Recorded as **UNPROVEN, not protection** (the HO 549 discipline). **(1) `classifyMotion → null` (tail drop):** measured tail = 0 across the residual, so the drop has never executed; it's the guard against an unrecognized motion form appearing in a future sync. **(2) The `chair` label:** the sole Decision-of-the-Chair row (`119-samdt-2360`) is anchored, so it's suppressed and no chair-labelled line renders anywhere in prod. Note the precision — the **orthogonal branch itself IS exercised** (cloture, e.g. `119-samdt-4732`, renders tally-only with no arrow), so what's unproven is the chair *label string*, not the code path. *Close:* eyeball the next unanchored chair motion or the next unrecognized form; both are cheap and inert until then.
- **`when`-subordinate-clause mis-read family (HO 555 M5).** *Owner:* Code. *Instrument:* `npx tsx scripts/diagnostic/disposition-vocabulary-555.ts` — M5 lists agreed-classified rows whose `latest_action_text` contains `when` (hand-readable under 20). Reads **0** today: SA 3963 was alone (it now reads failed via the `fell` token). *What it catches:* a NEW kill phrasing outside the measured vocabulary whose row carries a trailing subordinate "… agreed to" — the SA 3963 shape with a different verb — would classify agreed and surface here. *Instrument honesty:* the probe embeds its own verbatim classifier copies (OLD = pre-555, CANDIDATE = shipped) — it measures the CORPUS against the shipped vocabulary, not prod code; any future edit to `deriveDisposition` must update the probe's CANDIDATE in the same change or M5 reads a stale classifier (the same-as-success trap). *Re-run:* on the next disposition/classifier touch, or if the agreed count moves oddly. *Promote:* to a vocabulary fix if any listed row is a subject-kill mis-read.
- **Doubled prod-crawl load (HO 548).** *Owner:* Code. The smoke crawl now hits each route **twice** (cache-miss + cache-hit), so an unattended `e2e-prod.yml` run's route crawl makes **74 document requests** (`ROUTES.length` = **37** × 2, counted from the array — 31 literal entries + the 6-entry `...STAGES.map` spread + the HO 540 `vote` route; was 36 single-hit before 548) against a `playwright.config.ts` that documents "keep the run gentle so we don't look like an attack." Still gentle at once-daily + on `deployment_status`, and the 2× is the whole point (the hit path is where HO 533 lives) — but the headroom halved. *Promote to a fix* if the crawl ever trips a rate limit / BotID challenge, or if `ROUTES` grows enough that a 2× run approaches a timeout: the lever is to split the double-hit onto a **subset** of routes (the `unstable_cache`-backed dynamic ones — every route today, but a future static route needn't double-hit) rather than doubling the whole list. *Instrument:* the run wall-clock (**1.9m** for the full smoke suite — the 37-route crawl × 2 hits, plus the gate + interaction tests, = **41 test cases**) + any 429/403 in the crawl's own bad-subrequest log.
- **`billIds` shape asymmetry between the precomputed and live LDA paths (HO 570).** *Owner:* Code. The precomputed `readLdaTables` path builds `billsByUuid`/`codesByUuid` with **no `SELECT DISTINCT`**; the live `hydrateFilings` path does. The two now converge only because `FilingRow` dedupes both chip arrays at render (HO 570 C3). **Inert** — the HO 440 distinct-filings ranking is safe by the `Set<string>` inversion in `computeBillDrill` (`lda-rollup.ts:459-464`), so a bill repeated inside one filing still contributes its uuid once, and nothing counts the raw array. *Promote to a fix* (add `DISTINCT` to `readLdaTables`, or dedupe in `rowToFilingSummary`) if a **second** consumer of `FilingSummary.billIds` appears that doesn't dedupe, or if anything ever counts the raw array. *Instrument:* `grep -rn "\.billIds" app components lib` — a consumer outside `FilingRow` that maps or counts it unguarded.
- **`amendment_votes` walk `deferred` counter — transient-vs-pinned instrument (HO 552/553).** *Owner:* Code. `walkAmendmentVotes` now returns `deferred` = HAMDTs left unstamped because a constructed `voteId` had no `votes` row yet (pending, not settled — retried next run, the HO 552 non-stamp fix). **HO 553 STEP 0 proved the branch fires** (scratch-perturbed `voteId` → `deferred=1`, unstamped, retry completed on restore — recorded as **proven protection, not an untriggered number**). The reading to watch: a **transient** sync lag shows `deferred` non-zero on one run and back to 0 the next; a **permanent** gap (a `voteId` that never syncs) shows `deferred` **PINNED** across consecutive runs with `remaining` stable — the walk re-fetches `/actions` (one GET) per stuck HAMDT every run indefinitely. Permanent retry was judged **acceptable** at HO 553 (~228 HAMDTs, one GET/stuck/run; the alternative — stamp after N — reintroduces the exact permanent-drop the fix removed). *Promote to a bound* only if `deferred` ever pins on more than a handful (a real upstream gap, not a same-tick lag): the fix is a stamp-after-N-attempts counter, weighed against re-opening the drop. *Instrument:* the cron payload's `deferred` field run-over-run — pinned + non-trivial = a real gap.
- **`BillVoteRow` all-zero tally suppression unexercised (HO 542).** *Owner:* Code. The `bvote-none` path — a by-name / procedural own-vote where `yea+nay+present+not_voting === 0`, so the "0–0" tally is suppressed and the row renders label + result only (the HO 540 empty-state rule) — is **tsc-verified but never rendered on live data**: none of the three STEP-0 sample bills (`119-hr-1` / `119-hr-8800` / `119-hr-5371`) carried a zero-tally own vote, and the by-name votes that would exercise it (`house-119-1-2` Election of the Speaker) carry no `bill_id`, so they never reach a bill's VOTES tab. *Promote/close:* render-confirm on the next by-name own-vote that does surface — same posture as the HO 540 `/vote/[id]` empty-state WATCH. Cheap and inert until then.
- ~~**`/amendments` blurb carries a qualitative chamber-split claim (HO 537).**~~ — **DISCHARGED HO 539** (`588bed0`): the blurb no longer asserts "near-evenly"; it states the contrast as two interpolated percentages (filings 96% Senate vs recorded votes 52% Senate), guarded on `voted > 0`, so there's no adjective to rot. See roadmap "Also (HO 539–540)".

- **`e2e-prod.yml` global concurrency group (HO 504).** The workflow's `concurrency.group` is `e2e-prod` — **global, not per-ref** — with `cancel-in-progress: true`, so a deploy burst can cancel an in-flight scheduled run, and a cancelled run reports as **neither green nor red**. Low probability at once-daily, and arguably correct (newer state wins). Did **not** occur on the HO 504 push (one deploy, one event, nothing cancelled) — latent, not demonstrated. **Recognition symptom:** if the daily crawl ever appears to *stop reporting*, check for cancelled runs before debugging the schedule. *Promote* only if it bites. **Checked HO 572 — did not fire:** the 07-29/07-30 reds are `failure`, not `cancelled` (the recognition symptom made the check cheap), so this WATCH is looked-at-and-inert, not the cause of the `pageErr` finding below.
- **`e2e/console-noise.ts` is gate-critical (HO 504).** The shared console-error allowlist (extracted HO 504, imported by both specs) now gates a **real** unattended run — `e2e-prod.yml` reds on any un-allowlisted console error — so adding an entry has teeth, not housekeeping. **One of its four entries** is an open loop whose fix will delete it (`MissingSecret` → the NextAuth `AUTH_SECRET` OPEN LOOP above); the file carries per-entry provenance so it stays grep-findable when that loop closes. (The `Encountered two children with the same key` entry was **retired at HO 570 C3** once the `FilingRow` dup-key was fixed at the source — the demonstrated close of a gate entry.) *Promote* if an entry is ever added to silence a *real* regression rather than known-benign noise.
- **Doc duplication between SKILL.md and oddities.md (flagged HO 494; third instance HO 502; fourth instance HO 505 — PROMOTED).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. SKILL.md and oddities.md duplicate the same facts, so every correction is a two-file operation with nothing enforcing the second file. **The first three were caught by noticing** — `/lobbying` went stale twice after shipped changes (HO 491, 494), and the force-dynamic mechanism was corrected in oddities while SKILL kept asserting the old one (HO 502). The fix adopted is the **ownership split**: decide which file OWNS a fact so the other points at it instead of restating it. **What's different about the fourth — why this is a promotion, not another failure:** HO 505 wrote the trigger down *in advance* ("if you catch yourself writing the same sentence into both files, that's the fourth instance"), and it **fired during this sweep, pre-commit** — SKILL's first draft restated the oddities guard-inversion mechanism, was caught against that trigger, and cut to a pointer before the commit landed (oddities owns the mechanism + the 73-test numbers; SKILL names it and links). So the ownership split now works as a **checkable pre-commit rule**, not just a post-hoc catch — the practice succeeded on contact. **Open question (the real residue):** nothing *enforces* the split when no handoff is scripting the check — it held here only because HO 505 had the rule in hand. *Close / next:* decide whether the split can be made self-enforcing (e.g. a doc-test that flags a distinctive sentence appearing in both files) or whether it stays a discipline that depends on someone holding the handoff — until then it's a promoted-but-unenforced practice, not a solved problem. **Fifth instance (HO 538):** SKILL's `getBillAmendmentVotes` line restated the stale "~114" Senate count that HO 537 M8 had corrected to 97 — caught only by *reading the SKILL diff out of the `537-review` ref* (the review-ref route, now written into SKILL.md), not by anything enforcing the split; still unenforced.
- **`fit-finish.spec.ts` manual posture — cost now demonstrated (HO 570).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. The manual/local-only posture is a **deliberate HO 504 call**, not an oversight — oddities records it at the skip-on-empty entry ("still carries the bulk of the guards and stays manual, by design"), and `e2e-prod.yml` runs `smoke.spec.ts` explicitly and never globs `e2e/`. What HO 570 adds is the **demonstrated cost**: the `#lobby-drill` soft-assert red (FIT & FINISH above) sat undetected until someone ran the suite by hand during HO 570 verification — a suite nobody runs doesn't even emit the green the HO 503 skip-on-empty warned about. *Decision to scope when appetite arrives (three named options, not a chore):* (a) add `fit-finish.spec.ts` to `e2e-prod.yml`; (b) promote its stable subset into `smoke.spec.ts`; or (c) keep it manual and **stop counting its greens as coverage**. Until then the posture holds — record the cost, don't reframe the decision. **HO 572 update — the blocker cleared:** the `#lobby-drill` red was fixed (HO 572) and the suite is now green (41 pass / 0 fail), so the three-option decision is **takeable**. One caution before wiring: the newly-found `pageErr` intermittent (OPEN LOOP below) means `smoke.spec.ts` itself is **not reliably green right now**, so adding *more* suites to the unattended runner before that's characterized would pile noise onto a signal that's already firing. **(HO 574 update:** that signal is now *characterized* — React #418 hydration — but still unfixed, so `smoke.spec.ts` remains not-reliably-green and the caution stands.)
- **The backlog-claim convention problem — promote from a header note to a decision (HO 570).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. The header rule ("a backlog line's mechanism is a claim; probe before building") has now been tripped enough that "check before building" is doing the work a convention should do: two wrong-mechanism claims in three HOs (HO 512/514), and HO 570 alone added **two more** (the `.v2f-sponsor*` dead-set glob; the "HO 502 redirect pattern" that doesn't exist) plus stale line numbers in three places. It's past the threshold. **The decision owed** is what changes: whether a stale/wrong line gets **corrected in place when a probe disproves it** (the HO 512/514/546 precedent, done ad hoc), whether entries carry a **verified-at** stamp so age is visible, or whether it stays a discipline that depends on someone holding a handoff. Same shape as the **doc-duplication WATCH above** — promoted-but-unenforced since HO 505. Frame it as a decision, not a chore.
- **Guard-rejection telemetry (HO 239).** `[stage] rejected impossible downgrade` / `[stage] downgrade pending` warns are **flicker-rate telemetry**: rejection frequency is the live signal of classifier noise, and it **informs the action-code tier's priority** (BANKED). *Promote* the action-code tier when rejection frequency climbs or when a stage-gated surface ships. *Instrument:* grep the summarize cron's 30-min log window for `[stage]`.
- **`stage_transitions` validator regression.** `scripts/diagnostic/validate-stage-transitions-232.ts` is now the HO 239 guard's regression check (A↔B invariant + impossible-bucket). Standing spot-check, no close date → lives here, not OPEN LOOPS. *Promote* to action if it ever FAILs. (Companion: `scripts/diagnostic/backward-hop-238.ts` — backward pairs should stay ~9, impossible bucket 0.)
- **B-sync transition-logging path unproven (HO 383).** 383's **sync-side** stage-transition logging is typechecked but only fires when a live sync observes a real stage advance (vs the summarize runner, which logs on re-summarize). *Promote / close:* on the first natural stage advance after `cae39fa`, confirm a `stage_transitions` row written **by sync** (not the runner), that it surfaces on `/changes`, and that the runner **noops** on the later re-summarize (no duplicate row). *Instrument:* `validate-stage-transitions-232.ts` + eyeball `/changes` after the first observed advance.
- **Manual `sync:votes` leaves `member_participation` stale (HO 595/596).** *Owner:* Code. The participation refresh hangs off the **route** `/api/sync-votes`, not off the writers. `npm run sync:votes` / `npm run sync:senate-votes` call `runVotesSync` / `runSenateVotesSync` directly, so a manual run writes `member_votes` **without** triggering the refresh and the materialized table sits stale until the next `0 10 * * *` cron. *Instrument (this is the whole reason it exists):* the `refreshed_at` stamp — compare it against `MAX(vote_date)`; `scripts/diagnostic/participation-falsify-595.ts --stamp` prints it. There is **no standalone refresh entry point** today. *Close:* either hang `refreshMemberParticipation()` off the **writer** (`lib/votes-sync.ts` / `lib/senate-votes-sync.ts`) so every write path triggers it, or add an `npm run refresh:participation`. The writer option is the better shape — it makes the invariant "member_votes changed implies the table was rebuilt" structural rather than conventional.
- **Participation refresh headroom (HO 595).** *Owner:* Code. The refresh's own worst was **11.95s WITH the covering index** (M3), which is why it runs on a **dedicated 30s client** rather than `getDb()`'s 10s — the refresh breaches the very bound it exists to let the request path escape. Without the index it is 15.44s median / 27.14s worst, so the index is now load-bearing on the **refresh** path (its purpose inverted; see SKILL). *Watch:* that 30s headroom shrinks as `member_votes` grows (365,996 rows today, ~661/member). *Promote* if the refresh ever times out — the `[participation] refresh-failed:` log line and a non-advancing `refreshed_at` are the tells, and the non-destructive path means a failure shows up as **stale, not empty**. Fix direction would be an incremental refresh (only members whose votes changed) rather than a full rebuild.
- **FEED_DATE_DRIVE_MIN margin watch (HO 382).** The `2000` threshold that routes the giant-bucket feed onto the date-drive index is safe **only** because `committee` (~14.8k) is the lone bucket above it and `floor`'s next is ~800 — a wide margin. It's **corpus-distribution-dependent**: a second bucket climbing past 2000 silently flips onto the date-drive too (a plan change nobody asked for). *Promote* to a re-check if the funnel shape shifts materially (a stage backlog growing). *Glance* whenever the funnel distribution changes; no action while committee stays the lone bucket over 2000.

Note: roll_call is the actual marginal feed (last in fixed order, budgetStopped 3/7, skipped on the 2 slow ticks) but still gets its turn, so no fix warranted; if feed-order rotation is ever done it's to protect roll_call, not the_hill. This item now covers only the genuine multi-helper fan-out residue.)**
- ~~**Gated-aggregate mis-plan — systemic-pass residue (HO 280).**~~ — CLOSED HO 335 (the HO 332 site-wide audit found + the fix closed all of it). The three named siblings: (1) **`getFeedBills`'s row-returning SELECT** — fixed with a sort-conditional hint (`idx_bills_latest_action` / `idx_bills_introduced_date`, bare/chamber-only), pre-ordered walk + LIMIT short-circuit, no covering-the-world index needed. (2) **`getSponsorsRanked`** — was dead code, **deleted** (the COALESCE-rewrite never needed). (3) **`searchBills`** — hinted onto `idx_bills_summary_feed`, which collapses the MULTI-INDEX OR to one scan (the plan deliverable) **but does NOT fix the latency**: the `LIKE` over `title`/`summary` still full-scans the corpus and cold-aborts on common terms (`tax` → 1290 matches, both old and new plans >10s) — so **`/search?q=` still 500s** (every tab, since the page runs all 4 counts in one `Promise.all`). This is the LIKE-scan limit the WATCH always named; it's now its own OPEN LOOP (FTS / cheaper count). HO 335 improved the plan, did not fix the route. See DONE → HO 335 + OPEN LOOPS.
- **Weekly-report cron mode-specific patches (HO 285 residue).** The HO 285 daily catch-up makes the report cron reliable (a dropped week lands the next day), so these are now optional hardening, not reliability-critical: (1) **tighten the Gemini 503 retry ladder** in `generateReportWithRetry` (`[2000,4000,8000]` ~14s exhausts on a sustained-overload day — #74/#82); (2) **warm/guard the cold Turso connection** on the weekly Monday tick so the first `gatherReportData` query doesn't eat the 10s+10s `boundedFetch` double-timeout (#287 — the HO 238 cold-stall class). The `gatherReportData` ~10-query cold-stall **folds into the gated-aggregate systemic-pass WATCH above** (same statless-planner family). *Promote* only if the catch-up itself starts visibly lagging (a multi-week gap that doesn't drain one-per-day).
- **`/api/sync` heavy-cron soft-timeouts — the absorb-vs-fix call (HO 312 update).** The morning `/api/sync` run soft-times-out intermittently (Turso cold-stall / slow-Gemini, the HO 238 / gated-aggregate family that also touches markets, bills, members) — and **HO 312 confirmed it now delays the report catch-up too**: the Jun 19/20 morning syncs timed out, so the June 8 report didn't generate until the Jun 20 16:57 run, about a day late (absorbed by the catch-up, no user-visible bug). The affected set is now markets · bills · members · **reports-catchup**. The catch-up absorbs the report case across days, but with a fourth consumer riding these timeouts it's **becoming a candidate to address deliberately** (warm/guard the cold connection, or split the heavy steps) rather than keep absorbing. *Promote* when a timeout starts dropping something the catch-up can't recover, or when the morning-sync timeout rate climbs. *Instrument:* `cron_runs` for `/api/sync` (`status=timeout`).

The standing rule — *never take the HO from an on-disk filename, take it from the roadmap* — still holds against filenames, and is what it does **not** survive that matters: **a roadmap that stopped tracking.** At `b634f7e` the roadmap's highest reference is **646** while commit subjects carry **648**, so taking the next number from the roadmap alone would have collided with committed work. **The roadmap is authoritative only while it is CURRENT, and it is current only after a sweep — so a deferred sweep silently degrades the one instrument that was supposed to be safe against filenames.** **The raw gap figure is an artifact and must not be quoted:** subtracting the two sets yields **204**, but that assumes the roadmap is a per-HO index and it is not (112 `Also (HO N)` blocks, 314 distinct HO tokens against 494 in commits) — an HO covered by theme prose without a numeric tag reads as missing. **Bucketed, the real reading is clean: within HO >= 500, where the per-HO ledger convention actually holds, exactly 2 are missing — 647 and 648, both already known.** Zero others from earlier deferred sweeps, which is the question the probe was asked. *Watch:* whether a third accumulates before the next sweep. Belongs in SKILL eventually; it sits behind the self-mod guard, so not from a probe.

## FIT & FINISH

*Audit findings, severity-tagged. P1 = wrong data shown · P2 = inconsistent across surfaces / misleading · P3 = cosmetic.*

- **P3 · C5 — the dashboard renders the SAME two counts twice each, and both pairs are ONE predicate (found by eye HO 634, verified, filed NOT fixed; NOT 634 scope).** *Owner:* Corey. **C5 is a review rule, so an eye-catch is its intended mechanism — but "two call sites that agree today" is not a C5 hit, and that is what the verification settles.** Both pairs resolve to the **same cached function**, not to two predicates that happen to agree: **243** — `getStageChangesCount({}, 7)` at `components/WeeklyBand.tsx:166` renders "243 STAGE TRANSITIONS ▲15", and `getStageChangesCount({}, 7, filters)` at `app/page.tsx:102` feeds `activityCount.total` → `ActivityTabs` "MOVERS (243)". **138** — `getNewBillsThisWeekCount()` at `components/WeeklyBand.tsx:167` renders "138 NEW BILLS ▲33", and the **identical zero-arg call** at `app/page.tsx:103` feeds `newCount` → "NEW THIS WEEK (138)". The stage-changes count is in fact read at a **third** site on the same page, `components/ActivityTicker.tsx:57`, for its `+N more` remainder. *The one real asymmetry, and it is by design:* the tab count passes `filters` and the week strip does not — the strip is corpus-wide, the tab rebases on an active filter — so the two **legitimately diverge under a filter** and are identical unfiltered, which is the state they were seen in. *Recommendation:* **the week strip survives** — it carries the WoW deltas (▲15, ▲33), the sparkline and the hover breakdown, which the bare tab count does not; the tab label is the redundant render. *Promote* on a dashboard density pass. *Do not* "fix" by making the strip read the filtered count — that would break the strip's corpus-wide contract.

- ~~**P3 — `ActiveFilterStrip` is a far-right-anchored row (1,347px interior @2560), visible only when a filter is active; pack left per C1.**~~ — **CLOSED HO 611** (`c42ddb1`), as the predicted rider on P4's first HO. **The lesson outlives the fix and is why the entry keeps its text: this row was invisible to every unfiltered crawl.** `/` read M1 0 at the end of P3 while `/?stage=committee` — the same page with a filter active — held one row at 1,347px. **Conditional chrome is a coverage class, not a defect class:** any element that renders only in a state the crawl does not enter is unmeasured, and the audit's route list is where that gets decided. `--route home-stage-committee` exists in the route list for exactly this reason and should not be pruned as a duplicate of `/`.
- **P3 — narrow-width debt on `/` (M5), opened by HO 610.** *Owner:* Code. **P3-cosmetic, wide-viewport phase deliberately did not chase it.** The dashboard's narrow ladder moved slightly the WRONG way across P3 slice 3: at **720px, overflowing elements 82 → 89**, and **extra-wrap +1 at both 900 and 720** (the audit's flex-line count vs the same containers at 2560). Two causes, both from HO 610 and both understood: the hearings **week ribbon** is five fixed 30px cells plus a trailing total on one line, and the **masthead** gained SIGN IN as an inline item when its spacer was deleted, so the line has one more thing to wrap. Neither is a defect at wide widths, which is the only place P3 was scored. Full ladder for the record at the P4 baseline (`e6bbc08`): `/` **1024 → 88 overflow / 4 extra-wrap · 900 → 78 / 4 · 720 → 89 / 3**. **Do not fix this by re-pinning anything to the right** — that trades a narrow-width wrap for the C1 defect the phase just removed; the ribbon wants a wrap rule and the masthead wants its items to wrap in a sensible order. `/members` remains far worse (721/222/1297) and is P4's C8 work, not this.
  **UPDATE HO 615 — THE INSTRUMENT HALF IS RESOLVED AND MOST OF THIS ENTRY'S ANXIETY WAS ABOUT A NUMBER WITH NO DEFECT IN IT.** M5's overflow population now splits: **M5x** = the element's OWN computed style declares `text-overflow: ellipsis` or a line clamp (authored truncation, permanent, un-actionable) vs **bucket (b)** = real. Re-measured under v3: **`/members` 721 / 4 / 472 at 1024/900/720 is 100% M5x, bucket (b) 0/0/0** — every one of the 1,297 is an authored `truncate`, and the "worst narrow-width surface on the site" had nothing to fix. `/bills` 3/3/0 is also all M5x. **The product residue that survives, with its numbers:** `/` bucket (b) **88 / 78 / 86**, of which the overwhelming majority is **the markets marquee counted at six nesting levels** (`markets-tape--scroll` 7519/976 · `-track` 7542/7538 · `-half` · `-set` · `-item`), plus at 720 the three ancestors carrying its real **+85px document overflow** (`home-shell` / `home-main` / `dash-left`, all 805/720). **So `/`'s one genuine narrow-width defect is the +85px document overflow at 720, caused by the tape, and it is a single item rather than 86.** The ribbon/masthead wrap causes above are unchanged and still un-chased. **Do not fix by re-pinning anything right** — unchanged. The marquee's classification is its own new entry below.
- **~~QUEUED — the `default` variant of the races strip is UNREACHABLE and still fully present (found HO 657).~~ — DELETED AT HO 658** (`c1b08cf`). All of it went: the 2×2 branch, the popover, the HO 230 position keys, `RaceHubBody`'s `preview` mode and its IN THE PRESS block, the three `variant === "v2"` ternaries, and the CSS — **578 deletions / 234 insertions across 7 files, `competitive-race` in the served bundles 34 → 0** (0 on prod too, against live neighbours still present). **The insurance question this entry raised is answered: leg 1 fired on all three HO 230 containment sites**, including `.dv2-racesbox-panels`, which wraps both panes and was therefore measured on each — **0 of 338 (RACES) / 0 of 22 (HEARINGS) visible nodes moved** on removal, against controls that moved 317/338 and 22/22, and **no descendant on either pane resolved its containing block to those boxes**. *Commit-2 audit (2026-06-12, measure-only):*

- **P2 · `/changes` · corpus-query latency near the HO 238 bound.** Prod renders 200 but in **6.69s** — within ~3.3s of the 10s `DB_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`. The HO 238 bound converts *slow-but-working* into a hard 500: corpus growth or a slow tick would push this corpus-wide aggregation past 10s → 500, not just slow. Same latency clock as the `/trends` force-dynamic tombstone. *Mitigation:* the BANKED dashboard fan-out/caching pass. (`/patterns` prod 0.82s — fine.) **Promote-criterion (the fuse):** move the BANKED caching pass to QUEUED-top when `/changes` prod latency crosses **8.5s**, or on its **first 10s-bound 500**. The 238 bound made latency a hard failure mode, so the headroom number is a fuse — act on it before it blows, not after. **HO 404 re-measure — well under fuse:** prod `/changes` returned 200 in **0.25–0.76s** (warm) at the audit; the cold-start query-plan audit shows `getStageChanges` index-only (GOOD) and `getStageChangesCount` timed 29ms. - ~~**P2 · `/committee/[systemCode]` (+ `/trends`) · uncaught React hydration mismatch (#418) on every committee page.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`fd74357`) — collapsed the four multi-child SVG `<title>` sites to a single child (`CommitteeActivityChart` + `BillsTimeSeries` were the live/latent throwers; `PrimaryDistrictModal` + `RaceDistrictModal` fixed for class-consistency, byte-identical text). `/committee/[systemCode]` + `/trends` now console-clean, no #418; `grep "<title>"` shows only single-child titles. *Original:* loaded `/committee/hlig00` (+ `ssju00`, `hsju00`) in a real browser; each throws an uncaught `#418` ("Hydration failed because the server rendered HTML didn't match the client. As a result this tree will be regenerated on the client"). Data-independent (all 3 committees). *Root cause (dev-trace-pinned):* `components/CommitteeActivityChart.tsx:145-147` — the bar `<title>` has **multiple adjacent JSX children** `{formatMonthLabel(month)} · {bucket} · {value}` inside an SVG `<title>`; React's SSR text-segment markers don't survive the browser's SVG-`<title>` parse → hydration fails → React client-regenerates the chart subtree (flash + wasted client work; no wrong data shown, hence P2 not P1). The **same idiom is in `components/BillsTimeSeries.tsx:124`** (`{formatMonthLabel(month)} · {topicLabel(topic)} · {value}`), so `/trends` carries the same latent bug (not exercised locally — force-dynamic corpus page). `PrimaryTimeline`'s `<title>` uses a single template-literal child → unaffected — which is the fix shape: collapse each to one string child, `{`${formatMonthLabel(month)} · ${bucket} · ${value}`}`. *Reproduced on the local prod build; near-certainly live on Vercel prod (same build output).*
- ~~**P2 · `/lobbying` · pagination silently drops the selected `?issue=` drill.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`cc416fa`) — `app/lobbying/page.tsx` now builds a `carry` from the raw `params.issue` (only when present, so it never injects `?issue=` on the unfiltered feed) and hands it to `<Pagination>`, matching `/amendments` + `/nominations`. *Original:* loaded `/lobbying?issue=HCR`, clicked NEXT in the recent-filings pager → URL became `/lobbying?page=2` (the `issue=HCR` is gone). The Section-2 IssueDrill then re-selects the default top issue (`issues[0]`), so a reader paging the feed loses the per-code drill they were reading. *Root cause:* `app/lobbying/page.tsx` passes `carry={new URLSearchParams()}` (empty) to `<Pagination>`, unlike `/nominations`, `/amendments`, and `/trades` — all of which pass a populated `carry` that preserves their filters across pagination. So `/lobbying` is the lone aggregate surface that drops its filter param on NEXT. *Close:* build the `carry` from `issue` (mirror the other three surfaces).
- ~~**P3 · `/` feed row expand · Esc does not close an expanded row.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`156dfe5`) — added an Escape `keydown` effect to `components/useSingleOpenPanel.ts` (guarded on `expandedId === null`), so all four consumers (`BillRowList`, `TopStallsList`, `V2FeedList`, `CompetitiveRacesStrip`) inherit Esc-to-close; the electoral modal's independent handler is untouched. *Original:* expanded a `.v2f-row`, pressed Escape — the row stays open (`.v2f-group.open` count = 1). The single-open invariant and re-click-to-close both work correctly; **only Esc is a no-op.** *Root cause:* `components/V2FeedList.tsx` / `useSingleOpenPanel` wires Enter/Space to toggle but has no Escape-to-close handler. Note the app is internally inconsistent here — the electoral `RaceDistrictModal` DOES close on Esc (verified), the inline feed expand doesn't. Arguably an inline accordion doesn't require Esc (hence P3), but the HO 472 brief named "re-click / Esc closes" as a thing to verify, and Esc fails. *Close:* add an Escape keydown to close the open panel, or accept as by-design.
- **P3 · `/amendments` status hero is a non-interactive strip while `/nominations`' equivalent disposition strip filters.** *What:* the `/amendments` "By status" segmented bar segments are title-only `<div>`s (0 clickable — logged live); the sibling `/nominations` disposition strip segments are `<Link>`s that rebase `?disposition=`. *Judgment:* **not a dead affordance** — `/amendments` offers the same disposition filter via its "Acted"/"Filed-only" chips, so the hero bar is an informational distribution readout, not a broken control. Filed only as a **cross-surface consistency** nit: two sibling aggregate surfaces present the same "status bar" affordance differently. *Close (optional):* either make the amendments hero segments filter-links too, or leave as an intentional readout.

## DONE

- ~~**`BillExpandPanel` collapses in a narrow container (HO 609 finding → HO 610)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`2ca2c96`), prod-verified at `a620e20`. Inside `/`'s 400px feed column the panel is 372px and its 1.35fr/1fr body resolved to **99px / 273px — 71px of prose, 1.4 words per line over 48 lines**. **The fr ratio was not the cause:** the metabox's ~273px min-content is a floor the 1fr track cannot go under, so the entire squeeze lands on the summary — worth knowing before tuning fr values on any two-column panel. Fixed with a **container query on the panel's own width** (stacks below ~440px), NOT a viewport query and NOT a raised column floor: `/bills` renders the same component at 1108–2228px and never crosses the threshold, so one component serves two containers with no route branch. After: **6.9 words/line over 10 lines** on `/`, `/bills` unchanged in shape. **The container query also made `.bxp` a containing block for absolute descendants** — the sponsor hover card lives inside it and was re-checked (still resolves against `.bxp-sponsor`, still on screen).

Code §7-11-55** because the deceased Republican nominee had general-election opposition (verified 2026-08-05, SC Election Commission notice + Ballotpedia corroboration). **Not dormant** — it fed `getPrimaryCalendar` -> `PrimaryTimeline` and `getDashboardPrimaries`, inflating the Aug 11 tick **48 -> 47** contests; the footprint was a **count inflation, not a rendered label**. Both halves in one commit (the seed loader has no delete pass, so either half alone is undone by the other); delete was one literal id, print-before-delete, dry-run default, zero-candidate gate enforced at runtime. Seed note rewritten with the statute, its sources and the verification date. See roadmap "Also (HO 600-601)". Docs: HO 602.

Replaced the page-type gate (`box-special === page-special`, which inverted against itself because `isSpecialElectionPage()` reads the `<title>` and Ballotpedia serves the FL/OH special article at the *regular* URL) with **`<h5>` classification routed against the seed registry**: a special box goes to `senate-{ST}-2026-special-{party}` **iff seeded**, else to the base id. **§1's own rule was rejected on the §2 gates** — routing on the `<h5>` alone produced 4 unseeded targets and would have frozen FL and OH silently. Also: specialness-aware dedup key (Mark Lynch runs in both SC contests), the C2 regular-page fallback kept for a **cursor-driven** reason (the senate slice may not reach SC before Aug 11), the special pass filtered to `isSpecial` so it writes only its invoked ids, and C3's result object replacing a `string[]` that **could not read FAIL**. Shipped HO 527/535; 365,996 rows scanned to return ~537. *Fixed in two steps, and the first was not enough:* HO 594's covering index took steady state 20-24s -> 140ms-1.5s but left an **8.2-14.1s cold first touch (worst >20s — that "20.005s" is the `lib/db.ts` 10s+10s ABORT CEILING, not a duration; corrected HO 597. - ~~**member_votes self-heal (HO 567)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (diag `26879b5` · C1 `547d358` · C2 `f6aab1b`), prod-verified. Closes the zero-roster inflow race (tally publishes before the member positions; the WINDOWED watermark then freezes the empty roster). **STEP 0 gated the cause** — all four zero-roster votes returned populated rosters upstream with tallies matching stored exactly (transient publication, not memberless rolls). Chosen: a **bounded self-heal pass** over holding the vote row (tallies stay live, permanent loss → bounded lag, repair + fix one mechanism — the first run drained the 4, no backfill). Predicate **zero-roster only** (`tally>0 AND NOT EXISTS member_votes`, newest-first, `HEAL_CAP=10`/chamber) — self-limiting (a partial heal exits the set), so no deficit-predicate spin; reuses the per-vote write unchanged; permanent retry (553). **Both branches proven** (549/553): scratch-forced `269` → `pending`, row unchanged, `253`/`254` healed live; revert clean; re-run healed. Drain: all four to full tally, zero residual (the 566 probe re-ran as the regression instrument — M2(a) 0/0). **Participation distortion (per the §0.4 read):** a zero-roster vote **deflates** the 119th participation denominator — `getParticipationStrip` / `participationAggCte` / `getChamberParticipationContext` compute `not_voting / COUNT(*)` over the member's OWN `member_votes`, so a vote with no rows is absent from every member's count (not a per-member MISSED inflation); the career path (`member_career_votes`, Voteview) is unaffected. **Render payoff:** `senate-119-2-146` (Kim Amdt 5545 budget waiver) motion-line split — all-zero → HO 533-suppressed — now renders 44 D + 2 I yea / 53 R nay / 1 D nv. `memberVotesHealed` / `memberVotesHealPending` → payload (id 6056). No theme-% change. See roadmap "Also (HO 566–568)" + oddities + SKILL. Docs: HO 569.

The deliberate version of the 563/564 accident: a grep of the delete-rebuild shape picked three sync targets, all measured before anything was scoped. **M1** committees (236; 10 zero-member, all **source-absent**; 0 source-rostered-but-DB-empty, 0 trailing-by-≥3). **M2** 1,516 votes, 4 zero-roster, **1–4 partial bucket empty in both chambers** (the deficit shape diagnosed the inflow race). **M3** both vote syncs **WINDOWED** (a stored vote is skipped forever by the watermark). **M4** Senate `amendment_votes` 97==97, drift 0/0 (the HO 537 pure-recompute contract's first external check). **M5** 2,259/2,624 zero-bill meetings = expected sparsity. Classification: **committees STANDING-zero-victims · member_votes WINDOWED (4 frozen first-writes) · amendment_votes INERT.** Promoted to **standing regression instrument** (M1 committee rosters · M2 member_votes deficits · M4 amendment_votes drift). - ~~**House roster-erasure fix + repair (HO 564)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`154d779` C1 · `95fdd10` C2), prod-verified, main `95fdd10`. The rule: *a sync must never convert absence of evidence into deletion.* `syncHouseDistricts`' status fork is now **four-way** — `special`/`no_page`/`no_section` all skip the write block (a missing `Candidates_and_election_results` anchor after three retries is a parse failure, not evidence a contest exists), `no_candidates` stays on the row-creating path (that page parsed) — and the per-contest `DELETE`+insert runs **only when the incoming roster is non-empty**, on BOTH chambers. Senate widened on **structure not measurement** (its fork already skipped non-`ok`; STEP 0 S3 sized the empty-per-contest-roster edge at **28 rows** outside the HO 560 freeze), composing with that freeze as **two independent refusals** — settled-freeze refuses a *rewrite* (substitution), the non-empty gate refuses a *delete backed by nothing* (erasure). `rosterDeletesRefused` on both summaries + into `cron_runs.payload`. **Falsified before recorded as protection** (HO 549/553): MD-05 — forced `no_section` → roster byte-identical 27→27 + skip logged; forced empty-`ok` → `rosterDeletesRefused` naming both contests; scratch reverted, nothing committed. **Repair, measured:** House candidate rows **1,911→2,294** (+383) · contests-with-roster **594→718** (+124) · contests-carrying-a-share **389→410** (+21, result sets recovered not just rosters) · zero-candidate **143→73**; refusals NE 44 · S 34 · MW 1 · W 7. Residual 73 decomposed (40 legit-uncontested-with-rostered-sibling + 32 genuinely non-parsing across 16 districts — WATCH), not stuck. Post-re-enable confirm tick `cron_runs` id 6042 `rosterDeletesRefused: []` — wiring **live in the prod payload**, not guard-evidence (that's the MD-05 falsification). No theme-% change. See roadmap "Also (HO 563–564)" + oddities ×2. Docs: HO 565.

M4 (`.cache/` uncommitted → M2/M3 are live Ballotpedia, not a local artifact) · M5 (cursor house 288/435; M1 rows written 2026-06-18 … 07-21, visited-recently-and-empty). No theme-% change. The residual 73 is the decomposed floor in the HO 564 tombstone above (40 legitimately uncontested with a rostered sibling + 32 genuinely non-parsing across 16 districts), **not an open remainder**. - ~~**House settled-row clobber guard (HO 560 follow-on)**~~ — **CLOSED at HO 564**, and the queued framing was **wrong**: it was banked as the HO 560 settled-freeze predicate *ported across to the House*, but HO 563 M3 showed the House failure is **erasure** (a non-parsing scrape deleting a real roster), not **substitution** (rewriting a settled row around a later contest's field) — a freeze keyed on "already carries a share" cannot protect a row whose shares are what gets deleted. So it shipped as a **different guard entirely** (the non-empty-roster delete gate), not the ported predicate. Measuring the failure (563 M3) rather than reasoning from the analogy is what separated them. See roadmap "Also (HO 563–564)". Docs: HO 565.
- ~~**SC special-primary ingestion path (HO 561)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`5122a79` special-page pass · `d521145` dated trigger; STEP 0 `48c2bf3`), main `d521145`. `syncSenateCandidates` runs a second, special-election-page fetch per state **only when a seeded `-special-` row exists** (opt-in by seed presence closes the FL/OH regression surface by construction); it writes only seeded ids (never auto-creates a row), and a 404 — the unpublished-field norm — lands in `fetchFailures`. `scrapeSenateSpecialCandidates` added to the scrape module (special-page-only, no fallback; the FL/OH fallback untouched); `buildSenateMatcher` / `isSettled` extracted so base and special writes make the same call. `runSpecialPriorityPass` scrapes seeded specials within **±7 days** of primary/runoff date ahead of the cursor (cap 3 states/tick, cursor never advanced, `PrimariesCronResult.priorityScraped`). **Ships un-run** — SC special page 404 at ship, hit branch unexercised (WATCH, ~Aug 13). No theme-% change. See roadmap "Also (HO 559–561)". Docs: HO 562.
- ~~**SC Aug 11 special primary seed + settled-row clobber guard + twice-daily cron (HO 560)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`87a4366` seed · `aa9093a` guard · `65d0692` cadence), prod-verified. Seeds `senate-SC-2026-special-{D,R}` (Aug 11 / runoff Aug 25, `race_id=S-SC-2026`, `election_round='primary'`) so it renders as a new `getPrimaryCalendar` → `PrimaryTimeline` tick without a third round vocabulary; freezes **settled** rows (past-dated AND ≥1 recorded share) against `syncSenateCandidates`' unconditional delete-rebuild, so a Ballotpedia page rebuilt around a later contest can't overwrite a past election's results (**blast radius 431**: senate 42 / house 389); cron `0 12` → `0 0,12`. Guard is narrow — past-shareless + future rows stay open — with `settledSkipped` surfaced and `reingest-primary-slate.ts` the escape hatch. **Amendment:** the seed loader's empty-`candidates` path leaves the roster untouched rather than clobbering it (oddities). No theme-% change. See roadmap "Also (HO 559–561)". Docs: HO 562.
- ~~**Forward-sync re-poll of voted-but-NULL contests (~6-week window)**~~ — **CLOSED ALREADY-SATISFIED at HO 559** (`6db8fd0`, read-only probe). The premise "a slow/mid-count state is never re-polled" was **false**: the cron cursor wraps forever and every unit delete-rebuilds, so M5(a) measured all **431** resulted contests rewritten after their date (none ≤0d; 90+d=150). Measured cycle **44.6 days** (471 units, 10.56 advanced/tick, 14/45 budget-stops), which **HO 560 C3 halved** to ~22 (twice-daily) rather than adding a priority queue. The SC 2026 special clause is discharged by HO 560/561. No build. See roadmap "Also (HO 559–561)". Docs: HO 562.

The owed work is a **deletion sweep** (QUEUED), not a fix. No build. See roadmap "Also (HO 559–561)". Docs: HO 562.

- ~~**Motion-aware procedural-amendment-outcome surface (HO 537 M8 → GO'd 550 → STEP-0'd 554 → SHIPPED 557)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`568d58d` leaf · `7027d10` query · `b40b462` surface; diagnostics `7798997` / `d213cb7` / `70a23ae`), prod-verified at `bf820bd`. The 64 Senate roll calls that touch an amendment without being its up-or-down vote now render a motion line — motion, tally, party split, `/vote/[id]` drill, and a fate clause where the motion decides; **60 amendments gain a line** — 55 with a "→ amendment killed" clause, the rest tally-only; the 4 anchored are suppressed and **none of them are killed rows** (a motion-killed amendment never receives its own up-or-down vote, so suppression can't hide a fate — HO 558 M2). **The STEP 0 gate fired and changed the model.** HO 554's DISAGREE=0 tripwire had been measured against the pre-555 classifier; reconciling the probe's pinned `deriveDisposition` copy (the HO 556 WATCH, discharged) took it 0→2 on `119-samdt-3946`/`3947` — the pair HO 550's 54/56 polarity validation missed. Cause: `impliedFate` described the amendment's status **at the motion**, the dot describes its **outcome**; a failed tabling motion leaves an amendment pending, and it can still die later (both fell with their parents). Fixed by narrowing to **`motionFate → "killed" | "undecided" | "orthogonal"`** — **no positive fate**, since tabling and waiver are kill mechanisms that decide in one direction only (tabling agreed → killed, waiver failed → killed, opposites → undecided). Re-gate `dot=agreed AND fate=killed` measured **0**; `killed=55 · undecided=4 · orthogonal=5`. Rejected: comparing fate against the dot at render time (couples the motion model to `deriveDisposition`'s vocabulary — a future widening would silently change behavior). **No writes, no migration, no index, no sync/cron touch, Senate only, dot untouched, `getAmendments` filter semantics untouched** (hero byte-identical: 336/254/35 + 187 voted). **No theme-% change** — combined bar holds at 92. See roadmap "Also (HO 557)" + oddities ×2. Docs: HO 558.
- **`deriveDisposition` widened to the measured Senate kill vocabulary (HO 554 M2 → HO 555)** — SHIPPED `2ce1d6c` (read-only probes `be0fc4d` HO 554 · `c180054` HO 555), verified locally on the cache-hit double-hit and on prod **by convergence** (oddities — the sharpened HO 533 rule). HO 554's motion-model STEP 0 found the prod dot rendering muted `other` on 53/64 procedural-residual rows whose `latest_action_text` states an unambiguous fate; HO 555 refused to patch the examples and clustered the FULL `other` bucket (115/625 acted): the failed-family gains anchored "ruled out of order" + word-boundaried "fell", rescuing **80/115** (agreed 337→336 · failed 173→254 · otherActed 115→35). Withdrawn / rendered-moot / cloture-not-invoked deliberately stay `other`; "stricken" is not a kill token. Zero collision with the 4-string `votes.result` vocabulary, so one shared function serves both input families. **One accepted flip:** `119-samdt-3963` agreed→failed — the OLD read was the wrong one (a trailing "agreed to" from a subordinate "fell when …" clause; the failed-first ordering now decides it — oddities). Voted block untouched (0 flips); the `?disposition=` filter + voted EXISTS bypass `deriveDisposition` (M4 grep), so filter semantics never moved. M5 (the subordinate-clause instrument) reads 0 → WATCH. **No theme-% change** — combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92. See roadmap "Also (HO 554–555)". Docs: HO 556.
- ~~**House amendment-vote mislink: procedural roll calls rendering as amendment outcomes (P1 — HO 550 M5 → 551 fix → 552 edge)**~~ — **FIXED** (`c03f1b7` filter + one-time delete · `20770e3` defer-stamp; probe `9383b9f`), prod cache-hit verified. `lib/amendment-votes-walk.ts` (HO 532) linked EVERY `recordedVote` off a HAMDT's `/actions` with no question filter (the mirror of the anchored Senate `SENATE_AMDT_Q`), so `119-hamdt-40`→`house-119-1-187` and `119-hamdt-175`→`house-119-2-122` rendered an "On Ordering the Previous Question" procedural tally through the amendment-framed `VoteLine` as the amendment's own outcome (green agreed dot from `deriveDisposition("Passed")`) — wrong on the number and the outcome. **HO 551:** the decisive-form gate `HOUSE_AMDT_QUESTION_LIKE = "On Agreeing to the Amendment%"` — derived from the **full 92-row enumeration** (89 "On Agreeing to the Amendment" + 1 ", as Modified" + 2 procedural, vocabulary CLOSED), NOT the two offenders — added to `lib/amendment-vote-key.ts` beside the Senate one (the asymmetry survived because the House had no counterpart there); applied via a JOIN to `votes` in the INSERT's LIKE; the 2 bad rows deleted one-time, scoped to the enumerated procedural set (dry-run confirmed exactly 2), both offenders keeping their real "On Agreeing to the Amendment" vote, `119-hr-8800` 19-amendment control byte-identical. **HO 552:** the gate collapsed "votes row not synced yet" (pending) and "synced, wrong question" (settled) to one no-link, but 551's own comment falsely claimed the absent case self-heals on re-walk — the queue predicate (`update_date > walked_at`) can't deliver it (a just-voted HAMDT already spent its `update_date` bump getting queued), so a decisive link dropped permanently; fixed by leaving an absent-row HAMDT UNSTAMPED to retry (`deferred++`, `continue` — the `fetchErrors` non-stamp precedent). **HO 553 STEP 0 proved the defer fires** — a scratch `voteId` perturbation gave `deferred=1` / unstamped / `remaining=1` pinned across 3 runs, and the retry completed on restore; nothing committed. **No theme-% change** — a correctness fix on the amendments pillar; combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92. See roadmap "Also (HO 550–552)" + oddities. Docs: HO 553.

The LD-2 free text (p99≈1.8k / max 24.4k chars, HO 485) was a fixed 140px scroll box; now a 4-line clamp closed + SHOW MORE / SHOW LESS reveal (`FilingDescription` client island — a `<button>`, the `FillerWatchStrip` precedent, NOT `<details>/<summary>` which would toggle on select-to-copy), open state a bounded 420px scroll so even the 24k monster stays within the HO 492/493-tuned page. CSS additive under `.lob-exp-desc*` only — no `.lob-*`/`.mc-*`/`PAGE_SIZE`/rail retune. **CDP verification caught a real bug:** the handoff's char-threshold + bare-clamped-below-threshold would have *silently clipped* a below-threshold description, because `pre-wrap` makes the 4-line clamp count hard newlines as lines (column CDP-measured at 56 chars/line → 4 lines ≈ 224, under the 280 threshold); fixed by rendering below-threshold UNCLAMPED (`--open`, clip-proof), demoting the threshold to cosmetic. Monster (`cd77f510…`) prod-renders 27 clamp+button / 4 short `--open` = 31 activities; open = +330px bounded, not +6000. Clears the QUEUED line-clamp item. See roadmap "Also (HO 545)" + oddities. Three NEW sibling queries (`getVoteById` reusing `VOTE_SELECT`/`rowToVote`, `getVoteMemberPositions`, `getVoteAmendmentContext` — per-query tags, never widening the shared SELECT); a flat sectioned page (not `RecordTabs`) with position-major grouping + party-then-surname ordering so defectors cluster; the tally on BOTH `/bill/[id]` and `/amendments` links in via the shared `AmendmentVoteLine` (the HO 537 C2 extraction paying off). **Three decisions (oddities):** amendment context fetched unconditionally (not null-`bill_id`-gated — the chamber asymmetry); the party split structural via skip-unless-D/R/I (agrees with `getBillAmendmentVotes` regardless of join); two empty states (lag vs by-name/procedural). The last banked piece of the `recordedVotes` gap: how many amendments drew a recorded floor vote, corpus-wide. **C1** materialized the Senate links into `amendment_votes` (symmetric across chambers now; House was HO 532's walk) — a pure DB recompute, one `db.batch` delete-then-insert scoped by a `votes.chamber='senate'` join, returning a `changed` delta; the Senate parser moved to a leaf `lib/amendment-vote-key.ts` shared by the materializer + `getBillAmendmentVotes` (anti-drift), with a `--senate` CLI mode + a cron materialize step before the House walk. **C2** extracted the shared `VoteLine`/`voteVerb`/`dispositionColor` (`components/AmendmentVoteLine.tsx`, byte-identical). **C3** added the `getAmendmentsSummary` voted block (canonical/latest vote via `ROW_NUMBER` → `deriveDisposition`), a `voted` disposition + page-scoped hydration (`hydratePageAmendmentVotes`) on `getAmendments`, and the page readout + `Voted` chip + exclusion-disclosure line; both cached fns gained the `votes` tag. **The finding:** **187** voted (42 agreed / 145 failed; **97 Senate / 90 House — near-even, unlike the lopsided 96%-Senate filings**). **M8 completeness detour:** the 64 procedural residual (table/cloture/chair/budget-waiver) are NOT clean amendment outcomes — resolving ≠ sufficient — so the anchored matcher is correct by design (114→97 corrects the HO 529 reading; oddities).
